BranchBook Architecture

Appointment Booking System · living architecture

BranchBook solution architecture

A branch-appointment system for a fictional South African bank, designed so that double booking is impossible, confirmations cannot be lost, and every rule has a test that proves it.

The brief, verbatim

“Create a system (front end and back end) for customers to schedule branch appointments and communicates a simulated confirmation of the appointment.”

Stack
Java 25 · Spring Boot 4.1 · PostgreSQL 17 · React 19 + TypeScript
Shape
One jar and one database: make up locally, AWS Cape Town through CDK when hosted
Budget
35 focused hours in tiers, a bookable UI by hour 19
Theme
Fictional Fynbos Bank, clearly not affiliated with Capitec
Living documentChecked against 0991304 on 27 October 2026 · M0 to M7 built and tagged v1.0.0; the hosted demo is live at branchbook.click · Source on GitHubBuiltPartly builtPlanned

Changes to this page

Plan

Build status

This page is the design for BranchBook, and it changes as the code does. At the end of every milestone it is checked against the repository: each section is marked built, partly built or planned, and anything built differently from the plan is listed below with its reason.

Milestones

MilestoneScopeStatusEvidence
M0Day-0 spike, scaffold, one-command run, green CIBuiltThe Java 25 spike passed. make up, CI on every push, and continuous deployment to AWS; since f71b1ae, ci deploys the hosted demo too. Commits 7bcfb74 to cc07871.
M1Schema, constraints, seeds, holiday rule, catalogue APIBuiltThe schema with every named constraint proven by raw SQL, six seeded branches, public holidays for 2026 and 2027, the branch API and four ArchUnit rules. Commits f429ac3 to b2628e9.
M2SlotCalendar and availability APIBuiltSlotCalendar with every slot rule, the availability API with the seats left, the holiday-coverage warning, and the committed OpenAPI contract with the web app's types generated from it. CI also runs the API tests in America/New_York. The contract lists every error code. Commits 6bd3330 to 0db73fa.
M3Booking transaction, outbox row, lookupBuiltBooking in one READ COMMITTED transaction, with idempotency and the PENDING confirmation SMS written in it; lookup with the reference and the last four digits, behind a limit that counts each lookup before the database is read; LogRedactionIT over the application's and PostgreSQL's logs. Two rounds of adversarial review found a race in the lookup limit and several smaller faults; each was fixed and has a test that fails without the fix. Commits c595aa6 to d43b51b.
M4Tracer-bullet UIBuiltThe five-step booking flow with its choices in the URL, the confirmation page that follows the SMS from Sending to Sent or Not delivered, /manage, the simulated inbox, and the landing, privacy, not-found and error pages, built to the UI design recorded in web/README.md. 181 tests run under TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York against a fake API; a real-browser pass on the jar under the strict CSP, at 390 and 1280 px, found one violation, zod's eval probe, now switched off. Commits 0b84e57 to d326e88.
M5Proofs, dispatcher, delivery statusBuiltThe dispatcher on its own thread, one transaction per message, with backoff and a final FAILED, through a simulated SMS gateway; the proofs under contention: twenty racers for one seat, two requests with one key, an outbox that commits with the booking, a held seat lock and a forced deadlock; make demo-409 and docs/runbook.md. A focused review before the commit found three dispatcher gaps and four test gaps, all fixed. Commits 03f9680 to 0786558, on top of the inbox API (ced007f).
M6Tier-2 slot, UI states and accessibility, web tests, end-to-end tests, CI completionBuiltCancel before the appointment starts, which frees the seat and queues a cancellation SMS; retry of a failed message from the simulated inbox; Book again; three demo appointments on a fresh compose stack; two Prometheus metrics, the booking outcomes and the outbox backlog; and each page loaded when it is first opened. Playwright with axe books, looks up, fails a delivery, cancels and books by keyboard alone in a real browser at 390 and 1280 px, in CI against the compose stack. 208 web tests run under both time zones. The hosted demo gained real SMS through AWS End User Messaging and deletes each booking once its appointment is over. Commits 9c32fbe to 4c13314.
M7Docs final pass, clean-clone rehearsal, tagBuiltThe clean-clone rehearsal passed on 11e2482: a cold build, make up, the README's API examples, smoke, make test, the end-to-end suite, make demo-409 and make down. The final documentation pass merged in d4d015f: every guide and diagram matches the code, and it added docs/tour.md and wrote the README's AI assistance section, with a suggestion accepted and two rejected. v1.0.0 is tagged on 0991304, 27 October 2026.
AWSHosted demo in AWS Cape Town, owned by CDKBuiltBranchbookAccount (us-east-1), which the administrator deploys, and in af-south-1 BranchbookRelease, BranchbookData and BranchbookApp. The hosted demo has been live at branchbook.click since 26 October 2026, open to anyone with the address, until its teardown date of 30 November 2026. It was deployed by hand until f71b1ae; since then ci's deploy job deploys it on every push to main once api, web, stack and infra pass, smoke-tests it, and only then records the commit in BranchbookRelease. On 27 October 2026 the pipeline deployed 0991304, which runs two Fargate tasks, one in each of two availability zones. A number outside the demo block 082 555 0000 to 0199 is sent a real SMS through AWS End User Messaging SMS, to South Africa only and at most five a day, though only verified numbers receive one while the AWS account is in the SMS sandbox; a cancelled booking is deleted at once, and every other within the hour after its appointment ends. The pipeline smoke-tests every deployment, and the end-to-end suite has passed against the demo too. Commits 152b96f, a0952bc, fdc94bc, e3c413a, 21798b6 and 44b8d1c.

Where the build departs from the plan

The sections below already reflect these changes. Each departure is recorded where a reviewer would look for it.

The plan saidWhat was builtWhyRecorded in
Java 25 LTS, with Java 21 as the fallback if the day-0 spike failed.Java 25 LTS. There is no fallback any more.The spike passed: every plugin, Testcontainers and the exclusion constraints worked on Java 25.README, Versions
Migrations V1__schema.sql, V2__reference_data.sql, V3__public_holidays_2026_2027.sql.V1__enable_btree_gist.sql, V2__schema.sql, V3__reference_data.sql, V4__public_holidays_2026_2027.sql.The extension the exclusion constraints depend on is its own migration, so the schema migration only creates tables and constraints. Released migrations are never edited.api/src/main/resources/db/migration
The 4 November 2026 proclamation is dropped if it cannot be verified.It is seeded, with its source.The President's proclamation of the local government elections was verified.V4__public_holidays_2026_2027.sql, README
BookingWindow and closed days computed by scheduling.BookingWindow, ClosedDay and BranchCalendar live in catalogue.domain.The branch API (FR-2) already returns the bookable window and the closed dates, so the catalogue owns them and scheduling will reuse them.catalogue/domain/package-info.java
Deploy on v* tags only.Every push to main is tested and deployed; tags still work.The hosted environment always shows the latest work, and a broken deployment is caught on the commit that caused it.ADR-0007
Dependabot paused (open-pull-requests-limit: 0) until the release tag.Weekly updates for Maven, npm, Docker, Compose and GitHub Actions, minor and patch grouped; deliberate major pins are ignored. Security alerts and security updates are on.Keeping dependencies current is part of the work; deliberate pins (Java 25, Node 24, TypeScript 6, PostgreSQL 17) are written down rather than left to chance..github/dependabot.yml
A WARN at start-up and a holidayData health detail once today + horizon passes the last seeded year of public holidays.HolidayCoverageCheck logs a WARN at start-up and every day at 06:00 SAST: from 60 days before the booking window reaches the end of the holiday data, saying how many days are left, and again once the window has passed it. There is no health indicator.A warning that starts only once the window has crossed the data leaves no time to add the next year, and an instance that runs for months would never restart to log it. The log line replaces the health detail.BR-3 in docs/domain-rules.md, HolidayCoverageCheck.java
OpenApiContractTest rewrites docs/openapi.yaml in CI, and git diff checks it and schema.d.ts, as soft gates until M6.OpenApiContractIT fails when the running API no longer generates the committed document, when a parameter pattern is not anchored, and when a real response does not match its schema. npm run check:api fails when schema.d.ts is stale. Both are hard gates from M2, and make contract regenerates both files.An API change then shows in review as a diff of the contract, and the web app cannot compile against a stale one. A soft gate would let drift reach main.ADR-0005, ci.yml, Makefile
/v3/api-docs and Swagger UI on in dev and docker only, off in prod; a relaxed policy for /swagger-ui/** only if Swagger UI turns out to need one.Both are on in every environment. Swagger UI needs inline styles, so /swagger-ui/** alone gets style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; inline scripts stay refused there, and every other path keeps the strict policy.The API is public and describes nothing that is not already in this repository, so it can be read and tried wherever it runs.application.yml, SecurityConfig.java, SecurityHeadersIT
client.ts turns a network or non-JSON failure into an ApiProblem with code NETWORK.A network failure rejects with the browser's own error. An error response whose body is not a problem, such as a proxy's HTML page, becomes an ApiProblem without a code.A screen can tell offline from refused without a code the API does not define.web/src/api/client.ts, web/README.md
GetAvailabilityHandler, SlotResponse.java, SastFormat, SlotCalendar.slotsFor and BookedIntervalsPort.activeFor.AvailabilityQueries, a read-only query service like catalogue's BranchQueries; SlotResponse nested in AvailabilityResponse; shared.time.TimeFormat.isoOffset; SlotCalendar.slotsOn; and BookedIntervalsPort.activeBetween.Each name says what the type does: availability only reads, so it is a query, and the port returns the appointments that overlap a period.The code; the Back end section uses the built names
For a service the branch does not offer, availability is empty and booking returns 422.Availability also answers 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH, as it does for an unknown or inactive service code.An empty list would look like a day without slots. The 422 tells the client that the branch and the service do not go together, as FR-3 already listed.BR-6; docs/errors.md
SlotCalendar.validate is reused by availability and booking.Availability lists the grid with slotsOn. validate checks one requested start and gets its production caller in M3. A test proves the two agree for every minute of every seeded hours pattern and duration.Availability needs the whole grid and booking one answer; the agreement test keeps them from drifting apart.BR-1; SlotCalendarTest
SlotCalendar takes the hours, holidays, duration, capacity, active appointments and now, and has a closedReason(date).It is built for one moment from the branch calendar, the service offer, now, the lead time and the horizon; the booked seats are passed to slotsOn. The closed-day reason comes from BranchCalendar.closure.Everything it needs is passed in, so the class is pure and is tested at fixed instants, and the catalogue keeps the one answer to why a day is closed.SlotCalendar Javadoc
Slot holds OffsetDateTime values.Slot holds Instants; the API applies the branch's offset once, at the edge.The domain never carries an offset it does not need, and the output never depends on the JSON library or the JVM's zone.TimeFormat; BR-20
The booked-seat query filters appointments by active status and overlap with the day.It also bounds the start from below by the longest possible appointment, 120 minutes, the limit of services_duration_grid.Past appointments stay active in this version (BR-25), so the overlap test alone read every one of them. On 32,400 rows a sequential scan of 681 buffers became an index range of 3. JdbcBookedIntervalsIT ties the bound to the schema.JdbcBookedIntervals Javadoc
NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest arrives with the booking transaction in M3.Built in M2, with a test of its own scan.M2 added the first time-dependent native SQL, the booked-seat read, so the guard came with it.BR-20; api/README.md
A ProblemDetail schema; only 404, 405 and 415 have no code.A hand-written Problem schema in which type and code are optional; 406 joins the failures without a code. The code, when present, is an enum of every ProblemCode.These HTTP-level failures carry neither field, and the contract must match the wire. The controllers declare JSON, so an Accept header the API cannot satisfy gets a 406.docs/errors.md; OpenApiConfig
Health details shown in development and Docker, never in production.Health shows no component details in any environment; there are no profiles.The health body reveals nothing about the database or the data. The probes use /readyz and /livez.ApplicationStartsIT.healthRevealsNoComponentDetails
The ArchUnit rule domainHasNoSpringImports also bans the application, infrastructure and api packages.It bans Spring only. No domain class imports those layers today, but no rule checks it.The rule checks what docs/architecture.md promises: no Spring in any domain package.ArchitectureTest
LogRedactionTest, run by Failsafe through an explicit include and excluded from Surefire, checking the ROOT logger and the container log.LogRedactionIT, an ordinary integration test. It takes one synthetic customer through every outcome of a booking and a lookup, then reads the application's formatted log output and PostgreSQL's own log for the name, the number in any form and the reference.It needs the database and the real logging configuration, so it is an integration test by nature. The IT suffix keeps the unit and integration split a naming rule, which SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest enforces.BR-19; SECURITY.md
Booking hands the appointment to ConfirmationOutbox; notification also depends on catalogue.ConfirmationOutbox takes a Confirmation: the appointment's id, reference, start and time zone, the recipient's number, and the branch's and the service's SMS names (ServiceOffer.smsName). notification depends on booking alone.The booking transaction has just loaded the branch and the service, so passing their short names keeps notification free of catalogue and of booking's entity.docs/architecture.md, section 3; Confirmation.java
A reference collision re-runs the booking transaction once.One re-run. A second collision in the same booking is 500 INTERNAL_ERROR and writes nothing.Two collisions in a row among 318 references mean the random source is broken, and another retry would hide it.BR-11; BookAppointmentIT.secondReferenceCollisionReturns500AndLeavesNothing
The booking's translator maps 55P03 and 57014 to 503 TRY_AGAIN.The global ProblemDetailHandler maps 55P03, 57014, 40P01 and 40001 from any endpoint to 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2. ConstraintViolationTranslator maps constraints by name, and the deadlocks and serialisation failures the booking re-runs.Any endpoint can wait on a lock or pass the statement timeout, not only booking.docs/errors.md, Retry-After; ProblemDetailHandlerTest
An it profile for the integration tests, with lock_timeout=10000.No profiles. The integration tests run with the application's own 2-second lock and 5-second statement timeouts.Tests then exercise the timeouts that ship, and none has needed longer. One configuration also keeps a single Spring context and container for the whole run.application.yml; ApplicationStartsIT.databaseSessionsGiveUpOnSlowLocksAndStatements
Five failures per reference per hour, the window opening at the first failure, counted after the lookup.The same window, measured on the injected clock. Each lookup is counted as in flight before the database is read and released if its digits were right, so failures and lookups in flight together stay under the limit. A lookup refused only because another is in flight gets Retry-After: 1.The M3 review showed that lookups arriving together all passed a check made before any was counted. Releasing right lookups means a customer's own page never blocks them.BR-13; docs/architecture.md, section 4
A key is honoured for 24 hours and until the hourly purge, so for at most 25 (IdempotencyIT.purgedKeyIsTreatedAsNew).Exactly 24 hours. The claim takes over an expired key in the same statement (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE it has expired), and the purge only keeps the table small.The purge first runs an hour after start-up, so every restart postponed it, and frequent deploys could keep keys alive indefinitely.BR-12; IdempotencyKeyPurgeJobIT
Log lines name messageId, the reference and the channel; the MDC holds the correlation id, reference, branch and slot.Log lines name appointments by id, with codes and constraint names; the MDC holds only the correlation id. AppointmentReference, MobileNumber and CustomerName print redacted forms if logged by mistake.A reference and the last four digits open an appointment, so a reference in a log is half a credential.BR-19; CONTRIBUTING.md
org.hibernate.engine.jdbc.spi.SqlExceptionHelper logging off.org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error logging off.Hibernate 7 logs failed statements under that category, so the old setting silenced nothing; LogRedactionIT showed it and now fails if the database's message reaches the application log.application.yml; LogRedactionIT
scripts/smoke.sh books at Sandton with a random mobile, replays the key and waits for SENT (FR-32).scripts/smoke.sh writes nothing. It checks that booking and lookup answer, with requests refused before the database is touched and a lookup that is never counted.The same script runs against any instance, the hosted demo included, so it must leave no data. Booking is proven by the API tests and by the README's walkthrough on make up; delivery status arrives in M5.scripts/smoke.sh
ConcurrentBookingIT through @SpringBootTest(RANDOM_PORT) and RestClient, with a LockWaitProbe.The twenty racers go through MockMvc from twenty threads released by a latch, in the one shared test context. Requests held mid-transaction use BookingPause, a test-only trigger that waits on an advisory lock the test holds.The transactions, locks and connection pool are the real ones, and the HTTP server is not what is under test; a second context would add a second database container to every run.ConcurrentBookingIT; ADR-0002 Notes
NotificationDispatcher on a single-thread outboxScheduler bean.DispatcherLoop, a lifecycle component, owns a private single-thread scheduler, outbox-1, and calls NotificationDispatcher.runOnce.A TaskScheduler bean would replace Spring Boot's default scheduler and move the hourly key purge and the daily holiday check onto the dispatcher's thread. DispatcherLoopIT checks both.ADR-0003 Notes; DispatcherLoop Javadoc
An OutboxHealthIndicator with the pending and failed counts and the oldest pending age.Not built.Health shows no component details in any environment, so it would show nothing. The simulated inbox and the SQL in docs/runbook.md show the outbox instead.ADR-0003 Notes; docs/runbook.md
GET /api/v1/messages under @Profile dev and docker only, a 404 in production, with MessagesApiIT.A setting, branchbook.inbox.enabled: off by default, on in compose.yaml, make dev-api and the tests; off, the path is a plain 404. InboxApiIT and InboxSwitchedOffIT prove both. The retry endpoint, POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry, followed in M6 behind the same setting.The application has no profiles. A setting that is off unless a deployment asks for it gives the same safety.application.yml; docs/errors.md; api/README.md
Idempotency-Key keyed on a SHA-256 hash of the payload, stored inside the details draftA UUID kept with a 53-bit cyrb53 fingerprint of the request in its own sessionStorage record, branchbook.idempotencyThe hash only has to tell one request from another in one tab, not resist an attacker, and the browser offers SHA-256 only asynchronously; the record holds no name or numberweb/src/booking/useIdempotencyKey.ts
A 422 for the time or date sends the customer back to the time stepAn alert on the review step with the code's copy and a link to choose another time or dateEach refusal is answered where the customer can act on it, and the link takes them to the timesweb/src/booking/ReviewStep.tsx
The two Idempotency-Key codes show nothingThey show the generic 'Something went wrong on our side' with the correlation id to quoteOnly a bug in the app could cause them, and the id lets support find the requestweb/src/api/problemCopy.ts
After a 429, the /manage form disabled until Retry-After passesThe alert says lookups for that reference pause for up to an hour, even with the right digits; a 429 with Retry-After: 1 is retried once after a secondThat 429 means another lookup of the reference is still being checked, not a pause; every other try counts towards the five, so none is repeated by itselfweb/src/pages/ManagePage.lookup.ts
Skeleton rows while loading, inline spinners on buttons, and offline detection that pauses pollingLoading lines, 'Confirming…' on the Confirm button, and the offline copy from the code tableThese states were not drawn, so they are built from the designed partsweb/README.md (Building it)
An ErrorBoundary with reloadThe router's error element, ErrorPage, inside the shellWithout it React Router shows its own developer error page, which names the error to the customer; inside the shell the banner and footer stayweb/src/pages/ErrorPage.tsx, web/src/routes.tsx
The appointment query key without the digits['appointment', reference, last4]An answer is cached as opened with the given digits, so other digits never read it; the digits stay in memory, never in the URL or a logweb/src/api/queries.ts
The date pager disabled at the edges of the windowaria-disabled at the edges, so it stays focusableA keyboard user who pages to the end is not dropped to the top of the pageweb/src/booking/DateStrip.tsx
Booking retried once on a network error and once on a 503 after Retry-AfterOne retry, on a 503 only, after Retry-After capped at 5 s (2 s when absent); after a network error the customer's next tap resends the same keySo the customer is not left waiting on the review stepweb/src/api/queries.ts
slot in the URL as the ISO startsAt with +02:00slot as HH:MM in SAST, joined to the date by sastInstant when the request is builtThe date is already its own parameter, so the clock time is enoughweb/src/booking/bookingParams.ts
Register branchbook.co.za at a .co.za registrar and own its hosted zone in a BranchbookDns stack, with name servers delegated by handbranchbook.click, registered through Route 53, which created the hosted zone; no DNS stack, and the zone's ID is in infra/cdk.jsonRegistering through Route 53 creates and delegates the zone, so no stack needs to own it; https://branchbook.click is 24 characters, inside the 28 the SMS allowsADR-0006 Notes, infra/README.md
The pipeline deploys BranchbookApp on every push to main, with DEMO_BASIC_AUTH as a GitHub secret and smoke-aws.sh waiting for the new versionci's deploy job, on a push to main once api, web, stack and infra pass, builds the ARM64 image on an ARM runner, deploys BranchbookData and BranchbookApp, runs scripts/smoke.sh against https://branchbook.click, and only then deploys BranchbookRelease and reads the commit back. There is no DEMO_BASIC_AUTH secret and no smoke-aws.sh, and deploy.yml and infra.yml are deleted. The administrator deployed the demo by hand from 26 October 2026 until f71b1aeThe release record then names a commit that is live and smoke-tested, and a red check anywhere means no deployment. The demo has no sign-in to pass, and smoke.sh writes nothing, so the script a reviewer runs locally also checks the live addressADR-0007 Notes (2026-10-27); docs/delivery.md; .github/workflows/ci.yml
prod and demo Spring profiles (application-prod.yml, application-demo.yml), with actuator on management port 9091Environment variables on the Fargate task (DB_URL with sslmode=require, PUBLIC_BASE_URL, BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_ENABLED, BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES, BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED, the BRANCHBOOK_SMS_* settings and SERVER_FORWARD_HEADERS_STRATEGY); actuator stays on 8080 exposing only health and info, and WAF blocks /actuator with 403The task sets every value it needs, so the application runs as make up runs it and the profile files are not neededADR-0006 Notes, infra/README.md
Demo numbers 082 555 0101 to 0110 and 0000, each locked to a fictional persona name that GET /api/v1/config listsNo personas. Where a deployment asks, branchbook.booking.demo-numbers-only refuses any number outside 082 555 0000 to 0199 with 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED; the hosted demo used it until it began texting real SMS on 27 OctoberAccepting only the reserved demo range kept real numbers out without locking each one to a persona. Real SMS replaced it on the hosted demo, where the demo numbers stay simulated and each booking is deleted once its appointment is overADR-0006 Notes, infra/lib/app-stack.ts
A parameter group with log_error_verbosity=terse and log_min_error_statement=panic keeps customer data out of PostgreSQL's logThe parameter group sets only rds.force_ssl=1, and PostgreSQL's own log is not exportedA constraint error's DETAIL line can quote a mobile number, and no exported log may hold personal data (BR-19)infra/README.md, infra/lib/data-stack.ts
postgresVersion in cdk.json, from describe-db-engine-versions, with automatic minor upgrades offPostgreSQL 17.11 as the constant POSTGRES_VERSION in data-stack.ts, with autoMinorVersionUpgrade on17.11 is the version make up and the tests run, and Cape Town offers itADR-0006 Notes, infra/lib/data-stack.ts
A container health check on /livez, so ECS replaces a task whose dispatcher has stopped tickingNo container health check; the load balancer checks /readyz, and the deployment circuit breaker rolls back a version that never becomes healthyNot recorded; the dispatcher liveness indicator that check would rely on is not built yetinfra/lib/app-stack.ts
UI states, accessibility and six Vitest/RTL/MSW tests in M6 (hours 27 to 29)With the screens in M4: loading, empty, error, stale and not-found states, native radio times, focus to each page's heading, and 181 Vitest tests in 31 files under two time zonesThe UI design had drawn the screens and their states before M4, so each screen was built and tested with its statesweb/README.md (Status (M4), UI design)
ESLint forbids the style prop (react/forbid-dom-props)No such rule: styles are CSS Modules only and no component sets a style attribute; a real-browser pass under the strict CSP found no violationNot recorded; the convention and the browser pass stand in for the ruleweb/README.md (Building it) states the convention only
First PNGs via page.screenshot() and a README walkthrough draft in M4The screenshots came in M6: with SCREENSHOTS=1, book-appointment.spec.ts saves each step as a full-page PNG at 390 and 1280 px in docs/assets/walkthrough/. client.ts logs ApiProblem to the console in developmentThe app writes nothing to the console; the customer copy for a failure on our side carries the correlation id to quoteThe id is what support needs to find the request in the server's logsweb/src/api/problemCopy.ts, web/src/api/client.ts
React Router 7, with the wizard state /book?branch=&service=&date=React Router 8.4.0, with the time and the step in the URL too: &slot=&step=The web app was built on the release it pins (each library is at least two weeks old); keeping the time and step in the URL lets Back and a refresh land on the same step.web/package.json, web/README.md (UI design), README Versions
TanStack Query refetches on window focus, with data stale after 60 sNo refetch on focus; 30 s stale by default and 5 min for the branches; the time step shows a Refresh banner after ten minutes without activitySo times do not change under the customer's finger.web/src/app/queryClient.ts
The confirmation page stops polling after 60 s and shows 'Still sending' with a link to the inboxIt polls every 2 s while any message is PENDING, with no cap, and says the booking is already saved while it waitsNo reason is recorded; the dispatcher settles every message at SENT or FAILED within about 35 s, so polling ends by itself.web/src/api/queries.ts (useAppointment)
Components named ConfirmationPanel, SimulatedInbox, SlotGrid, CustomerDetailsForm, LookupForm, problem.ts and formatSastConfirmationPage, InboxPage, TimeGrid, DetailsStep, a form inside ManagePage, problemCopy.ts and lib/time.tsEach file is named for the page or booking step the UI design draws.web/README.md (Layout)
Per-IP limits and a WAF cut from v1AWS WAF in front of the hosted demo: /actuator blocked, 10 POSTs per IP in 5 minutes under /api/v1/appointments, and AWS managed rule groups; the app itself still has no per-IP limitA public demo needs an edge that keeps the actuator private and limits bookings, which belongs at the edge rather than in the app.ADR-0006, infra/README.md (The hosted demo)
A basic-auth demo gate in front of the hosted demoNo sign-in: the demo is open to anyone with the addressA sign-in prompt in front of a demo turns away the people it is for. With the inbox off no visitor can see another's booking, each booking is deleted once its appointment is over, and SMS go to South African numbers only, at most five a day to one numberADR-0006 Notes; infra/lib/app-stack.ts
CancelDialog: a native modal <dialog> with a focus trap that restates the appointment and the no-fee rule and links to that day's availability in a new tab; from the start time, 'This appointment has started or passed'CancelSection: an inline step under the appointment, a region whose heading takes focus, naming the appointment and saying that cancelling releases the time and cannot be undone; Escape and 'Keep my appointment' close it. No fee wording or availability link. From the start time Cancel is not offered, with no line saying why; a cancel refused as too late says 'This appointment has already started.'jsdom, where the web tests run, does not implement showModal(). No reason is recorded for leaving out the fee wording, the availability link and the started-or-passed line.web/src/pages/ConfirmationPage.tsx (CancelSection); web/README.md (end of UI design)
After a cancel: a CANCELLED status badge, 'We have queued a cancellation SMS', and 'Book again' with branch and service prefilled'This appointment was cancelled' and 'Its details have been deleted.', with the masked number the cancellation SMS is on its way to; a 'Cancelled' badge over the service, branch, date and time it was for, and when it was cancelled, in SAST; no reference, no Cancel button and no delivery status. 'Book this service again', with branch and service prefilled, and 'Book another appointment'. 'Book this service again' is on every appointment, cancelled or notThe server deletes the appointment as it cancels it, so the cancel's answer is the last it gives and nothing more can be watched; the tab remembers what was cancelled and when, without the name or the number, so a refresh shows the same page. The retry is one guarded UPDATE whose NOT EXISTS reads appointments, and the dispatcher's claim reads the appointment's status.Both ask booking's AppointmentDirectory. The retry locks the message FOR UPDATE, RetryPolicy decides, and an UPDATE ... RETURNING resets it. The dispatcher asks, after the claim and before the send, whether the appointment is cancelled; a question that fails leaves the message PENDING for the next tick.notification never reads booking's table. RetryPolicy also computes the inbox's retryable flag, so the Retry button and what it does cannot disagree. The runbook's SQL fallback keeps the guarded UPDATE.RetryPolicy, RetryMessageHandler and NotificationDispatcher Javadoc; docs/architecture.md, section 5
Log events appointment.cancelled {appointmentId}, outbox.capped {messageId, channel} and outbox.superseded {messageId}.cancel.deleted, cancel.gone and cancel.refused; outbox.capped {messageId, kind, channel, cap} at WARN, with stored=false for a cancellation that is never stored; outbox.confirmations_deleted {appointmentId, count} from the cancel; outbox.superseded {messageId} and outbox.deleted {messageId, kind} from the dispatcher; outbox.retry_reset and outbox.retry_unchanged; retention.purged, cap_ledger.purged, lookup_attempts.purged, lookup.count_not_settled and demo_data.*.Not recorded. Each event is named for the code that writes it, and none carries a name, a number or a reference (LogRedactionIT).CancelAppointmentHandler, NotificationOutbox, NotificationDispatcher, RetryMessageHandler, RetentionPurge, RetentionPurgeJob, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJob, LookupAttemptLimiter, LookupAttemptPurgeJob and DemoDataLoader
Cancel: CONFIRMED to CANCELLED with a version-checked UPDATE; a repeat cancel, or the loser of two concurrent cancels, is a 200 no-op; the transaction supersedes any PENDING or FAILED confirmation and queues the cancellation SMS; the same Idempotency-Key replays the booking with its current status.A cancel deletes the booking at once. In one transaction it locks the appointment's row with its version checked, deletes every confirmation of it whatever its status, writes the PENDING cancellation SMS and deletes the appointment, which frees the seat; the 200 answer is built from the appointment in memory before the commit. The cancellation SMS is kept, with no appointment, until it is sent or has failed for good, when the dispatcher deletes it, and the key keeps its 24 hours. From the commit the reference is unknown: a repeat cancel, the second of two concurrent cancels and a lookup each get a 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND counted as a wrong try, and the key with the same details gets 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED, so it never books again. V6 lets a cancellation SMS and a key outlive their appointment.The project owner's privacy decision of 27 October 2026: a cancelled booking is personal data with no further use, so it is destroyed when it is cancelled. A deleted reference answers exactly as one never issued, so the two cannot be told apart.BR-12, BR-13, BR-14 and BR-24; ADR-0003 and ADR-0006 Notes (2026-10-27, a cancel deletes the booking); V6__cancel_deletes_the_booking.sql
LookupAttemptLimiter in a bounded Caffeine cache (maximumSize 100_000, expireAfterWrite PT1H), in memory.The counts live in PostgreSQL, in lookup_attempts (V8): one row per reference with an open window, reached through the LookupAttemptStore port and locked with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE, each count in a transaction of its own. A lookup in flight holds its place for a 30-second lease at most, and LookupAttemptPurgeJob deletes closed windows every hour. Caffeine is no longer a dependency.The hosted demo runs two tasks, and counts in each task's memory would give each its own five tries and be lost on a restart. In PostgreSQL every instance shares them, as it already shares the dispatcher's claims, the idempotency keys and the seat locks.BR-13; ADR-0004 Consequences and ADR-0006 Notes (2026-10-27, two tasks); docs/architecture.md, section 4; V8__lookup_attempts.sql
Every failed send backs off 2, 4, 8 and 16 s, and the message goes FAILED on the fifth failed attempt.A refusal that sending again may cure still backs off to a fifth-attempt FAILED; the simulated gateway's refusal of a number ending 0000 is one, so the demo still shows five attempts. A refusal the gateway calls permanent marks the message FAILED with DELIVERY_FAILED on that attempt: from AWS, a ConflictException (such as a number the SMS sandbox has not verified), AccessDeniedException, ValidationException, ResourceNotFoundException or a monthly spend limit reached. The outbox.retry and outbox.failed lines give the refusal's fixed code.Sending again cannot cure these, so four more attempts only spent calls and showed Not delivered 30 seconds late; while the demo is in the SMS sandbox, every unverified number is refused this way.BR-17; ADR-0003 Notes (2026-10-27, a permanent refusal is not retried); AwsSmsGateway
One always-on Fargate task (desiredCount: 1), about $82 a month, under a $90 budgetTwo always-on Fargate tasks, one in each Availability Zone (desiredCount: 2). The lookup counts moved to PostgreSQL (V8), and the SMS cap's key comes from Secrets Manager, so nothing depends on one task. monthlyBudgetUsd is $110One task can fail or be replaced while the other servesADR-0006 Notes (2026-10-27, two tasks); infra/lib/app-stack.ts; infra/cdk.json

How this page stays current

  • At the end of every milestone, each section is checked against the code and its status updated. The header shows the commit it was last checked against.
  • A change that departs from the design updates this page in the same milestone, and adds a row to the table above.
  • The repository holds the detailed, versioned records: docs/architecture.md, the ADRs in docs/adr/, and docs/domain-rules.md, where every business rule is marked proven or planned and CI fails if a cited test does not exist.
  • A post-commit hook, enabled by make hooks, prints a reminder after every major commit, so a change this page describes is not missed between milestones.
  • Every edit to this page is listed under Changes to this page.

PlanPartly built

The plan on one page

Status. Built: the plan above through M6: the schema and its proofs, the catalogue and slot rules, booking with idempotency and lookup, the outbox and its dispatcher, the web app, and in M6 cancellation, retry, demo data, two metrics and the Playwright tests. After M6: real SMS through AWS End User Messaging SMS on the hosted demo at https://branchbook.click, at most five a day to one number, and deletion of each hosted booking once its appointment is over; a cancel that deletes the booking at once; the lookup limit's counts in PostgreSQL; and continuous deployment of the hosted demo, on two Fargate tasks, from main once every check passes. Not built: the Tier 3 email channel. M7 is built: the final documentation pass is merged, the clean-clone rehearsal passed, and v1.0.0 is tagged on 0991304.

With BranchBook, a customer of the fictional Fynbos Bank picks a branch, a service, a date and a slot, then enters a name and a South African mobile number. They get a persistent confirmation with reference APT-XXXX-XXXX and an SMS, delivered through a transactional outbox and shown live on the confirmation page, and they can cancel until the appointment starts. The SMS is simulated locally, where a simulated inbox lists it, and for the published demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 0199; the hosted demo sends every other South African mobile a real SMS through AWS End User Messaging SMS, which reaches only verified numbers while the AWS account is in the SMS sandbox. The system is one Spring Boot 4.1 jar serving a React 19/TypeScript SPA over one PostgreSQL 17 database, started with make up, and hosted as a demo at https://branchbook.click in AWS Cape Town (af-south-1). Double booking is impossible at the database (btree_gist EXCLUDE constraints). The handler makes the outcome deterministic: a per-mobile lock and the branch_services row lock come first, then the lowest free seat is chosen. A 20-thread Testcontainers race test proves it. Client retries are absorbed by an Idempotency-Key table. There is no login, no admin and no broker. Every cut is recorded as an ADR line, and BookAppointmentHandler.book reads top to bottom in ten numbered steps.

to scheduleA five-step flow: branch, service, date and time, your details, review. Slots respect trading hours, public holidays, a two-hour lead time, a 30-day horizon and per-service capacity./book POST /api/v1/appointments

Delivery status shows on the confirmation page and in a simulated inbox. On the hosted demo, a number outside the demo block 082 555 0000 to 0199 is sent a real SMS through AWS End User Messaging SMS, to South Africa only; the block stays simulated. The five hard problems, and the test that proves each one

ProblemMechanismProof
Two customers book the last seat at the same momentThe handler takes a lock on the customer mobile, then locks the branch_services row and picks the lowest free seat. Two EXCLUDE constraints reject any overlap the code might miss.ConcurrentBookingIT.twentyRacersForOneSeatProduceExactlyOne201
SchemaConstraintIT.overlappingSeatIsRejectedEvenWithoutTheApplication
A double-click or a mobile network retryThe Idempotency-Key row is the first insert of the transaction. The same key with the same body returns the same booking as a 201, re-rendered from the current appointment.IdempotencyIT.concurrentSameKey_bookOnceAndBothReceiveSameReference
A confirmation that gets lost, or is sent for a booking that rolled backThe SMS row commits with the booking. A dispatcher claims rows one at a time with SKIP LOCKED, retries after 2, 4, 8 and 16 seconds, and marks FAILED on the fifth failure, or at once on a refusal that sending again cannot cure. A cancel deletes its booking's confirmations with the booking, so none is sent after it.BookAppointmentIT.failureAfterTheSmsIsWrittenRollsTheSmsBackWithTheBooking

No personal data reaches the logs or error bodies. Design principles

The database decides; the handler only makes the outcome deterministic.

A seat invariant enforced in code is bypassed by the next feature or a raw insert; appointments_seat_no_overlap and appointments_customer_no_overlap cannot be. The per-mobile advisory lock and the branch_services FOR UPDATE lock in front give the loser a clean 409 (DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT, CUSTOMER_OVERLAP or SLOT_FULL with alternatives, in that fixed order) instead of a retry loop or an index-order-dependent code. SchemaConstraintIT proves the constraint is real. The one time-relative rule, one upcoming appointment per mobile per service, cannot be a constraint (now() is not immutable), so it is checked under the per-mobile lock and the overlap EXCLUDE stays as the backstop. The doc says so instead of pretending otherwise.

Booking is the transaction, communicating is the side effect, the outbox joins them.

notification_messages rows are written inside the booking and cancel transactions by a direct call through the booking-owned ConfirmationOutbox port, implemented by NotificationOutbox. A lost or orphaned confirmation is therefore impossible, and a cancel deletes its booking's confirmations with the booking, so none is sent after it. NotificationDispatcher claims, sends and marks one row per transaction (SKIP LOCKED, backoff, FAILED), so one bad row or a crash re-sends at most one message. Appointment status never depends on delivery status.

One finished vertical slice, built as a tracer bullet; every cut written down.

Reviewers read a platform built in place of a feature as poor judgement. By hour 19 a clean clone books in the browser and shows the persisted PENDING SMS on a real confirmation page. Proofs, dispatcher and polish follow. Scope is tiered: must = Tier 1, should = Tier 2 (built only if M5 is green by hour 25), could = Tier 3 (cut by default); the hours 25-27 Tier-2 slot is the swing buffer and M7 (hours 31-35) is reserved. Each out-of-scope item carries its reason and its production design in ADR-0001..0005 or the README 'production adds' section, which costs an hour instead of fifteen.

Every rule has a name and a test, and the names are checked.

docs/domain-rules.md is the single BR -> Class.method registry. scripts/check-test-refs.sh fails CI if a rule marked proven, or a README row not marked planned, cites a test that does not exist. Names read as requirements (twentyRacersForOneSeatProduceExactlyOne201, failedBookingLeavesNoKeyRow), so a reviewer who never runs the code still sees what is proven, and the README cannot cite a test that is not there.

Collect the minimum and prove it (POPIA s10, s11(1)(b), s18, s19).

Collect first name, last name and mobile; email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships. No ID number, no consent checkbox, no login. Show the mobile as '082 *** 0101' only to the booker and to a caller who has just proved it, and as '082 *** ****' in the inbox. Logs, MDC and metrics carry no recipient. No problem body reveals another booking's reference. LogRedactionIT runs on real PostgreSQL, so Hibernate, pgjdbc and server logs are checked too. The hosted demo deletes each booking, with its messages and idempotency keys, within the hour after its appointment ends, and its database backups expire after 7 days. A cancel deletes the booking at once, in every deployment: only its cancellation SMS outlives it, until it is sent or has failed for good, beside an unkeyed hash of the request under its Idempotency-Key and, where the daily cap is on, a keyed fingerprint of the number, each for about 24 hours. In production the bank's records and privacy officers would set the period (docs/production-notes.md).

Time is data, never arithmetic.

timestamptz for every instant. branch.timezone converts wall-clock hours. A java.time.Clock bean is injected everywhere and application SQL binds :now from it, never calling now(). TZ=UTC in the JVM, containers and CI, with the API's unit and API tests run again under TZ=America/New_York. Response records format instants at the branch offset themselves rather than trusting a Jackson setting. Web tests run under two zones. The bookable window comes from the server, and AvailabilityApiIT.rendersStartsAtWithJohannesburgOffset pins the literal '+02:00'.

Design for the reviewer's first fifteen minutes.

make up takes the measured cold time stated in the README (target under three minutes). A 90-second walkthrough uses exact two-tab steps at the capacity-1 Polokwane branch and a demo-numbers table. The README is under 300 lines and grows with each milestone. handle() fits on one screen, with docs/architecture.md listing every file on the path. A committed OpenAPI contract lets a reader check the hard claims without running anything, and scripts can be re-run, so the second try behaves like the first.

PlanPartly built

Requirements and scope

Status. Built: every Tier 1 functional requirement, with scripts/smoke.sh as recorded in the departures; all of Tier 2 in M6: cancel (FR-12), retry (FR-18), demo data (FR-28) and the two metrics (FR-30); from Tier 3, the election proclamation (FR-33), the per-recipient SMS cap and the cancel Playwright spec; and, beyond the plan, the failed-delivery and keyboard Playwright specs. Not built: the email channel (FR-37), the request-body limit (FR-36), notifications_dispatched_total, the slot-taken spec, the README GIF and the three README screenshots.

Scope is tiered so that a short week still ships something complete. Tier 1 is never cut. Tier 2 is built only if the proofs are green by hour 25. Tier 3 is cut by default. Requirement priorities map onto the tiers: must is Tier 1, should is Tier 2, could is Tier 3.

Tier 1Never cutThe booking path and all its proofs. Appointment lookup, the confirmation page and the read-only /manage lookup. The SMS outbox and dispatcher, the simulated inbox and the guarded runbook SQL that replays a failed message. The lookup rate limiter. Capacity-1 and capacity-3 race tests, demo-409.sh and smoke.sh. The book-appointment Playwright spec (built in M6), three README screenshots, and ADR-0001 to ADR-0007, all already written.
