ArchUnit beyond four rules, Mockito handler tests and a prod-profile context test. More than three feature PRs. Trivy runs only if everything is green at hour 30.
All 37 functional requirements, with their tier
IDRequirementTier
FR-1GET /api/v1/branches returns the six seeded active branches (code, name, suburb, city, province, opensSunday); client caches 5 min.Tier 1
FR-2GET /api/v1/branches/{code} returns address, timezone, operatingHours[], the services offered with durationMinutes and documentsNeeded[], bookableFrom/bookableTo (dates in branch.timezone from the server Clock: the half-open window [today, today + P30D), exactly 30 dates), and closedDates within that window with reason CLOSED_DAY | PUBLIC_HOLIDAY (name). Unknown code returns 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND.Tier 1
FR-3GET /api/v1/branches/{code}/availability?service=&date= returns one day's slots {startsAt, endsAt, seatsAvailable} with +02:00 offsets formatted by the response record (ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone), plus branchCode, serviceCode, date, timezone, generatedAt, closedReason|null and holidayName|null. A closed day is a 200 with no slots. Errors, in the order they are checked: 400 VALIDATION_FAILED, 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND, 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH (also for an unknown or inactive service code), 422 DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW.Tier 1
FR-4SlotCalendar generates slots at opens_at + k x duration that end by closes_at: last weekday 30-min start 16:30; last Saturday 45-min start 11:45 (12:30 is on the grid but SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS); last RUS Saturday 45-min start 11:00. It skips closed weekdays and public holidays (Schedule 1 + PROCLAMATION rows + the s2(1) derived Monday) and applies lead time PT2H and horizon [today, today + P30D) in branch.timezone with an injected Clock. SlotCalendar.validate returns exactly one code by fixed precedence: DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW > SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY > SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS > SLOT_OFF_GRID > SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME. Booking reuses the same function.Tier 1
FR-5POST /api/v1/appointments takes header Idempotency-Key (UUID) and body {branchCode, serviceCode, startsAt, customer{firstName, lastName, mobile}} (customer.email? is added only with FR-37). It returns 201 + Location + AppointmentResponse (reference, status, branch, service, startsAt/endsAt +02:00, customer with mobileMasked, canCancel, cancelledAt, notifications[]).Tier 1
FR-6At most branch_services.capacity active appointments overlap per (branch, service). The handler locks the branch_services row through the booking-owned BookingLocks port and selects the lowest free seat via generate_series NOT EXISTS. If none is free it returns 409 SLOT_FULL with up to three alternatives[], computed after rollback outside the transaction. appointments_seat_no_overlap is the backstop, mapped by constraint name.Tier 1
FR-7Per-customer rules are checked under pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)), taken after the idempotency insert and before the branch_services lock, in this order. First, one upcoming active appointment per (mobile, service): status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN') AND ends_at > :now, else 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT with no reference, branch or time in the body. Then no overlapping active appointment for the mobile at any branch, else 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP, with appointments_customer_no_overlap as the database backstop. 40P01/40001 cause one re-run, then 503 TRY_AGAIN. Past appointments never block.Tier 1
FR-8Idempotency: the idempotency_keys row is inserted as the first statement (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the stored key has expired, RETURNING) and stores request_hash, appointment_id and response_status. Same key + same SHA-256 request_hash re-renders the current AppointmentResponse with 201 + Idempotent-Replayed: true. A different hash, or the same one once the key's booking has been cancelled and so deleted, returns 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED and never books again; a missing or non-UUID key returns 400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED. A 409/422/503 rolls the key back. A key is honoured for exactly 24 hours, purged or not; the hourly IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob (DELETE ... WHERE expires_at <= :now) only keeps the table small.Tier 1
FR-9Every appointment gets APT-XXXX-XXXX from the 31-character alphabet via SecureRandom, stored uppercase with UNIQUE + CHECK regex. A collision re-runs the transaction once. Lookup input is trimmed and upper-cased, hyphens optional.Tier 1
FR-10MobileNumber.parse (libphonenumber, region ZA) accepts a number only when libphonenumber reports type MOBILE and the E.164 result matches ^\+27(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$ (the same regex as the CHECK). '082 555 0101', '0825550101', '+27 82 555 0101', '+27 (0)82 555 0101', '27825550101' and '0027825550101' all normalise to +27825550101. Landlines 01-05, VoIP 087 and 080/086 return 422 MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED. Only E.164 is stored. MobileNumber.masked() '082 *** 0101' (the mobileMasked field) is returned only to a caller who proved the number (POST 201, GET/cancel after the last-4 check); redacted() '082 *** ****' is used everywhere else.Tier 1
FR-11GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference} with header X-Mobile-Last4 returns the appointment with embedded notifications[{channel, kind, status, attempts, sentAt, failureReason, recipientMasked, body}]. An unknown reference, a cancelled one (the cancel deleted it) or wrong digits all return 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND. A malformed or missing header returns 400 VALIDATION_FAILED and is not counted. LookupAttemptLimiter (counts in PostgreSQL, in lookup_attempts from V8: one row per reference with an open window, shared by every instance, closed windows purged hourly) returns 429 LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED with Retry-After after 5 failures per reference per hour (the window opens at the first failure and does not slide). begin counts each lookup as in flight before the database read, and end turns it into a failure for wrong digits or releases it, so lookups that arrive together cannot pass the limit; one refused only because failures and lookups in flight reach five gets Retry-After: 1.Tier 1
FR-12POST /api/v1/appointments/{reference}/cancel with X-Mobile-Last4 loads the Appointment through JPA; Appointment.cancel(now) applies the guard (at or after startsAt it is 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL, and nothing changes). In one transaction the cancel then locks the appointment's row with its version checked, deletes every CONFIRMATION of it whatever its status, queues a CANCELLATION message and deletes the appointment with its version checked, which frees the seat; the 200 answer is built from the appointment in memory before the commit. The cancellation SMS is kept, with no appointment, until it is sent or has failed for good. From the commit the reference is unknown: a repeat cancel, the second of two concurrent cancels and a lookup each get a 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND that counts as a wrong try. On the appointment page, an inline step names the appointment and warns that cancelling releases the time and cannot be undone; the page then says the appointment was cancelled and its details deleted, and 'Book this service again' opens the date and time step with the branch and service chosen. Built in M6; the delete since V6.Tier 2
FR-13NotificationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation/enqueueCancellation write notification_messages rows (SMS always; EMAIL only with FR-37) inside the caller's transaction by direct call through the booking-owned ConfirmationOutbox port. DispatcherLoop runs NotificationDispatcher.runOnce every 2 s on outbox-1, a single thread it owns rather than a scheduler bean; branchbook.dispatcher.enabled switches it off, as the tests do. Each tick processes up to 20 rows, one transaction per row: claim 1 with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED WHERE next_attempt_at <= :now, send, mark with an immediate UPDATE, commit. Failures back off 2/4/8/16 s and the row goes FAILED on the fifth failed attempt, or on the first for a refusal the gateway calls permanent, such as AWS's for a number the SMS sandbox has not verified. A CANCELLATION is deleted where a confirmation would be marked SENT or FAILED. A mark failure is set FAILED (failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR, last_error 'MARK_FAILED') in a separate short transaction. last_error is truncated to 500 and provider_message_id to 64.Tier 1
FR-14NotificationTemplates (Locale.ENGLISH) render an SMS within 160 GSM-7 septets: service sms_name, branch sms_name, SAST time, reference, 'Bring ID + docs.' and the /manage link. On startup NotificationProperties (through NotificationTemplates.longestConfirmationSeptets) checks the worst case from the column bounds (service sms_name 18, branch sms_name 20) with the configured public base URL, and the app refuses to start if it would exceed one segment (the base URL budget is 28 septets). No message contains the full mobile number.Tier 1
FR-15SimulatedSmsGateway (150 ms, fails for recipients ending 0000) implements the MessageGateway port and returns a provider_message_id. Where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, as on the hosted demo, RoutingSmsGateway keeps the demo block 082 555 0000 to 0199 on it and sends every other number through AwsSmsGateway, AWS End User Messaging SMS (built after M6). SimulatedEmailGateway waits for FR-37.Tier 1
FR-16ConfirmationPage is a persistent page. Focus moves to the heading. It shows the reference, what, where and when in SAST, the documents, and the per-channel delivery status. Status is polled every 2 s while PENDING until SENT ('Sent at 10:42' with the rendered SMS) or FAILED (about 35 s for a 0000 recipient; the booking stands and the reference is the proof). Polling has no time cap: it stops once no message is PENDING.Tier 1
FR-17GET /api/v1/messages?limit=50 (limit 1-200; only when branchbook.inbox.enabled is true, as in compose, make dev-api and the tests; off by default, when the path is a plain 404) lists the newest messages first with id, reference, kind, channel, recipientMasked (redacted '082 *** ****'), subject, body, status, failureReason, retryable, attempts, nextAttemptAt, lastError, createdAt and sentAt. InboxPage at /messages shows them under a 'Demo operator view. It lists every booking's messages, recipients redacted, and is switched off in production. Here every message is simulated, as on any local run.' banner and polls every 2 s while any row is PENDING. Where branchbook.inbox.redact-references is on, every reference reads APT-****-****.Tier 1
FR-18Built in M6, behind the simulated inbox's switch: POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry exists only while branchbook.inbox.enabled is true, and resets a retryable FAILED message (failure_reason DELIVERY_FAILED or DISPATCH_ERROR) to PENDING, attempts 0, next_attempt_at :now, last_error and failure_reason NULL. PENDING, SENT, superseded or capped rows return a 200 no-op with retryable=false, and an unknown id, or a cancellation already deleted once final, returns 404 MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND. The inbox's Retry button, shown on a message the server marks retryable and named for that message, calls it and announces the outcome in a status region. Where the inbox is off, as on the hosted demo, the guarded runbook SQL is the replay path.Tier 2
FR-19The web wizard runs on /book?branch=&service=&date=&slot=&step= (URL-driven, survives refresh): BranchStep, ServiceStep (duration, documents), DateStrip rendering exactly bookableFrom..bookableTo with closed reasons, TimeGrid with 'Full' and '1 left', DetailsStep with an inline POPIA notice and no consent checkbox, and ReviewStep. Every query has four states (loading, empty, error, stale). No date logic uses the device clock.Tier 1
FR-20409 SLOT_FULL recovery stays on the review step: 'That time was just taken' with the alternatives[] as buttons and a link to all times; availability is refetched, the customer's details are preserved, and Confirm waits until a new time is chosen. useIdempotencyKey rotates the key only when the payload changes. It persists {payloadHash, key} with the draft in sessionStorage, so a refresh after a timed-out POST reuses the same key; both are cleared on 201.Tier 1
FR-21ManagePage has a lookup form (reference + last 4), which is Tier 1 because the confirmation SMS links to /manage; the right digits open the appointment page, which offers the Tier 2 cancel (built in M6). {reference, last4} is kept in sessionStorage so a refresh works, and arriving from the SMS /manage link prompts for both.Tier 1
FR-22PrivacyPage at /privacy carries the s18 notice naming 'Fynbos Bank (fictional demo)', the purpose, the voluntary fields, the customer's rights and the Information Regulator. It also says what outlives a booking: a cancelled booking is deleted at once but for its cancellation SMS, kept until it is final, and for about a day a one-way fingerprint of the booking's details and, on the hosted demo, a keyed fingerprint of the number.Tier 1
FR-23AppointmentStatus {CONFIRMED, CHECKED_IN, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_SHOW} with canTransitionTo and parameterised tests; only CONFIRMED -> CANCELLED is reachable through the API.Tier 1
FR-24Every error is RFC 9457 application/problem+json from ProblemDetailHandler, with type urn:branchbook:problem:<CODE>, code, correlationId, errors[] and alternatives[]. HTTP-level failures the API never returns on purpose (404 for an unknown path, 405, 406, 415) carry no type and no code, only the status, title, instance and correlationId. No problem body ever contains another appointment's reference, time or the caller's input. Codes and their HTTP statuses are catalogued in docs/errors.md: the 19 spine codes plus MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND, 20 in all (no PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE, because the request-body size filter is cut, and no MESSAGE_SUPERSEDED, because retrying a superseded row is a 200 no-op). 500 INTERNAL_ERROR carries only correlationId.Tier 1
FR-25CorrelationIdFilter accepts or generates X-Correlation-Id, puts it in MDC and echoes it on every response; processes outside a request (DemoDataLoader) use 'sys-<uuid>', and the dispatcher logs each message under the correlation id stored with it, the one of the booking that wrote it.Tier 1
FR-26docs/openapi.yaml is committed. OpenApiContractIT fails when the running API no longer generates it exactly, when a parameter pattern is not anchored, and when a real response sends a property its schema does not declare or lacks a required one. web/src/api/schema.d.ts is generated from it with openapi-typescript, and npm run check:api fails CI when the file is stale. make contract regenerates both after an intended change. Both checks are hard gates.Tier 1
FR-27Flyway seeds six fictional branches with per-weekday hours (Polokwane Savannah capacity 1; Sandton Summit and Umhlanga Ridge open Sunday 09:00-13:00; Rustenburg Bojanala Saturday 08:00-12:00; Gqeberha Baywater without HOME), six services at 15/30/45 min with documents and sms_name <= 18, capacities, and public_holidays 2026-2027 with a source column. A WARN is logged at start-up and every day at 06:00 SAST from 60 days before the booking window reaches the end of the holiday data, and again once it has passed it.Tier 1
FR-28DemoDataLoader (built in M6, behind branchbook.demo-data.enabled, which only compose sets) books three fixtures through the real BookAppointmentHandler on the next open day chosen by SlotCalendar, only at Bellville Kingsway and Umhlanga Ridge (never Polokwane, never Sandton). One recipient ends 0000. It uses correlation id 'sys-<uuid>', runs only when appointments is empty, books nothing where only demo numbers may book or where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, and logs instead of failing start-up.Tier 2
FR-29Actuator: health (the db indicator), readiness/liveness, and info with the build version. Readiness gates compose and CI. Health shows no component details in any environment (ApplicationStartsIT.healthRevealsNoComponentDetails), so there is no outbox health indicator; the simulated inbox and the SQL in docs/runbook.md show the outbox (ADR-0003 Notes).Tier 1
FR-30Custom Micrometer metrics at /actuator/prometheus, tiered by the delivery plan: appointments_booked_total{outcome} labelled from one BookingOutcome enum (confirmed|replayed|slot_full|duplicate|customer_overlap|validation|try_again) and the outbox_pending gauge are Tier 2, built in M6 (served under make up; the hosted demo serves only health and info); notifications_dispatched_total{channel,outcome} is Tier 3 (cut by default). Boot's default metrics are exposed regardless.Tier 2
FR-31SpaForwardingController serves index.html for /book, /appointments/:reference, /manage, /messages and /privacy; one origin, no CORS.Tier 1
FR-32scripts/smoke.sh writes nothing, so it runs against any instance, the hosted demo included. It checks readiness, the app and its routes, the branch and availability APIs and their error contract, that booking and lookup answer (with requests refused before the database is touched, and a lookup that is never counted), the OpenAPI document, closed operational endpoints (a 404 from the app or a 403 from an edge firewall), the metrics endpoint and the security headers. CI runs it before and after the end-to-end tests.Tier 1
FR-334 Nov 2026 election day seeded as PROCLAMATION with the 15 Sep 2026 Presidency statement cited in the migration comment (verified and seeded).Tier 3
FR-34Customer names are trimmed and NFC-normalised. U+2018, U+2019 and U+02BC are mapped to an ASCII apostrophe. Each name is 1-60 characters of Unicode letters plus combining marks, space, hyphen and apostrophe. The same rule runs in zod and in the domain; otherwise 400 VALIDATION_FAILED NAME_INVALID.Tier 1
FR-35scripts/demo-409.sh (make demo-409) discovers a Polokwane FICA slot with seatsAvailable = 1 at run time, searching back from the last bookable date, and fires 20 parallel curls with 20 distinct demo numbers (082 555 0100 to 0199, from a random start). It prints the slot and the 1 x 201 / 19 x 409 split and exits non-zero on any other split.Tier 1
FR-36Not built in v1: the delivery plan cuts the request-body size filter, so there is no 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE code; the README claims no in-app body limit and names it as an ingress (gateway/WAF) concern. If it were ever added, a body above 16 KB would return 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE through a counting input-stream wrapper that also covers chunked bodies.Tier 3
FR-37Email channel: the optional customer.email (trimmed, lower-cased, <= 254), EMAIL CONFIRMATION/CANCELLATION rows, NotificationTemplates email text (address, SAST window, documents, manage instructions, privacy line, 'simulated message' sentence) and SimulatedEmailGateway (fails for @fail.example) ship together or not at all (POPIA s10).Tier 3
All 18 non-functional requirements
IDRequirementRationale
NFR-1From a clean clone, make up produces a bookable system (docker compose up --build --wait gated on /readyz). Target: under three minutes cold on the rehearsal machine, helped by BuildKit cache mounts, -DskipTests and a layered jar. The README states the measured time. A GHCR prebuilt-image fallback is added only if it exceeds five minutes. APP_PORT is overridable via .env, and the raw commands sit beside each Makefile target for Windows.This is the hard gate: a reviewer who cannot run it quickly stops reading, and a time claim must be measured, not hoped.
NFR-2With 20 concurrent racers with distinct mobiles for the last seat, sent through MockMvc from twenty threads released together by a latch, after one warm-up booking: exactly one 201, 19 x 409 SLOT_FULL, zero 503, one appointment row and one SMS outbox row. The ITs run with the 2 s lock_timeout every session sets (ApplicationStartsIT.databaseSessionsGiveUpOnSlowLocksAndStatements). lockTimeoutReturns503TryAgainWithRetryAfter pins the 503 deterministically, and a raw-JDBC insert on a taken seat is rejected by appointments_seat_no_overlap.This is the one hard problem in the brief: two customers clicking at the same instant. A flaky proof is worse than none.
NFR-3All instants are timestamptz. Wall-clock hours are converted through branch.timezone. java.time.Clock is injected, and application SQL binds :now from it (NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest), with DEFAULT now() only on audit columns. TZ=UTC in the JVM, images and CI, and CI runs the API's unit and API tests again under TZ=America/New_York. Response records emit +02:00 via ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone with no Jackson time-zone setting, and web tests pass under TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York.Timezone bugs are the most common appointment-system defect, and a bank engineer notices them in two minutes. Two clocks would make the time tests lie.
NFR-4./mvnw test (unit and ArchUnit) runs without Docker in under 20 s: Surefire runs every *Test and Failsafe every *IT by their default patterns, and SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest (two ArchUnit rules) fails if a *Test is meta-annotated with @SpringBootTest or depends on org.testcontainers, or a test that starts the application is not named *IT. ./mvnw verify runs all *IT, OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT among them, on one shared Testcontainers PostgreSQL 17. In CI the api job finishes under six minutes and the full pipeline under fifteen, with api, web and stack running in parallel; stack runs the end-to-end tests.Fast feedback for whoever changes the code, and a reviewer without Docker still gets a green unit run.
NFR-5Every response carries X-Correlation-Id and every ProblemDetail carries correlationId. Every log line written during a request carries its correlation id, in Spring Boot's plain-text format; there is no JSON log. MDC holds only correlationId, never a recipient. Log lines name an appointment by its id and a message by messageId, kind and channel, never by its reference.Production-mindedness in an afternoon: a reviewer pastes an id into docker compose logs and sees one story with no personal data.
NFR-6Stateless Spring Security: permitAll on every request with the reason in SecurityConfig, CSRF off. CSP default-src 'self' (self-hosted font) with no inline style attributes in the SPA (enforced by lint); /swagger-ui/** gets style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline', with the reason stated. Also nosniff, frame-ancestors 'none', Referrer-Policy no-referrer and Cache-Control no-store on /api. Only health, info and prometheus are exposed, prometheus from M6 with the Tier 2 meters; the hosted demo's task exposes only health and info, and its WAF blocks /actuator. Strict JSON, parameterised queries only, compose ports bound to 127.0.0.1. Of the abuse controls for an SMS-sending endpoint, the per-recipient daily cap is built (five a day on the hosted demo); OTP and CAPTCHA are documented as production adds.Bank-appropriate basics without login theatre. One origin removes the CORS class of misconfiguration, and Playwright catches CSP breakage before Docker does.
NFR-7POPIA by construction: only name and mobile are stored (email only with FR-37); the mobile is masked or redacted outside the domain. No personal data appears in logs, problem bodies or fixtures beyond synthetic numbers, including database-side logs: pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false, Hibernate's SQL error log (org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error) OFF, and PostgreSQL started with log_error_verbosity=terse and log_min_error_statement=panic. LogRedactionIT checks the application's formatted log and PostgreSQL's container log. Data stays in the local container, or, on the hosted demo, in RDS for PostgreSQL in Cape Town (af-south-1), where each booking is deleted within the hour after its appointment ends and backups expire after 7 days. In every deployment a cancel deletes the booking at once, keeping only its cancellation SMS until that is final.A bank's customers expect their data to be protected first, and constraint DETAIL lines are where mobiles leak.
NFR-8Fail fast, never hang. HikariCP connectionTimeout is 3 s, with lock_timeout=2000 and statement_timeout=5000 on every session; all map to 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After, and 40P01/40001 get one re-run first. Bookings for other branch-services never wait on the hot row lock but share the 10-connection pool, so pool exhaustion surfaces as 503 within 3 s. Tomcat graceful shutdown lets in-flight bookings commit.Resilience in a bank means fast detection and containment, so a stuck lock must surface as an error that can be explained.
NFR-9WCAG 2.2 AA text contrast and 3:1 control edges, 44 px targets, 390 px first, a slot grid of native radio inputs (keyboard-navigable with no custom key handling), aria-live delivery status, focus moved to the confirmation heading, no console errors and no CSP violations; one axe scan on the happy path. Built in M6: axe finds no serious or critical WCAG 2.2 AA violation on the review step or the confirmation page at 390 and 1280 px, and a keyboard-only booking passes.Customers book from their phones, and a flow that works with a keyboard and a screen reader is one every customer can finish.
NFR-10Contract stability: docs/openapi.yaml is compared exactly by OpenApiContractIT (rewritten by make contract); generated-client drift fails npm run check:api in CI; error codes and their HTTP statuses are stable and catalogued.Contract testing becomes literal and visible, and type drift between SPA and API becomes impossible.
NFR-11Pinned and current by a scaffold-day rule: Spring Boot 4.1.x latest patch; Java 25 LTS (the day-0 spike passed); postgres:17-alpine exact patch tag; Node 24 LTS via .nvmrc with npm ci; Actions by major tag. Every version is recorded in a README Versions table. Dependabot is weekly, with minor and patch updates grouped and deliberate major pins ignored. No latest, no H2, no ddl-auto=update.An out-of-support version is a security finding in a bank, and uneven currency (Boot 4.1 beside old majors) raises the same concern.
NFR-12Bounded responses: availability is one day; messages limit 1-200; the branches list is six rows; the bookable window is 30 dates; the lookup limiter's table holds one row per reference with an open window, and closed windows are purged hourly; no unbounded ranges anywhere.Smallest responses and simplest cache keys; it behaves like software that has run in production.
NFR-13A confirmation is dispatched within about 2 s of commit. Delivery is at-least-once and idempotent on message id. A crash or database error mid-tick re-sends at most one message, two dispatcher instances never double-send, and a confirmation is never delivered after its appointment's cancellation.The customer watches the status flip, so the outbox must survive a crash, a poisoned row and horizontal scale without spamming.
NFR-14Maintainability: four ArchUnit rules (no module cycles, domain free of Spring, api never touches infrastructure, transactions only in application). The ten steps are the private BookAppointmentHandler.book, which fits one screen, each line commented with its step number; handle adds the one re-run around it. Spotless and Prettier run in CI. The README stays under 300 lines and every claim is backed by a file or a checked test name.A reviewer must find the booking quickly, and the code must read as one deliberate author.
NFR-16Deterministic tests: DatabaseReset truncates notification_messages, idempotency_keys, appointments, sms_recipient_sends and lookup_attempts before and after each test of a class that books, which also resets the cap's ledger and the lookup limiter, and forgets scripted references. MutableClock is reset to 2026-10-26T06:00:00Z. concurrentSameKey forces its interleaving with BookingPause, a test-only trigger that holds the first booking at step 10 on an advisory lock, and polls pg_stat_activity until the second backend waits on the key's lock, and twoDispatchers forces its interleaving with a gateway latch. Dispatcher ITs call runOnce(now) with the instant advanced 2/4/8/16 s and never sleep.A shared container, singletons and wall-clock sleeps make ITs order-dependent and flaky on submission day, and concurrency tests that can pass sequentially prove nothing.
NFR-17Traceable claims: docs/domain-rules.md is the single BR -> Class.method registry. scripts/check-test-refs.sh, run in CI, fails if a rule marked proven, or a README row not marked planned, cites a Class.method that does not exist under api/src/test.The README's credibility rests on its cited test names existing.
NFR-18Toolchain freeze: the Boot version, the Java version (25) and every image tag are decided in the day-0 spike. Fallbacks run 4.1.x, then 4.0.x, never 3.5.x, decided before the first domain commit and recorded in the README. No dependency, image or JDK change after hour 30; Dependabot PRs are triaged before then or left closed.A submission-day toolchain change is a self-inflicted red build.

Assumptions

  • Branch hours are representative per-branch data (Monday to Friday 08:00 to 17:00, Saturday 08:00 to 13:00, Sunday closed unless a row exists), not any real bank's official hours; the README says so.
  • Every seeded branch uses Africa/Johannesburg; the timezone column exists so the no-DST assumption is data, not code.
  • Services run 15, 30 or 45 minutes and define their own slot grid. Each service has dedicated desks: capacity is the number of concurrent seats (consultants) per service per branch. A branch-wide consultant pool is the documented production model, and it is why ranges, not start-time equality, carry the invariant.
  • Lead time is PT2H and the horizon is the half-open [today, today + P30D), exactly 30 bookable dates (BookingProperties); both are environment-configurable and the server supplies the window to the SPA.
  • Public holidays are seeded for 2026 and 2027 from gov.za Schedule 1; branches.open_on_public_holidays defaults false.
  • A customer is identified by an unverified normalised mobile; there is no OTP, login or account linkage. The per-number rules therefore bound honest customers only; beyond the hosted demo's per-recipient SMS cap and WAF limit, abuse controls are production adds.
  • Past appointments stay CONFIRMED in v1 because no sweeper is built; every per-customer rule filters on ends_at > :now, so this is harmless.
  • SMS is the only Tier 1 channel. Email is Tier 3 and its field exists only if the channel ships. Locally, and for the demo numbers everywhere, no real provider is called; since 27 October 2026 the hosted demo texts every other South African mobile through AWS End User Messaging SMS.
  • Demo numbers are synthetic: 082 555 0101, 0102 and 0103 always succeed (one upcoming booking per service per number), any number ending 0000 always fails on the simulated gateway, and the Tier 2 DemoDataLoader books three fixed numbers of its own, the last ending 0000.
  • A reader may never run the code, so the screenshots, test names and walkthrough must carry the demo.
  • The budget is about 35 focused hours over one week. Scope is tiered (must/should/could): Tier 2 only if M5 is green by hour 25, Tier 3 cut by default, M7 (hours 31-35) reserved, no new feature after hour 26 (9 h before the end), no toolchain change after hour 30, and no should item starts while any must item is red.
  • Testcontainers requires Docker for every *IT (OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT included); unit tests and make up do not.

Out of scope, and why

ItemWhy it is out
Customer login (JWT, OIDC, OTP)Not in the brief, and a one-week implementation is security theatre. Reference + last-4 with 404-not-403 and a limiter is proportionate, and no problem body reveals a reference. The OIDC production path is ADR-0004 and one README paragraph.
Staff or admin endpoints and UI (check-in, complete, no-show sweeper, listings, API keys)The brief asks for a customer feature, not a platform, and staff transitions need an actor the brief does not include. The state machine and its tests exist. The endpoints and a 15-minute NO_SHOW job are described in next steps, and per-customer rules filter on ends_at > :now so unswept past rows do no harm.
Reschedule endpointCancel plus rebook covers the need. The appointment page's cancel step warns that cancelling releases the time, and 'Book this service again' opens the date and time step with the branch and service chosen. The atomic seat swap (acquire new, release old, same transaction, same reference) is written up in ADR-0002 consequences as the next feature.
Reminder messages 24 h beforeIt needs a second scheduler plus time-travel tests, for marginal demo value. The outbox kind column already accommodates REMINDER.
Ad-hoc branch_closures table with cascade cancellationPublic holidays and per-branch hours already prove the closed-day logic. The cascade would reuse the same outbox and is described, not built.
SA ID number collection, Luhn validation, encryption at rest, HMAC lookupPOPIA s10 minimality: the booking does not need it, and identity is verified in branch. The validator and the AES-GCM/HMAC design are documented in the production notes.
Retention/de-identification job, append-only audit table with trigger, split database rolesBank-grade on paper, but each one is an hour the UI and tests need. Built for the hosted demo only: an hourly job deletes each booking, with its messages and idempotency keys, once its appointment is over, and backups expire after 7 days. In every deployment a cancel deletes the booking at once. De-identification, the audit table and split roles are not built; docs/production-notes.md leaves the retention period to the bank's records and privacy officers.
.ics calendar attachmentRoughly an hour that competes with UI states. SAST correctness is already proven by tests and the +02:00 literal. It is first in next steps.
Real SMS/email providers, Mailpit/MailHog SMTP, WhatsAppThe brief says simulated, and locally it stays so. Real SMS was built after all for the hosted demo: AwsSmsGateway, one class behind MessageGateway chosen by configuration, with credentials from the ECS task role rather than secrets (ADR-0003 Notes). Mailpit adds a container and is still not SMS, and WhatsApp needs Meta template approval.
Kafka/RabbitMQ, microservices, Spring Modulith, Kubernetes manifests, Terraform, API gatewayDisproportionate for one deployable and one consumer. The dispatcher's claim loop is the documented Kafka/MSK seam (ADR-0003), and the Dockerfile is the EKS seam.
Public live demo on free-tier hostingA sleeping instance makes the reviewer's first click look broken, and a fake bank collecting real phone numbers outside South Africa is a bad look. The hosted demo at https://branchbook.click runs on AWS in Cape Town instead (ADR-0006), and compose, the PNGs and the walkthrough still carry it.
Per-IP rate limiting (Bucket4j), request-body size filter, WAF, Trivy image scans, coverage gates, mutation and load testsThese are gateway or platform concerns, or theatre for a take-home. Only the lookup limiter, keyed on the reference, is in-app; the hosted demo's AWS WAF allows 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes. Trivy is optional in the last hour if CI is otherwise green.
Dark mode, i18n, nearest-branch geolocation, maps, month-end hours overrides, HELD/REQUESTED states, waiting listsNo reviewer value inside 35 hours. Booking is single-step and transactional, so no hold timer is needed. These are listed as non-goals with one-line reasons.
Overall cap of N active appointments per customerAn application-level count across services adds a policy the brief does not ask for. The per-service 'one upcoming appointment' rule under the per-mobile lock and the customer-overlap EXCLUDE constraint already stop one number holding parallel bookings.
SMS abuse controls (OTP verification of the mobile before CONFIRMED, per-recipient and per-prefix daily SMS caps, CAPTCHA on POST, provider geo/fraud controls, slot holds limited per verified identity)Locally the simulated gateway sends nothing, so there is no cost or harassment exposure. The hosted demo sends real SMS, so there the exposure is real. In production, an unauthenticated endpoint that texts any typed number is the SMS-pumping and slot-squatting vector, so these controls are written into ADR-0004 and the README production section. Built for the hosted demo since 27 October 2026: the per-recipient cap (five SMS a day), a protect configuration that allows SMS to South Africa only, a maximum price per message, the WAF limit and AWS's monthly spend limit. OTP, CAPTCHA, per-prefix caps and slot holds are not built (ADR-0004 Notes).

PlanBuilt

Stack decision

Status. Built: every layer, on the versions in the README. Added in M2: springdoc 3.2.0, openapi-typescript 7.13.0 and openapi-fetch 0.17.0. Added in M3: libphonenumber 9.0.41 and Caffeine 3.2.4, removed after M6 when the lookup counts moved to PostgreSQL (V8). Added in M4: React Router 8.4.0, TanStack Query 5.104.2, react-hook-form 7.90.0 and zod 4.6.6, with Vitest 5.0.5, Testing Library and MSW 3.0.3 for the web tests. Added in M6: Playwright 1.64.0 and @axe-core/playwright 4.13.0 for the end-to-end tests; after M6, the AWS SDK for Java v2 2.55.15 for real SMS.

Java and Spring Boot on PostgreSQL, with React on the front end, is a conventional and well-supported stack for banking software, so the design's value lies in correctness, restraint and clear writing. Spring Boot 4.1.x is the current OSS line (3.5.x ended June 2026; 4.0.x superseded), and the day-0 spike confirmed Java 25 LTS with every plugin. Nothing in the design needs a 4.x-only API, and instants are formatted explicitly. Maven and npm create vite defaults remove toolchain surprises, and every resolved version is recorded. The invariants live in plain PostgreSQL (EXCLUDE, ON CONFLICT, FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, advisory locks) inside Flyway SQL, and the contract lives in docs/openapi.yaml with generated types, so a .NET or Vue port re-implements adapters, not the design.

Recommended stack

LayerChoiceNotesWhy
LanguageJava 25 LTS (Eclipse Temurin), confirmed by the day-0 spikeDecided once in the day-0 spike, which compiles JaCoCo, Spotless/google-java-format and ArchUnit against the chosen JDK. spring.threads.virtual.enabled=true. No toolchain change after hour 30, and no M7 bump.Java 25 has been the current LTS for a year, and the day-0 spike showed every plugin works on it. Deciding on day 0 removed the risk of a late toolchain change.
Back-end frameworkSpring Boot 4.1.x (latest patch)Modular starters exactly as Initializr generates them (webmvc, validation, data-jpa with Hibernate 7, actuator, security, flyway + flyway-database-postgresql, postgresql, testcontainers). Jackson 3 (tools.jackson) and JUnit 6 are Boot-managed. Fallback: 4.0.x (same major, still OSS-supported at submission), never 3.5.x, decided before the first domain commit and recorded in the README.3.5.x OSS support ended June 2026 and 4.0.x is superseded. Nothing here needs a 4.x-only API, and instants are formatted explicitly, so no Jackson-version-specific setting is relied on.
Build toolMaven 3.10 wrapper (./mvnw)Surefire runs *Test and Failsafe runs *IT (OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT among them) by their default patterns, and SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest (two ArchUnit rules) guards the split. Spotless + google-java-format. JaCoCo report only. spring-boot-maven-plugin build-info for /actuator/info.The Initializr default and the least-surprise shape for a Spring reviewer. The default name patterns need no include or exclude list, and the guard keeps the unit run free of Docker.
DatabasePostgreSQL 17postgres:17-alpine pinned to the exact patch tag, started with -c log_error_verbosity=terse -c log_min_error_statement=panic. CREATE EXTENSION btree_gist in V1. HikariCP connectionTimeout 3 s with options -c lock_timeout=2000 -c statement_timeout=5000 on every session, the ITs included. pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false.The current mature major that maps to Aurora PostgreSQL. EXCLUDE constraints over tstzrange make the seat and customer-overlap invariants unbypassable, and the logging flags keep constraint DETAIL lines (full mobiles) out of logs.
MigrationsFlyway versioned SQLV2__schema.sql (eight tables, named constraints, varchar + CHECK instead of char(n), documents_needed as text[], and only a coarse 320-character SMS guard instead of a one-segment CHECK), V3__reference_data.sql, V4__public_holidays_2026_2027.sql; after M6, V5 to V8: an index for the SMS cap's count (V5, dropped by V7), a cancel that deletes the booking (V6), the cap's ledger sms_recipient_sends (V7) and the lookup counts lookup_attempts (V8), ten tables and 61 named constraints in all. spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=validate. No H2 anywhere.The SQL under test is the SQL in production, and constraint names become part of the application contract.
Data accessSpring Data JPA (Hibernate 7) for the Appointment aggregate only + JdbcClient for the catalogue, the outbox, the idempotency keys and the native statementsOnly Appointment is a JPA entity (an Integer @Version, null until persist, marks it new); notification_messages, idempotency_keys, sms_recipient_sends, lookup_attempts and the catalogue use JdbcClient only. AppointmentRepository.insert does persist + flush and translates SQLSTATE 23505/23P01/23503/40P01/40001 into domain exceptions inside the adapter. Cancel applies Appointment.cancel to the loaded entity, detaches it, locks its row with SELECT ... WHERE id and version FOR UPDATE, and deletes it with the version checked (a concurrent cancel finds the row gone and gets a counted 404). The catalogue is read as plain records via JdbcClient. Natives: the idempotency claim INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the stored key has expired RETURNING, pg_advisory_xact_lock, branch_services FOR UPDATE, the lowest-free-seat generate_series, the per-row outbox claim FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 1, retry reset, the cancel's version-checked lock and delete, and the lookup count's SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on lookup_attempts. Every native binds :now from the Clock. open-in-view off. TransactionTemplate in handlers at READ COMMITTED.Every write reaches PostgreSQL at the line it is written, so violations surface where they are translated. The validate surface is one table (appointments), and the SQL that carries invariants stays visible.
ValidationBean Validation + domain value objects + database constraintsRequest records use @NotNull/@NotBlank/@Pattern plus @PersonName (NAME_INVALID) and @IsoOffsetDateTime (OFFSET_REQUIRED). MobileNumber uses libphonenumber 9.x (region ZA, MOBILE type AND the CHECK regex). CustomerName does NFC normalisation, maps curly apostrophes and accepts letters plus marks. AppointmentReference uses a regex. FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES. CHECK/EXCLUDE are the last line, and ConstraintViolationTranslator reads ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint().Three layers with different jobs: shape at the edge, meaning in the domain, truth in the database. The domain and the CHECK use one regex, so a library bump cannot produce an unmapped 23514.
API docs and error modelspringdoc-openapi 3.x (the Boot 4 line) + RFC 9457 ProblemDetail/v3/api-docs and /swagger-ui.html. OpenApiContractIT fails when the live spec differs from docs/openapi.yaml; make contract runs it with -Dopenapi.update=true to rewrite the file instead. ProblemDetailHandler emits code, correlationId, errors[] and alternatives[], never another booking's reference. docs/errors.md is the catalogue of codes and statuses, kept in step with ProblemCode by ProblemCodeCatalogueTest. Instants are strings formatted ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone, with @Schema(format=date-time).The committed spec feeds the generated TypeScript client, so contract testing is literal. Explicit offset formatting survives Jackson 2 and 3.
Front-end frameworkReact 19 + TypeScript strict (no any)React Router 8 with URL-driven wizard state /book?branch=&service=&date=&slot=&step=; TypeScript major as scaffolded and recordedReact is the most widely used choice for this kind of app, and strict TS keeps the generated types honest.
Front-end buildVite (the major npm create vite scaffolds, recorded)Defaults verbatim. Node 24 LTS via .nvmrc, npm ci with package-lock. Dev proxy /api -> :8080. dist is copied into the jar's /static.One origin in prod and dev, no CORS anywhere, no nginx to defend. Node 24 is the active LTS, while Node 22 is in maintenance.
Data fetching and API clientTanStack Query 5 + openapi-typescript 7 + openapi-fetchNo refetch on focus (the time step offers Refresh after ten minutes instead), 30 s stale by default and 5 min for the branches, 2 s polling while a message is PENDING (confirmation and inbox). schema.d.ts is committed and drift-checked in CI. client.ts (built in M2) returns a success's body or throws an ApiProblem; the booking call adds Idempotency-Key. A 503 is retried once after Retry-After with the same key.Server state and polling without hand-written caching, and zero hand-written response types.
Formsreact-hook-form 7 + zod 4Schemas mirror the server codes and the CustomerName rule. zod runs jitless, because the CSP refuses the new Function probe its compiler makes. 422 errors[] are mapped onto fields with setError. problemCopy.ts maps every code to plain-language copy. The idempotency key and payload hash are persisted with the draft in sessionStorage.Instant feedback while the server stays authoritative; UI and API can never disagree silently.
StylingCSS Modules + custom-property tokens + @fontsource/nunito-sanstokens.css: primary #1E6B52, accent #B8405E (invalid-field borders and destructive actions only; error text #9A2F4A), warn #775C10, text #2B2B2B, surface #F6F7F5, border #DDE2DC for decorative edges, control #7D867F and control-soft #87908A for input and tile edges. 390 px first, WCAG 2.2 AA text contrast and 3:1 control edges, 44 px targets. The mockups are in web/README.md, UI design. No inline style attributes (react/forbid-dom-props style), no Tailwind, no component kit.The fictional palette avoids any real bank's brand colours, and no inline styles means CSP default-src 'self' holds in Docker exactly as in dev.
Unit testsJUnit (Boot-managed, 6.x) + AssertJ; Vitest (scaffolded major) + React Testing Library 16 + MSW 3The backend unit run takes under 20 s without Docker, with three mocked handler tests, BookAppointmentHandlerTest for a replay whose booking a cancel deletes before it is read, LookupAppointmentHandlerTest for a lookup whose read fails and CancelAppointmentHandlerTest for a cancel's response and a version conflict that answers a counted 404, and the AWS SMS gateway tested against a mocked AWS client (the ITs cover the rest end to end). The web suite runs twice (TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York). scripts/check-test-refs.sh checks the BR registry in CI.Pure tests for rules, plus network-level mocks so the real openapi-fetch client is exercised.

Failsafe *IT. ConcurrentBookingIT: warm-up booking, 20 distinct mobiles, CountDownLatch. Forced interleavings via BookingPause (a test-only trigger holds a booking at step 10 on an advisory lock) with pg_stat_activity polling, and gateway latches. SchemaConstraintIT uses raw JDBC. LogRedactionIT reads the application's formatted log and PostgreSQL's container log.Everything the database guarantees is proven against the real engine, never H2, and order-independently.
E2E testsPlaywright 1.64 (Chromium) + @axe-core/playwright 4.13Runs against compose in two projects, a 390 by 844 phone and a 1280 by 800 desktop, both in the New York time zone, one test at a time. book-appointment (both projects) books from the landing page, refuses a landline, waits for Sent and opens the booking on /manage; axe finds no serious or critical WCAG 2.2 AA violation on the review step or the confirmation page. cancel (both), keyboard and failed-delivery (desktop) are built too; slot-taken is not. Every test fails on a CSP violation, an uncaught error or an undeclared console error. Fixtures pick a random demo number from 082 555 0100 to 0199 and a free slot from the API. On CI a failed test is retried once and keeps its trace. SCREENSHOTS=1 writes the walkthrough PNGs to docs/assets/walkthrough. CI runs smoke.sh before and after Playwright, then demo-409.sh.Playwright drives a real browser, so the brief's end-to-end claim is proven on the stack that ships.
Architecture testsArchUnit 1.5Four rules: modulesAreFreeOfCycles, domainHasNoSpringImports, controllersNeverReachInfrastructure (api never touches infrastructure), onlyApplicationOpensTransactions. Cross-module access through application types is a convention, documented and not tested.Cheaper to explain than Spring Modulith; enforces the dependency graph without an exception list.
CIGitHub Actions.github/workflows/ci.yml runs four parallel jobs on every push to main and every pull request, with concurrency that cancels an obsolete run except on main, where runs queue, and Actions pinned by major tag: api (setup-java with the chosen JDK, ./mvnw -B -ntp verify with the contract check, then the unit and API tests again under TZ=America/New_York, then check-test-refs.sh); web (setup-node 24, check:api, lint, tsc, vitest x2, build); stack (docker compose up --build --wait, smoke.sh, Playwright at 390 and 1280 px, smoke.sh again, demo-409.sh, a credential grep of the logs, and the logs, report and traces on failure); infra (the CDK app's lint, type check and tests, then a synth of every stack). On a push to main, once all four pass, deploy builds the ARM64 image on an ARM runner, deploys the hosted demo through OpenID Connect, smoke-tests it and records the commit in BranchbookRelease. Dependabot weekly, minor and patch grouped.The review happens on GitHub. The api job finishes under 6 min and the pipeline under 15.
ContainersThree-stage Dockerfile + docker composeStages: node:24.22.1-alpine -> eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jdk-alpine with the Maven wrapper (BuildKit cache mounts, -DskipTests package, layered extract) -> eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jre-alpine. Runs as non-root uid 10001, TZ=UTC, HEALTHCHECK /readyz. Compose: app (127.0.0.1:${APP_PORT:-8080}) + db (no host port, pg_isready, terse logging). Makefile up/down/logs/psql/smoke/demo-409/dev-api/dev-web/test/test-api/test-web/test-docs/contract/fmt/hooks/secrets-scan; .env.example placeholders.Two services, one command, a measured cold start, and nothing reachable from the LAN.
ObservabilityActuator + Micrometer Prometheus + ECS structured loggingHealth with db, readiness/liveness, info; no component details in any environment, so the simulated inbox and docs/runbook.md show the outbox. Boot default metrics; custom appointments_booked_total{outcome} (one BookingOutcome enum, counted by BookingOutcomeMeter) and outbox_pending (OutboxPendingGauge), built in M6 and served at /actuator/prometheus everywhere but the hosted demo, and notifications_dispatched_total{channel,outcome} in Tier 3. Spring Boot's plain-text log, with the correlation id in every line written during a request (logging.pattern.correlation). CorrelationIdFilter + MDC without recipients. Hibernate's SQL error log (org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error) OFF.An afternoon of production-mindedness that maps to a Prometheus-scraping estate, with no personal data in any log line.
SecuritySpring Security (stateless) + a PostgreSQL table for the lookup limitpermitAll on every request with the reason in SecurityConfig; CSRF off. CSP default-src 'self', with /swagger-ui/** allowed style-src 'unsafe-inline'. nosniff, frame-ancestors 'none', Cache-Control no-store on /api. LookupAttemptLimiter: 5 failures per reference per hour, counted in lookup_attempts (V8), shared by every instance, with closed windows purged hourly.Proportionate to a loginless brief. The headers and the limiter are what a bank security review checks, and a table that holds only open windows, purged hourly, cannot exhaust memory and gives every instance the same five tries; the residual risk (someone who already holds a reference can block lookups for it for an hour) is documented.
SMS providerAWS SDK for Java v2 2.55.15 (pinpointsmsvoicev2), used only where branchbook.sms.gateway is awsAwsSmsGateway calls SendTextMessage of AWS End User Messaging SMS in af-south-1: TRANSACTIONAL, a maximum price of US$0.20 a message, through a protect configuration that allows South Africa only, with a dry-run switch. Credentials come from the SDK's default chain, the ECS task role on the hosted demo. The JDK's URL-connection client replaces the SDK's Apache and Netty clients; the SDK does not retry, and a call gives up after 10 s. A refusal sending again cannot cure, such as a number the SMS sandbox has not verified or a monthly spend limit reached, is permanent, so the dispatcher marks the message FAILED at once. RoutingSmsGateway keeps the demo block 082 555 0000 to 0199 simulated. Pinned in api/pom.xml.A real provider is one more MessageGateway chosen by configuration, as ADR-0003 expected. af-south-1 keeps the numbers in South Africa, and the task role means no stored secret.
Lint and formatESLint 10 flat + typescript-eslint + jsx-a11y-x; Prettier 3; Spotless google-java-formatAll enforced in CI; make fmt locally; react/forbid-dom-props style as an errorSixty files read as one deliberate author, and a11y lint catches dead buttons and missing labels.
Version policyScaffold-day versions table in the READMEDay-0 spike (one hour, go/no-go): Initializr 4.1 project with all plugins, one @ServiceConnection IT on postgres:17-alpine, an EXCLUDE USING gist created by Flyway, Hibernate validate on uuid/timestamptz/varchar (the appointments columns), npm run lint clean, and arm64 manifests checked. Record every resolved version and then freeze; no changes after hour 30.Currency without surprise. Readers see exact versions and why each was chosen, and nobody debugs a toolchain on submission day.
Version pinning and the toolchain freeze

Pinned and current by a scaffold-day rule: Spring Boot 4.1.x latest patch; Java 25 LTS (the day-0 spike passed); postgres:17-alpine exact patch tag; Node 24 LTS via .nvmrc with npm ci; Actions by major tag. Every version is recorded in a README Versions table. Dependabot is weekly, with minor and patch updates grouped and deliberate major pins ignored. No latest, no H2, no ddl-auto=update.

Toolchain freeze: the Boot version, the Java version (25) and every image tag are decided in the day-0 spike. Fallbacks run 4.1.x, then 4.0.x, never 3.5.x, decided before the first domain commit and recorded in the README. No dependency, image or JDK change after hour 30; Dependabot PRs are triaged before then or left closed.

DesignBuilt

Architecture

Status. Built: every module, the four ArchUnit rules, the web app served from the jar and, in M6, cancellation, the retry of a failed message, the demo data and the two meters; after M6, the AWS End User Messaging SMS gateway behind a routing gateway, and the retention purge, both switched on for the hosted demo only. Also after M6: a cancel that deletes the booking in the same transaction (V6), the daily cap's ledger sms_recipient_sends (V7), and the lookup limit's counts in lookup_attempts (V8), shared by every instance. The same jar runs the hosted demo, as two Fargate tasks in Cape Town. Not built: the Tier 3 email channel.

Modular monolith: one Spring Boot deployable, one PostgreSQL database, and one SPA served from the same jar. Packages are organised by capability (catalogue, scheduling, booking, notification, shared), each with api/application/domain plus infrastructure where it has adapters (scheduling has none). One dependency graph, enforced by four ArchUnit rules: scheduling -> catalogue; booking -> catalogue, scheduling; notification -> booking; all -> shared. Synchronous REST on the customer path. In the background run the in-process transactional outbox dispatcher, the seam where Kafka/MSK would attach, and scheduled jobs: the holiday-coverage check, the hourly purges of expired idempotency keys, of the daily cap's ledger rows once 24 hours old and of closed lookup windows, and, where a deployment switches it on, the hourly retention purge.

The browser loads the React SPA from the API jar and calls /api/v1 on the same origin. AppointmentController validates the request and passes a BookAppointmentCommand to BookAppointmentHandler, which runs one TransactionTemplate at READ COMMITTED in ten numbered one-line steps: (1) INSERT idempotency_keys ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the stored key has expired, RETURNING; (2) load the active branch and its offer; (3) SlotCalendar.validate with fixed code precedence; (4) pg_advisory_xact_lock on the mobile; (5) DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT then CUSTOMER_OVERLAP pre-checks filtered on ends_at > :now; (6) BookingLocks: SELECT branch_services FOR UPDATE; (7) lowest free seat via generate_series NOT EXISTS, else SLOT_FULL; (8) AppointmentRepository.insert, which persists, flushes and translates SQLSTATEs into domain exceptions; (9) ConfirmationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation by direct call; (10) complete the idempotency row (appointment_id, 201), then commit. Every statement binds :now from the Clock. The lock order is fixed (idempotency PK -> mobile -> branch_services); 40P01/40001 cause one re-run, then 503. Alternatives for a 409 are computed after rollback, outside the transaction. PostgreSQL holds the invariants: appointments_seat_no_overlap, appointments_customer_no_overlap and the composite FK. DispatcherLoop runs NotificationDispatcher every 2 s on outbox-1, a single thread it owns. It handles up to 20 rows, one transaction per row: claim 1 with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, send through the MessageGateway (SimulatedSmsGateway, or on the hosted demo RoutingSmsGateway, which sends numbers outside the demo block through AwsSmsGateway), then mark SENT or back off 2/4/8/16 s to FAILED, or mark FAILED at once on a refusal that sending again cannot cure; a cancellation is deleted instead once it is sent or has failed for good. A cancel deletes the appointment's confirmations, whatever their status, and then the appointment itself, in the same transaction that writes its cancellation SMS. The SPA polls GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference} until delivery settles, and InboxPage at /messages shows every message with a redacted recipient. Cross-module calls go only through application-level types: booking -> catalogue (BranchCalendar, ServiceOffer), booking -> scheduling (SlotCalendar), with scheduling's BookedIntervalsPort implemented by booking.infrastructure.JdbcBookedIntervals. notification -> booking: NotificationOutbox implements booking.application.ConfirmationOutbox, MessageQueries implements AppointmentNotifications, and MessageEraser implements NotificationEraser, through which the retention purge deletes an appointment's messages; NotificationDispatcher asks booking's AppointmentDirectory whether a confirmation's appointment is cancelled. docs/architecture.md lists every file on the booking path.

Modules and the dependency rule

Four rules, each an ArchUnit test in ArchitectureTest (cut from eight in v1; the delivery plan cuts anything beyond four). (1) modulesAreFreeOfCycles: slices().matching("za.co.fynbos.branchbook.(*)..").should().beFreeOfCycles(), with no exception: the demo loader arrived as booking.infrastructure.DemoDataLoader, inside the module it calls. The allowed arrows are scheduling -> catalogue; booking -> catalogue, scheduling; notification -> booking; all -> shared; no booking -> notification arrow. (2) domainHasNoSpringImports: noClasses().that().resideInAPackage("..domain..").should().dependOnClassesThat().resideInAPackage("org.springframework.."). It bans Spring only: no domain class imports an application, infrastructure or api type today, but no rule checks that. jakarta.persistence is allowed on the Appointment aggregate and jakarta.validation on the domain; this also keeps SlotCalendar, PublicHolidayCalendar, MobileNumber, AppointmentReference, NotificationTemplates and Gsm7 framework-free. (3) controllersNeverReachInfrastructure: no class in ..api.. depends on ..infrastructure.. (for response mapping), on ..shared.., Spring Web and Jakarta, never on any ..infrastructure... (4) onlyApplicationOpensTransactions: classes that depend on org.springframework.transaction.. (TransactionTemplate, @Transactional) must reside in ..application..; DispatcherLoop, IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJob, LookupAttemptPurgeJob, RetentionPurgeJob and DemoDataLoader (infrastructure) only trigger application code or a single auto-committed statement; NotificationDispatcher lives in application and opens one transaction per row itself. Cross-module access only through ..application.. (ports, handlers, records) or ..domain.. public types, with ..api.. and ..infrastructure.. module-private, is a documented convention, not a fifth rule. Port direction: booking owns ConfirmationOutbox, AppointmentNotifications and NotificationEraser in booking.application, so the arrow is notification -> booking only. Booking owns BookingLocks (the branch_services row lock and the mobile advisory lock are booking concerns, so catalogue stays read-only). Scheduling owns BookedIntervalsPort, implemented by booking.infrastructure.JdbcBookedIntervals, so scheduling never reads another module's table. Adapters convert SQLSTATE into domain exceptions, so no application class needs an infrastructure type.

Find the booking in five files

  1. AppointmentController
  2. BookAppointmentHandler
  3. Appointment / SlotCalendar
  4. AppointmentRepository
  5. NotificationOutbox

Key decisions

DecisionWhyRejected
Spring Boot 4.1.x with the Maven wrapper and Initializr defaults verbatim, on Java 25 LTS, confirmed by the day-0 spike and frozen.Java/Spring Boot and PostgreSQL are a conventional pairing for bank back ends, with first-class tools for everything this design leans on. 3.5.x left OSS support in June 2026, so it is not an option for new work. A day-0 decision removes the risk of a submission-day toolchain bump.Spring Boot 3.5.x, even as a fallback (end-of-support manifest; a downgrade also means Jackson 2, Hibernate 6 and renamed starters), a Java bump in M7 (red-build risk), Gradle Kotlin DSL (unfamiliar surface), a Node or .NET back end (documented as ports).
One jar serves the SPA from /static with an index.html fallback; compose is app + db only, both bound to 127.0.0.1.Fewest moving parts for a measured clean-clone start. One origin means CORS never exists, there is no nginx config to defend, and nothing is reachable from the LAN. Vite's dev proxy covers local work.nginx-unprivileged as a third service (extra config, CSP and proxy files, more build surface).
Two btree_gist EXCLUDE constraints (seat overlap and customer overlap over tstzrange '[)'), with a per-mobile advisory lock and a scoped branch_services row lock in front, then lowest-free-seat selection.The invariants must hold against any code path. Ranges, not start-time equality, are needed because customer overlap spans services with different durations, and because the production model of a branch-wide consultant pool puts services on unequal grids. With today's one-grid-per-service seats, overlap equals start equality, which is stated honestly. The locks make the loser's 409 deterministic with a fixed code order, and generate_series NOT EXISTS reuses holes left by cancellations.A partial unique index on starts_at (no answer for cross-service customer overlap or a shared consultant pool), a seat_no = count + 1 loop (false 409 after a cancellation gap), @Version only, SERIALIZABLE, application-level counts, global or Redis locks, and advisory locks keyed on branch (they couple unrelated bookings; keyed on the mobile they couple nothing).
The time-relative rule 'one upcoming active appointment per mobile per service' is enforced in the handler under pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(mobile, 0)), with DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT checked before CUSTOMER_OVERLAP and SLOT_FULL. The lock order is idempotency PK -> mobile -> branch_services, and 40P01/40001 get one re-run, then 503.A partial unique index cannot see time. Nothing moves past appointments out of CONFIRMED in v1, so ux_appointments_customer_service would lock a customer out of a service forever after one visit. The same-mobile lock also removes the exclusion-constraint deadlock and the index-order-dependent 409 code.ux_appointments_customer_service (permanent lockout; code depends on index order), a NO_SHOW/COMPLETED sweeper to make the index safe (a staff concern, out of scope), and a customers table with a row lock (POPIA minimality, extra entity).
The Idempotency-Key is stored in its own idempotency_keys table as the first statement of the booking transaction, using INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the stored key has expired, RETURNING. The row keeps request_hash, appointment_id and response_status, and a replay re-renders the current AppointmentResponse.Primary-key blocking serialises concurrent duplicates and the rollback frees the key. Re-rendering keeps names out of a jsonb copy and keeps JSON serialisation away from the locks. A cancel deletes the booking but keeps its key, with no appointment, for the rest of its 24 hours, so a retry is refused with 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED and never books it again; the kept key holds no name or number, only an unkeyed SHA-256 of the details, so it is pseudonymised, not anonymous. A key is honoured for exactly 24 hours, purged or not: the claim takes over an expired key, and the hourly purge only keeps the table small.A nullable unique column on appointments (index-order dependent), a key lookup after seat allocation (a same-key retry at the last seat returns 409), a plain JPA insert with catch (aborted transaction), and a stored response_body snapshot (stale state, duplicated personal data).
Every write reaches PostgreSQL at the line where it is written: AppointmentRepository.insert does persist + flush (a null @Version marks the row new) and translates SQLSTATEs into domain exceptions, the cancel changes the loaded Appointment in memory, then detaches it and locks and deletes its row in plain SQL, each with the version checked (V6), and application SQL binds :now from the Clock.JPA write-behind would push constraint violations to commit and break the JDBC step that references the new row. Detaching the entity before the plain-SQL lock and delete means JPA never writes the CANCELLED state the cancel then deletes, the version check makes the second of two concurrent cancels find the row gone and answer 404, counted as a wrong try, and one clock keeps MutableClock tests honest.Spring Data save() with assigned UUIDs (merge + extra SELECT), a native status-guarded UPDATE beside the JPA entity (stale persistence context), and SQL now() in business statements.
Outbox rows are written by a direct call through the booking-owned ConfirmationOutbox port, implemented by NotificationOutbox, inside the booking and cancel transactions. A cancel deletes the appointment's confirmations, whatever their status, before it deletes the appointment.Atomicity is the point, and it must be visible as one call in BookAppointmentHandler. The port direction keeps the module graph acyclic. An AFTER_COMMIT listener loses confirmations if the process dies between commit and send. Deleting the confirmations, after waiting for one being sent, prevents a backed-off 'confirmed' SMS from arriving after 'cancelled'.@TransactionalEventListener(AFTER_COMMIT), synchronous Spring events, sending on the request thread, a booking -> notification dependency (cycle), Kafka/RabbitMQ, and Mailpit SMTP.
NotificationDispatcher processes up to 20 rows per tick, one transaction per row (claim 1 with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, send, mark with an immediate UPDATE, commit), on outbox-1, a single thread owned by DispatcherLoop. Backoff is 2/4/8/16 s and a row goes FAILED on the fifth failed attempt, or at once on a refusal that sending again cannot cure; a cancellation is deleted where it would be marked SENT or FAILED.Claim, send and mark still share one transaction, which prevents double-sends and REQUIRES_NEW deadlocks. A database error or crash now re-sends at most one message instead of rolling back 20 successful sends into a resend loop. A mark failure is parked as FAILED (DISPATCH_ERROR, last_error 'MARK_FAILED'). One thread is enough for one fixed-delay loop; it is private to DispatcherLoop, not a scheduler bean, because a TaskScheduler bean would replace Spring Boot's default scheduler and move the key purge and the holiday check onto it. It gives isolation, not throughput.One transaction per batch with per-row try/catch (a database error aborts the whole batch), claim-then-process across transactions, a 2-thread pool (the second thread is idle), and WebSockets/SSE for status (2 s polling of one small resource is enough).
AppointmentStatus has five values (CONFIRMED, CHECKED_IN, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_SHOW) with guarded transitions and tests; only CONFIRMED -> CANCELLED is reachable through the API, and the cancel deletes the appointment in the same transaction (V6).An explicit state machine shows what check-in and no-show would do, even though the API reaches only cancel. The enum and tests cost 30 minutes, while staff endpoints would cost hours and an auth model. Per-customer rules filter on ends_at > :now, so unswept past rows are harmless.A two-state enum (too thin to show the lifecycle), REQUESTED/HELD states (booking is single-step and transactional), and staff/admin endpoints.
SMS is the only Tier 1 channel. EMAIL (the field, rows, template and SimulatedEmailGateway) ships together in Tier 3 or not at all, and a test double was the port's second implementation until real SMS added AwsSmsGateway, where a deployment asks.SMS is what SA retail-bank clients read. All three reviews cut email for budget, and collecting an address that nothing uses would breach POPIA s10. When email does ship, it is an additive optional field.Email in Tier 1 (budget), collecting email without sending it (minimality), an .ics attachment, and WhatsApp.
Customer fields live on the appointment; there is no customers table. Customer overlap is a database constraint, and the per-service rule runs in the handler under the per-mobile lock.POPIA minimality and no account linkage. The EXCLUDE constraint and the locked pre-check enforce 'one person, one place' without a customer entity. Because numbers are unverified, the README says these rules bound honest customers only, with OTP as the production answer.A customer aggregate with upsert-and-lock, and a 'max 3 active' application policy.
The availability endpoint returns one bounded day. Branch detail carries bookableFrom/bookableTo (the half-open [today, today + P30D), exactly 30 dates) and closedDates with reasons.Smallest response and simplest cache key. The date strip needs only reasons until a day is chosen, and a window supplied by the server means the device clock never shifts it.7- or 14-day range endpoints with per-day slot lists, and a window computed on the client.
No customer login. Access uses the reference plus X-Mobile-Last4, with 404-not-403, no reference in any problem body, two maskings (mobileMasked only to proven callers; redacted '082 *** ****' in the inbox; no recipient in logs) and a per-reference limiter (5 failures per hour) whose counts live in PostgreSQL, so every instance shares them.Proportionate to the brief and honest. Enumeration is infeasible (31^8), the second factor is rate-limited and is never printed beside the reference, and a typed number never yields a reference. The OIDC/OTP path is one ADR paragraph.JWT/OIDC/OTP login, existingReference in 409 bodies (disclosure to anyone who knows a number), a last-4 mask in the inbox and logs, a (reference, client IP) limiter key (a spoofable X-Forwarded-For multiplies the guess budget), and per-IP Bucket4j limits on booking (a gateway concern).
Fictional Fynbos Bank with its own palette and wordmark and an explicit non-affiliation line; the hosted demo runs at https://branchbook.click on AWS Cape Town under CDK, never on a free tier.Borrowing a real bank's brand colours or addresses would be impersonation. Free tiers sleep, and a fake bank collecting real numbers outside SA is the wrong first impression. A measured make up, PNGs and re-runnable scripts carry the demo.A real bank's name, logo, brand colours and e-mail addresses; Render/Neon hosting with a wake-up apology.
React 19. The committed OpenAPI contract confines a swap to another front end, such as Vue 3, to web/.React is the most widely used choice, and the generated client makes a swap a front-end-only change.Vue or Angular by default, and hand-written response types.
Packages by capability with one dependency graph and booking-owned ports. JPA annotations appear only on the Appointment aggregate. Four ArchUnit rules. BookAppointmentHandler.book fits one screen of ten numbered steps, and docs/architecture.md lists every file on the path.A reviewer can find and read the booking quickly without a false file-count claim. A pure-domain mapping layer would cost 400 lines for no reviewer value, and four rules are cheaper to explain than Modulith or an eight-rule list with exceptions.Spring Modulith, separate persistence models, a flat single package, a booking -> notification arrow, and a hard 60-line gate that pushes steps into helpers.
Tiered scope (must/should/could) with a tracer bullet by hour 19, a Tier-2 gate at hour 25 (M5 green), a reserved M7, a README that grows each milestone, and no new feature after hour 26 (9 h before the end).UI and docs starvation is the most likely schedule failure. Tiers and gates protect the race test, the bypass test, outbox atomicity, the UI slice, make up and the README walkthrough.Cutting by feel on the last day, a backend-only checkpoint, and docs written only in M7.
