Actors in the system context
ActorKindInteraction
CustomerpersonBooks, views and cancels appointments through the BranchBook SPA on a phone or laptop; sees the SMS's delivery on the confirmation page. Locally the SMS is simulated and the inbox page lists it; on the hosted demo a number outside the demo block receives a real SMS.
Reviewer / operatorpersonRuns make up or opens the hosted demo at https://branchbook.click, follows the 90-second walkthrough with the demo numbers, reproduces the 409 in two tabs at Review with different mobiles, watches a 0000 recipient reach FAILED on the confirmation page and, under make up, in the inbox, reads /actuator/health and docs/runbook.md.
BranchBook SPAcontainerReact 19 app in the browser. Calls the BranchBook API over /api/v1 with Idempotency-Key and X-Mobile-Last4, reads back the X-Correlation-Id the server assigns, polls delivery status every 2 s while PENDING, and renders only the server-supplied bookable window.
BranchBook APIcontainerSpring Boot 4.1 jar on Java 25. Serves the SPA, exposes REST and actuator, owns the booking transaction, and hosts the outbox dispatcher, the SMS gateways and the scheduled jobs.
PostgreSQL 17containerOwns the ten tables, the EXCLUDE constraints, idempotency_keys, the notification_messages outbox, the daily cap's ledger and the lookup limit's counts. It is the arbiter of double booking and the holder of the per-mobile advisory and row locks.
NotificationDispatchercomponentRuns inside the API every 2 s, up to 20 rows a tick, driven by DispatcherLoop on outbox-1, a single thread of its own; branchbook.dispatcher.enabled switches the loop off. Each row gets one transaction: claim with SKIP LOCKED, supersede a confirmation whose appointment is still kept as cancelled instead of sending it (M6; since V6 only one cancelled before then, or by hand), otherwise send, then mark SENT or back off to FAILED, or FAILED at once on a refusal that cannot succeed; a cancellation is deleted where it would be marked SENT or FAILED. A mark failure becomes FAILED (failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR, last_error 'MARK_FAILED').
SimulatedSmsGatewaycomponentMessageGateway adapter: 150 ms latency (branchbook.dispatcher.simulated-latency), a fake provider_message_id 'sim-<uuid>', deterministic failure (SIMULATED_FAILURE) for recipients ending 0000, such as 082 555 0000. It logs nothing; the dispatcher logs the outcome by messageId, never the recipient or the reference.
SimulatedEmailGatewaycomponentTier 3 only (FR-37): MessageGateway adapter for EMAIL rows, which exist only when the email field ships; fails for @fail.example.
Simulated inboxcomponentGET /api/v1/messages (only when branchbook.inbox.enabled is true, as in compose, make dev-api and the tests, but not the hosted demo; otherwise a plain 404) behind InboxPage at /messages. Recipients are redacted ('082 *** ****') and a banner reads 'Demo operator view. It lists every booking's messages, recipients redacted, and is switched off in production. Here every message is simulated, as on any local run.'. Where branchbook.inbox.redact-references is on, references read APT-****-****. POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry (M6) sits behind the same switch and the Retry button; where the inbox is off, the guarded runbook SQL replays a message.
AWS End User Messaging SMSexternalBuilt after M6, used only where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, as on the hosted demo: AwsSmsGateway, a second MessageGateway implementation selected by configuration, with credentials from the ECS task role. SendTextMessage from af-south-1, through a protect configuration that allows South Africa only, at most five SMS a day to one number; AWS's MessageId is the provider_message_id, and no delivery events are logged, because they carry the number. While the AWS account is in the SMS sandbox, an unverified number ends FAILED on its first attempt, or, for a cancellation, is deleted. OTP and an email provider are not built.
Kafka / Amazon MSKexternalNot built. The dispatcher's per-row claim loop becomes the producer when a second consumer appears (ADR-0003).
Prometheus / Grafana / InstanaexternalOptional scrape of /actuator/prometheus (Boot defaults; appointments_booked_total and outbox_pending from M6); nothing else is exposed, and the hosted demo serves only health and info.
GitHub ActionsexternalRuns the api, web, stack and infra jobs of ci.yml on every push to main and every PR. stack brings up compose with --wait, runs smoke.sh, the Playwright tests at 390 and 1280 px, smoke.sh again and demo-409.sh. On main, once all four pass, the deploy job of ci.yml signs in through OpenID Connect on an ARM runner, deploys BranchbookData and BranchbookApp, smoke-tests https://branchbook.click, then deploys the BranchbookRelease record and reads the commit back; deploy.yml and infra.yml are gone. The administrator deploys BranchbookAccount by hand.

DesignBuilt

The booking transaction

Status. Built in M3: the ten steps in BookAppointmentHandler, with the advisory and row locks, the lowest free seat, idempotency, the one re-run and the alternatives, on top of the exclusion constraints from M1, proven under contention from M5. Built in M4: the five steps in the web app at /book. Built in M6: a cancel frees the seat for the next booking, and BookingOutcomeMeter counts every booking request by outcome. Built since M6: a cancel deletes the booking, so a retry of its Idempotency-Key is refused with 422 (V6); wrong lookups are counted in PostgreSQL, so every instance shares the limit (V8); and, where a deployment switches them on, the per-recipient SMS cap is counted in its ledger in the booking transaction (V7) and ended appointments are deleted hourly.

This is the heart of the submission. One handler method, one transaction, a fixed lock order, and constraints that make the wrong outcome impossible even if the code is wrong.

The race for the last seat

Why the database decides

  • The database decides seats.
  • V1 runs CREATE EXTENSION btree_gist, and V2__schema.sql adds on appointments: CONSTRAINT appointments_seat_no_overlap EXCLUDE USING gist (branch_id WITH =, service_id WITH =, seat_no WITH =, tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') WITH &&) WHERE (status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN'));
  • CONSTRAINT appointments_customer_no_overlap EXCLUDE USING gist (customer_mobile WITH =, tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') WITH &&) WHERE (same predicate);
  • UNIQUE reference;
  • FOREIGN KEY (branch_id, service_id) REFERENCES branch_services. v1's ux_appointments_customer_service UNIQUE (customer_mobile, service_id) WHERE active is removed: it had no time bound and nothing closes a past CONFIRMED row in v1, so it locked a customer out of a service forever after one visit, and a partial index cannot say 'upcoming' (predicates must be immutable).
  • That rule is now checked in the handler under pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)) (steps 4-5) against ix_appointments_customer_active with ends_at > :now; the overlap rule is pre-checked under the same lock and keeps its EXCLUDE backstop.
  • The handler makes seats deterministic: SELECT ...
  • FROM branch_services ...
  • FOR UPDATE, then lowest free seat via generate_series(1, capacity) NOT EXISTS overlapping active row; none -> 409 SLOT_FULL with up to three alternatives.
  • No rule is ever read-then-write without the lock that serialises its writers; never count+1.
  • ConstraintViolationTranslator maps the constraint name from ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() (SQLSTATE 23505/23P01/23503) to codes (SchemaConstraintIT rejects a raw insert for each named constraint; ConstraintNamesIT checks that every mapped name exists, and that a violation's name is still read with the server detail off, and BookAppointmentIT.constraintViolationIsRaisedAtInsertNotAtCommit that the backstops surface at the insert as SLOT_FULL and CUSTOMER_OVERLAP): appointments_seat_no_overlap (23P01) -> 409 SLOT_FULL (alternatives[] from AlternativeSlots, after the rollback; should never fire while the row lock is held, proven real by SchemaConstraintIT); appointments_customer_no_overlap (23P01) -> 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP (should never fire while the mobile lock is held); appointments_reference_key (23505) -> re-run the whole booking transaction once with a fresh reference, a second collision -> 500 INTERNAL_ERROR; appointments_branch_service_fkey (23503) -> 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH (unreachable after step 2, mapped anyway); idempotency_keys_pkey never raises (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE); 40P01 deadlock_detected and 40001 serialization_failure -> re-run once, then 503 TRY_AGAIN (unreachable through the fixed lock order, mapped for any bypassing path); 55P03 lock_not_available (lock_timeout) and 57014 query_canceled (statement_timeout) -> 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2, which the translator leaves to ProblemDetailHandler; 23514 check_violation -> 500 INTERNAL_ERROR, logged by ProblemDetailHandler with its exception types and stack frames but no message (unreachable for parsed input: value objects share the CHECK patterns); any other SQLException -> 500 INTERNAL_ERROR carrying only correlationId.
  • No 409 body carries a reference, branch or time (v1's existingReference is removed: it let anyone who knew a cellphone number obtain that customer's reference and, with the last four digits they already had, view and cancel the appointment).
  • Proof: ConcurrentBookingIT.twentyRacersForOneSeatProduceExactlyOne201 (POL capacity 1, 20 distinct mobiles: 1 x 201, 19 x 409 SLOT_FULL, zero 503, one appointment row, one PENDING SMS row), ConcurrentBookingIT.twentyRacersForThreeSeatsProduceExactlyThree201s (SAN FICA, seats {1,2,3}), ConcurrentBookingIT.sameMobileTwoBranchesOverlappingConcurrentlyGivesOne201AndOne409CustomerOverlap (different services, latch-released), SchemaConstraintIT.overlappingSeatIsRejectedEvenWithoutTheApplication (raw JDBC, no lock), SchemaConstraintIT.activeStatusesMatchSchemaPredicate (pg_constraint and pg_index.indpred), BookAppointmentIT.pastConfirmedAppointmentDoesNotBlockSameServiceBooking, CancelAppointmentIT.cancelFreesSeatForNewBooking (hole reused by generate_series).

The constraints, from V2__schema.sql

CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS btree_gist;
-- The invariant of the whole system. '[)' ranges let 09:00-09:30 and 09:30-10:00 coexist; the partial predicate releases seats of CANCELLED/COMPLETED/NO_SHOW rows without a delete. Why EXCLUDE and not UNIQUE (branch_id, service_id, seat_no, starts_at): (1) the customer rule must compare ranges across services of different durations, which only an overlap operator can do; (2) the production move to a branch-wide consultant pool (EXCLUDE (branch_id WITH =, seat_no WITH =, range WITH &&), lock on the branches row) makes unequal grids share seats; (3) a per-service unique index is correct only while every slot sits on one grid, which no constraint enforces and a later change to a branch's opening hours would silently break.
ALTER TABLE appointments ADD CONSTRAINT appointments_seat_no_overlap EXCLUDE USING gist (branch_id WITH =, service_id WITH =, seat_no WITH =, tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') WITH &&) WHERE (status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN'));
ALTER TABLE appointments ADD CONSTRAINT appointments_customer_no_overlap EXCLUDE USING gist (customer_mobile WITH =, tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') WITH &&) WHERE (status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN'));
-- One upcoming appointment per mobile per service is time-relative; index predicates must be immutable and now() is not, so the rule is checked under pg_advisory_xact_lock and this index only makes the check a lookup.
CREATE INDEX ix_appointments_customer_active ON appointments (customer_mobile, service_id) WHERE status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN');
CONSTRAINT appointments_branch_service_fkey FOREIGN KEY (branch_id, service_id) REFERENCES branch_services (branch_id, service_id);
CREATE INDEX ix_appointments_slot_lookup ON appointments (branch_id, service_id, starts_at) WHERE status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN');
Steps 4 and 5, run before the branch_services lock: SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)); then SELECT 1 FROM appointments WHERE customer_mobile = :m AND service_id = :s AND status IN ACTIVE_SQL AND ends_at > :now LIMIT 1 (hit -> 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT); then SELECT 1 FROM appointments WHERE customer_mobile = :m AND status IN ACTIVE_SQL AND tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') && tstzrange(:startsAt, :endsAt, '[)') LIMIT 1 (hit -> 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP). Each statement takes a fresh READ COMMITTED snapshot after the lock wait, so it sees every booking by that mobile that committed before the lock was granted.

Lowest free seat, run under the row lock

SELECT s FROM generate_series(1, :capacity) s
WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM appointments a
WHERE a.branch_id = :branchId
  AND a.service_id = :serviceId
  AND a.seat_no = s
  AND a.status IN ACTIVE_SQL
  AND tstzrange(a.starts_at, a.ends_at, '[)') && tstzrange(:startsAt, :endsAt, '[)'))
ORDER BY s LIMIT 1

Idempotency

  • Header Idempotency-Key: a UUID, required on POST /api/v1/appointments only (missing or not a UUID gives 400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED).
  • The scope is global per key (table idempotency_keys, key uuid PK); one key represents one booking-attempt payload. request_hash is the SHA-256 of the parsed details, one per line: branch code, service code, startsAt (UTC instant), mobileE164, firstName and lastName (BookAppointmentCommand.requestHash).
  • Names are NFC-normalised, curly apostrophes are mapped to ASCII and whitespace is trimmed, so a retry from an iPhone hashes like the original.
  • Mechanism, as the FIRST statement of the booking transaction: INSERT INTO idempotency_keys (key, request_hash, created_at, expires_at) VALUES (:key, :hash, :now, :now + interval '24 hours') ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET request_hash = EXCLUDED.request_hash, appointment_id = NULL, response_status = NULL, created_at = EXCLUDED.created_at, expires_at = EXCLUDED.expires_at WHERE idempotency_keys.expires_at <= EXCLUDED.created_at RETURNING key Here :now is bound from the Clock.
  • Outcomes of that statement: - No conflict, or a row whose 24 hours have run out: it returns the key and the request books as new.
  • A key is honoured for exactly 24 h, whether or not the hourly purge has removed it: an expired row is taken over by the same statement, so the same key books as new (IdempotencyKeyPurgeJobIT.keyIsHonouredForTwentyFourHoursThenBooksAnewEvenBeforeThePurge). - A conflicting row still in force: it returns nothing (its WHERE is false, but DO UPDATE still locks the row, so the purge cannot remove it before the read below).
  • If the row is still uncommitted, the INSERT waits for its transaction.
  • If that transaction rolls back, the INSERT proceeds and books.
  • If it commits, the INSERT returns nothing, and the follow-up SELECT request_hash, appointment_id FROM idempotency_keys WHERE key = :key runs in a fresh READ COMMITTED snapshot and sees the committed row.
  • Replay: - Same key and same hash: 201 with the original Location and header Idempotent-Replayed: true.
  • The body is re-rendered from appointment_id by the same AppointmentViews and AppointmentResponse.from that serve GET, so it shows the current state.
  • Once a cancel has deleted that booking, the key stays with response_status 201 and no appointment until its 24 hours run out, and the same key and hash is 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED with a fixed detail saying the booking has been cancelled and a new key is needed, so a retry never books it again (IdempotencyIT.sameKeyAfterItsBookingIsCancelledReturns422AndNeverBooksAgain). A replay that finds its appointment deleted between its commit and its read is answered the same.
  • No new row and no new message are written (IdempotencyIT.sameKeySamePayloadReplaysOriginal201). - Same key and different hash: 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED. - No response body is stored, so the table holds no names or numbers; request_hash is an unkeyed SHA-256 of them, so a guess of the details can be confirmed against it: the row is pseudonymised, not anonymous, and a key kept after a cancel holds it for the rest of its 24 hours.
  • Step 10 records only appointment_id and response_status = 201.
  • Any non-201 outcome inside the transaction (404, 409, 422, 503, 500) rolls the key row back, so a retry with the same key attempts again (IdempotencyIT.failedBookingLeavesNoKeyRow).
  • Concurrent identical requests produce one booking, one SMS and two identical 201s (IdempotencyIT.concurrentSameKey_bookOnceAndBothReceiveSameReference).
  • The TTL is 24 h; expired rows are purged hourly by @Scheduled DELETE ...
  • WHERE expires_at <= :now (IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob; IdempotencyKeyPurgeJobIT.purgeRemovesOnlyKeysThatHaveExpired). The purge only keeps the table small: the claim already treats an expired key as new.
  • Client: useIdempotencyKey mints one crypto.randomUUID() per payload hash and persists {payloadHash, key} with the details draft in sessionStorage, so a refresh after a timed-out POST reuses the key.
  • It retries a network failure, or a 503 once after Retry-After, with the same key; rotates the key when the slot or any field changes; and clears it with the draft on the 201.
  • Lookup and retry are naturally idempotent (a repeat retry on a non-FAILED or non-retryable row is a 200 no-op). A cancel deletes the booking, so a repeat cancel finds nothing to change: it is 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND and counts as a wrong try.

Concurrency strategy

BookAppointmentHandler.handle, one TransactionTemplate at READ COMMITTED, under 60 lines with one named call per step, in this order: (1) INSERT INTO idempotency_keys (key, request_hash, created_at, expires_at) VALUES (..., :now, :now + 24h) ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the existing row has expired RETURNING key; nothing returned -> same hash replays 201 re-rendered from the stored appointment_id with Idempotent-Replayed: true, or is 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED once a cancel has deleted that booking, different hash -> 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED; (2) load active branch and its branch_services row (absent -> 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH); (3) SlotCalendar.validate(startsAt, clock) -> 422 codes in fixed precedence, ends_at computed; (4) pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)); (5) the DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT pre-check (same mobile and service, active, ends_at > :now) and the CUSTOMER_OVERLAP pre-check (same mobile, active, overlapping range) -> 409; (6) SELECT ... FROM branch_services WHERE branch_id=? AND service_id=? FOR UPDATE; (7) lowest free seat or 409 SLOT_FULL; (8) EntityManager.persist(appointment) (reference, ends_at computed server-side, status CONFIRMED, created_at/updated_at from the Clock) and flush(), so 23P01/23505 surface at this line inside the translator's scope; (9) ConfirmationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation(Confirmation) by direct call, booking's port that NotificationOutbox implements with Propagation.MANDATORY (JdbcClient INSERTs, which now see the flushed row through the FK); (10) UPDATE idempotency_keys SET appointment_id, response_status = 201; commit. Persistence rule: Appointment is the only JPA entity (Integer @Version null until persist, so Spring Data never merges an application-assigned UUID); everything else in the transaction is JdbcClient and never relies on Hibernate auto-flush; the single flush at step 8 orders the two worlds. Lock order is fixed (idempotency key row, mobile advisory lock, branch_services row) and rows for a mobile are written only while its lock is held, so no lock cycle exists between booking transactions: a same-mobile double submit at two branches waits on the advisory lock and ends as one 201 and one 409, never as the 40P01 that v1's exclusion-check-only design produced; 40P01/40001 from any bypassing path re-run once, then 503. Taking the mobile lock before the hot branch_services lock keeps the hot lock out of any wait on a narrow, rarely contended one. A reference collision re-runs once; HikariCP options -c lock_timeout=2000 -c statement_timeout=5000 and connectionTimeout 3 s map to 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After. The tests use no Spring profile, so they run with the same 2 s lock_timeout (ApplicationStartsIT.databaseSessionsGiveUpOnSlowLocksAndStatements); ConcurrentBookingIT (M5) books one warm-up slot before the latch, so 19 queued losers on a cold CI JVM are not slowed by first-use work; ProblemDetailHandlerTest.transientDatabaseFailureBecomes503WithRetryAfterTwo pins the 503 and its Retry-After: 2 for 55P03, 57014, 40P01 and 40001. Idempotency: the key row is inserted as the first statement, so two concurrent identical requests block in the INSERT ... ON CONFLICT on the primary key; one books, the other wakes, reads the committed row with a fresh statement and replays the same reference; a 409/422 rolls the key back so a retry attempts again; a key expires 24 h after its claim, from when the same INSERT takes it over, and IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob deletes expired rows hourly (DELETE ... WHERE expires_at <= :now); the UI mints one UUID per unchanged payload (useIdempotencyKey) and reuses it on network retry. Seat races: the branch_services row lock scopes contention to exactly one (branch, service) pair, bookings elsewhere never wait, and the loser computes its own 409 under the lock without touching the constraint; the EXCLUDE constraints are the backstop for any path that bypasses the lock. Customer races: the per-mobile advisory lock serialises bookings by one number (64-bit hash; a collision only adds a wait, never a wrong answer) and makes the 409 precedence DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT, CUSTOMER_OVERLAP, SLOT_FULL deterministic; no customers table. Cancel races: CancelAppointmentHandler counts the try with the limiter, then, in one READ COMMITTED transaction, loads the Appointment with JPA and lets Appointment.cancel(now) decide in memory; JpaAppointmentRepository.lockForDeletion detaches it and runs SELECT id FROM appointments WHERE id = ? AND version = ? FOR UPDATE; NotificationOutbox.enqueueCancellation deletes every CONFIRMATION of the appointment, whatever its status, and inserts the CANCELLATION through JdbcClient; the response is rendered; and DELETE FROM appointments WHERE id = ? AND version = ? removes the row, while the foreign keys set the cancellation's and the idempotency key's appointment_id to null (V6). A concurrent second cancel waits for the first's lock, finds the row gone (OptimisticLockingFailureException) and answers 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, counted as a wrong try; so does a cancel whose appointment the retention purge deleted after its digits matched. Cancel vs booking never conflict because cancel releases a seat only. Retention: where branchbook.retention.enabled is on, as on the hosted demo, RetentionPurgeJob runs RetentionPurge every hour from a minute after start-up, and it deletes ended appointments in batches of up to 500, one transaction each: it locks them with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, so a row another transaction holds waits for the next run, then deletes their messages, their idempotency keys and the rows. A cancel that loses its appointment to the purge answers 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, never a 500. Outbox: NotificationDispatcher.runOnce, run every 2 s by DispatcherLoop on its own single thread outbox-1, processes up to 20 rows per tick, each in its own transaction: SELECT ... WHERE status='PENDING' AND next_attempt_at <= :now ORDER BY next_attempt_at, id LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED; MessageGateway.send; explicit UPDATE to SENT (sent_at, provider_message_id) or attempts+1 with backoff 2/4/8/16 s, FAILED on the fifth failure or at once on a refusal that sending again cannot cure, and a cancellation deleted where it would be marked SENT or FAILED; commit. Claim, send and mark share that row's transaction, so two dispatcher instances never double-send (NotificationDispatcherIT.twoDispatchersNeverSendTheSameMessageTwice), a crash re-sends at most the one message in flight, and a database error on one row can no longer roll back and re-send nineteen others (a failed mark becomes FAILED with failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR and last_error 'MARK_FAILED' in a separate transaction). The cancel's DELETE of the confirmations waits at most one in-flight send for its row lock, so a confirmation is delivered before its cancellation or never; the dispatcher locks only notification rows, never appointment rows, so cancel and dispatch cannot deadlock. Before it sends a confirmation, it asks booking, by a plain read, whether the appointment is cancelled, and if so marks the message FAILED / SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION instead; since V6 only an appointment cancelled before then, or by hand, can be found so. A retry from the inbox locks its message with FOR UPDATE, waiting at most the 2 s lock_timeout for a send in flight, so it never races the dispatcher or a cancel. Reads (availability, lookup, inbox) run unlocked under READ COMMITTED and never write. Lookup brute force: LookupAttemptLimiter (per normalised reference, 5 failures per hour) keeps its counts in PostgreSQL, in lookup_attempts (V8), through the LookupAttemptStore port: JdbcLookupAttemptStore locks the reference's row with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE in a short REQUIRES_NEW transaction of its own, before and after the lookup's or the cancel's transaction, so lookups on any instance are counted one at a time and a restart forgets nothing; a lookup in flight holds its place on a 30-second lease at most, and LookupAttemptPurgeJob deletes closed windows hourly. A per-address limit on lookups at the edge is still open. Isolation stays READ COMMITTED everywhere; no SERIALIZABLE, no Redis or global locks, no application-level count decides a seat (the per-recipient SMS cap counts a number's rows of the last 24 hours in its ledger, sms_recipient_sends; bookings by one mobile count under its advisory lock, and cancels at the same moment can pass the cap by a message or two); the one advisory lock is keyed by a single mobile and couples nothing else.

Every step of the booking flow, including failure branches
#ActorActionNotes
1ClientOpens /book and chooses a branch on BranchStep.The SPA had loaded GET /api/v1/branches (staleTime 5 min); the URL becomes /book?branch=SAN.
2SPAGET /api/v1/branches/SAN; renders ServiceStep (duration, documents) and a DateStrip spanning exactly bookableFrom..bookableTo, with closed days from closedDates.Closed days are disabled with a reason ('Public holiday: Local Government Elections', 'Closed on Sundays'). The device date and time zone are never used for the window. 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND gives an error state with 'choose another branch'.
3ClientPicks a service and a date.The URL becomes /book?branch=SAN&service=FICA&date=2026-10-27, so refresh and back work.
4SPAGET /api/v1/branches/SAN/availability?service=FICA&date=2026-10-27; renders TimeGrid.seatsAvailable 0 shows 'Full' (disabled); 1 shows '1 left'. No refetch on window focus; after ten minutes without activity a banner offers Refresh, and generatedAt is shown as 'Times as of HH:MM'. 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH or DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW shows an inline alert (should be unreachable from the UI).
5ClientSelects a slot, fills DetailsStep (first name, last name, mobile, plus an optional email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships) under the inline POPIA notice, reviews on ReviewStep, and taps Confirm.No consent checkbox (POPIA s11(1)(b)). zod mirrors the server rules; the mobile pre-check is ^(\+27|0027|27|0)(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$ after stripping formatting. ReviewStep does not re-check availability, so a slot taken meanwhile is reported by the server's 409 rather than guessed client-side.
6SPAuseIdempotencyKey mints one UUID per payload hash and persists {payloadHash, key} with the draft in sessionStorage. POST /api/v1/appointments with Idempotency-Key and the body (the server assigns the correlation id); Confirm is disabled while the request is in flight.A network failure, a timeout or a refresh after a timed-out POST resends with the SAME key; a 503 is retried once after Retry-After with the same key. A payload edit or slot change rotates the key.
7APICorrelationIdFilter puts the id in MDC; AppointmentController validates the header and body (Bean Validation, strict JSON, @IsoOffsetDateTime on startsAt).Failure branches: 400 VALIDATION_FAILED with errors[], which the SPA maps onto fields by name; 400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED, which the SPA never causes and shows as a general error.
8DomainNames are NFC-normalised and apostrophes mapped; MobileNumber.parse (libphonenumber ZA, type MOBILE) produces E.164; the canonical request_hash is computed.Failure branch: 422 MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED with errors[{field:'customer.mobile'}], shown inline as 'We need a cellphone number to SMS your confirmation.'. Only where branchbook.booking.demo-numbers-only is on (off by default, and off on the hosted demo since it sends real SMS): 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED for a South African mobile outside 082 555 0000 to 0199, before anything is written.
9DBBookAppointmentHandler opens one TransactionTemplate (READ COMMITTED). Handler step 1: INSERT INTO idempotency_keys ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the existing row has expired RETURNING key.If nothing is returned, the handler reads the row in a fresh snapshot. Same hash: 201 replayed with Idempotent-Replayed: true and the body rendered from the current appointment (the SPA treats it as success, step 19). Different hash: 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED, and so is the same hash once a cancel has deleted the booking, which is never booked again. A concurrent request with the same key waits inside its INSERT until this transaction commits (it then replays) or rolls back (it then books). A key is honoured for exactly 24 h; after that the same statement takes it over, whether or not the hourly purge has removed it.
10DBHandler step 2: load the active branch and its branch_services row for the service.Failure branches: 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND; no branch_services row gives 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH. Rollback removes the idempotency row.
11DomainHandler step 3: SlotCalendar.validate(startsAt, clock) in branch.timezone; ends_at = starts_at + duration, computed server-side.Failure branch, precedence DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW > SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY > SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS > SLOT_OFF_GRID > SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME: 422 with errors[{field:'startsAt'}]. The SPA refetches availability and asks for another time, keeping the details.
12DBHandler step 4: SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)). Handler step 5, in order: SELECT 1 FROM appointments WHERE customer_mobile=:m AND service_id=:s AND status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN') AND ends_at > :now; and SELECT 1 FROM appointments WHERE customer_mobile=:m AND status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN') AND tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') && tstzrange(:startsAt, :endsAt, '[)').Same-mobile bookings serialise here, before the hot branch_services lock, so the pre-checks are race-free, past appointments never block a new booking, and two branches cannot deadlock on the exclusion constraint. Failure branches: 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT, then 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP. Neither body carries a reference or time; the SPA shows the code's copy from docs/errors.md ('You already have an upcoming appointment for this service.' or 'You already have an appointment at that time.') and links /manage.
13DBHandler step 6: SELECT ... FROM branch_services WHERE branch_id=? AND service_id=? FOR UPDATE.Serialises seat allocation for this branch and service only. Lock order (idempotency PK, mobile, branch_services) is fixed in every transaction. lock_timeout 2 s gives 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2; the SPA waits for Retry-After (at most 5 s) and resubmits once with the same key, while Confirm reads 'Confirming…'. Under a flood on one branch-service, the HikariCP pool (10) is the bulkhead and the excess fails fast with 503.
14DBHandler step 7, lowest free seat: SELECT s FROM generate_series(1, capacity) s WHERE NOT EXISTS (active appointment on seat s overlapping tstzrange(:startsAt, :endsAt, '[)')) ORDER BY s LIMIT 1.Failure branch (409, slot taken): if no seat is free, the transaction rolls back and alternatives[] (<= 3 later free slots that day from AlternativeSlots) are computed after the rollback, outside the lock. The SPA stays on the review step with 'That time was just taken', the alternatives as buttons and a link to all times (TimeGrid, with refetched availability), keeps the customer's details and mints a fresh key when a new slot is chosen. Holes left by cancellations are reused.
15DBHandler step 8: draw the reference (SecureReferenceGenerator, AppointmentReference.generate), so a re-run gets a fresh one; INSERT INTO appointments (id, reference, branch_id, service_id, seat_no, starts_at, ends_at, status='CONFIRMED', customer_*) via EntityManager.persist, flushed immediately.The flush makes backstop violations surface at this line, inside the translator. Mapping (ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and SQLSTATE): appointments_seat_no_overlap -> 409 SLOT_FULL; appointments_customer_no_overlap -> 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP; appointments_reference_key -> whole transaction re-run once; 40P01/40001 -> re-run once, then 503. Every non-201 outcome rolls back, including the idempotency row.
16DomainHandler step 9: ConfirmationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation(Confirmation.of(appointment, branch, service)) by direct call, implemented by NotificationOutbox. The Confirmation carries the appointment id, reference, recipient, start, zone and the branch's and service's SMS names, so notification never reads the catalogue. NotificationTemplates render the SMS (and, only with the Tier 3 email channel, the email when an address was given) in branch.timezone with Locale.ENGLISH; the per-recipient daily cap applies where it is switched on.Never a Spring event or an AFTER_COMMIT listener; the call is visible in BookAppointmentHandler. Where branchbook.sms.recipient-daily-cap is above 0 (5 on the hosted demo, 0 and so off by default), the outbox counts the number's rows in sms_recipient_sends since :now - 24 h, by its keyed fingerprint (HMAC-SHA256 under branchbook.sms.recipient-cap-key), and below the cap adds one row in the same transaction. At or above the cap, the row is inserted FAILED / RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP, adds no ledger row and is never sent, and the booking still commits. The demo block, 082 555 0000 to 0199, is never capped.
17DBINSERT notification_messages rows (SMS always; EMAIL only with the Tier 3 email channel) with status PENDING, attempts 0, created_at = next_attempt_at = :now and correlation_id (JdbcClient INSERTs, which see the flushed appointment). Handler step 10: UPDATE idempotency_keys SET appointment_id, response_status=201 (JdbcClient). COMMIT.The appointment and its confirmation commit or roll back together (BookAppointmentIT.failureAfterTheSmsIsWrittenRollsTheSmsBackWithTheBooking from M3; ConfirmationOutboxIT.bookingAndPendingMessagesCommitAtomically in M5). No response body is serialised under the lock. Any failure here returns 500 INTERNAL_ERROR with correlationId and persists nothing.
18API201 Created, Location: /api/v1/appointments/APT-7K3M-9Q2X, and an AppointmentResponse built by AppointmentViews and AppointmentResponse.from after the commit, with notifications[] PENDING. Metric appointments_booked_total{outcome=confirmed}, counted by BookingOutcomeMeter once the response is written; one INFO line (booking.confirmed: appointmentId, branch, service and seat, prefixed with the correlationId; never the reference).No name, mobile digits or reference in the application's log or PostgreSQL's (LogRedactionIT), and no constraint DETAIL either (logServerErrorDetail=false).
19SPANavigates to /appointments/APT-7K3M-9Q2X; stores {reference, last4} in sessionStorage; clears the draft and its key; moves focus to the h1 'Your appointment is confirmed'; renders ConfirmationPage and starts polling the delivery status.Hand-off to the confirmation flow. Idempotent-Replayed: true is treated exactly like a fresh 201.

DesignBuilt

Simulated confirmation

Status. Built in M3 to M5: the template and its one-SMS check, the PENDING row written in the booking transaction, the confirmation page and the simulated inbox, the dispatcher and the simulated gateway with retries and a final FAILED. Built in M6: the cancellation SMS, superseding a confirmation by a cancellation, and Retry in the inbox. Built since: real SMS through AWS End User Messaging where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, as on the hosted demo, with the demo block 082 555 0000 to 0199 always simulated, and the per-recipient daily cap, counted from V7 in a ledger of keyed fingerprints; from V6, a cancel that deletes its booking's confirmations with the booking, and a cancellation SMS deleted once it is final; and a refusal that sending again cannot cure, which fails the message at once. Not built: the email channel and the calendar invite.

'Communicates a simulated confirmation' is read as four obligations, each one checkable. (1) Compose the real customer-facing messages a bank would send (SMS always; email only if the Tier 3 channel ships and an address was given), covering what, where, when and what to bring, plus a reference. (2) Record them durably, so a confirmation can neither be lost after the booking commits nor exist for a booking that rolled back. (3) Deliver them through a channel abstraction whose last hop is simulated by default, or a real SMS where a deployment asks, with retries and a visible, pollable delivery status. (4) Never contradict the customer: a cancel deletes its booking's confirmations, so a confirmation is delivered before its cancellation or never, and a message is never sent twice in a loop. A console line, a send on the request thread or an in-memory list satisfies none of these. The domain never knows which gateway sends; only the adapter changes.

Channels

ChannelHow it is simulatedWhere it shows
SMS (primary, always queued; recipient = customer_mobile E.164; no sender id: South Africa supports none, so AWS sends a real SMS over a shared route, and the web app labels the message BRANCHBOOK)SimulatedSmsGateway implements MessageGateway. It waits 150 ms (branchbook.dispatcher.simulated-latency) and returns Accepted('sim-' + UUID). It logs nothing; the dispatcher logs each outcome by messageId, never the recipient or the reference. It returns Refused(SIMULATED_FAILURE) for recipients ending 0000, so retries and FAILED can be demonstrated. It is the default MessageGateway, and the one docker compose, make dev-api and the tests use. Where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, as on the hosted demo, RoutingSmsGateway keeps the published demo block, 082 555 0000 to 0199, on SimulatedSmsGateway, because anyone may type those numbers, and sends every other number a real SMS through AwsSmsGateway: AWS End User Messaging SMS (SendTextMessage, TRANSACTIONAL, a max price of US$0.20 a message) in af-south-1, with credentials from the ECS task role, so there are no secrets. While the AWS account is in the SMS sandbox, AWS refuses every number that is not verified, a refusal sending again cannot cure, so such a message ends FAILED on its first attempt and the booking stands. Per-recipient daily cap: branchbook.sms.recipient-daily-cap, 5 per rolling 24 h on the hosted demo, off by default, and never for the demo block, counted in the ledger sms_recipient_sends by an HMAC-SHA256 fingerprint of the number under branchbook.sms.recipient-cap-key, which the hosted demo takes from Secrets Manager.The delivery line on ConfirmationPage ('To 082 *** 0101' with a 'Sending' badge that becomes 'Sent at 10:05' or 'Not delivered', with the text behind 'Show the message'; digits shown because the viewer booked or proved the number). notifications[] on GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference}. InboxPage at /messages (recipient '082 *** ****', status, failureReason, attempts, nextAttemptAt, lastError, and Retry on a retryable FAILED row). Dispatcher log lines and the outbox_pending gauge.
EMAIL (Tier 3, cut by default: queued only when the channel ships and customer.email was given; subject plus plain-text body)SimulatedEmailGateway has the same shape: 150 ms, and a deterministic failure for addresses at @fail.example. The recipient is masked 'n***a@example.com' to a caller who proved ownership and 'n***@example.com' in /messages and logs. Per-recipient cap (Tier 3, only if it ships): 5 per rolling 24 h. Unless this channel ships, customer.email is absent from the request record, the spec and the form, and the channel CHECK keeps 'EMAIL' for the future.The same three places: the ConfirmationPanel email line, notifications[] in the appointment response, and /messages with subject and body.
On-screen confirmation (the persistent ConfirmationPage; not a message row, but the first thing the customer sees)Nothing is simulated. It is rendered from the 201 body (reference, service, branch address, SAST window, documents, manage link) and refreshed by 2 s polling while anything is PENDING./appointments/{reference}, immediately after booking, and again after a refresh or via /manage once the reference and the last 4 digits are entered.

The messages

BRANCHBOOK · simulated SMS
Appt confirmed: FICA update, Sandton Summit, Tue 27 Oct 09:30 SAST. Ref APT-7K3M-9Q2X. Bring ID + docs. Manage: http://localhost:8080/manage
140 of 160 characters · GSM-7 · one segment
SMS template rules and the cancellation SMS

Confirmation: 'Appt confirmed: {service.sms_name}, {branch.sms_name}, {EEE d MMM} {HH:mm} SAST. Ref {reference}. Bring ID + docs. Manage: {public_base_url}/manage'. Example: 'Appt confirmed: FICA update, Sandton Summit, Tue 27 Oct 09:30 SAST. Ref APT-7K3M-9Q2X. Bring ID + docs. Manage: http://localhost:8080/manage' (140 characters). The fixed text is 94 characters including '/manage' and the widest date 'Wed 30 Sep'. With sms_name <= 18 and branch sms_name <= 20 (the column bounds (varchar(18) and varchar(20)), used directly as the worst case), the worst case is 153 with the 21-character localhost base. That leaves a public-base-url budget of 28 GSM-7 septets: 'https://book.fynbos.example' (27) gives 159; a 33-character URL would give 165 and is refused. Guards: - NotificationProperties, through NotificationTemplates.longestConfirmationSeptets, rejects a base URL over 28 septets at startup (NotificationPropertiesTest.rejectsBaseUrlThatOverflowsSms), so configuration can never fail a booking. - Gsm7.septets, counting extension characters as 2, is the single authority. The notification_messages_sms_len CHECK is kept only as a coarse 320-character guard (the data model's decision), so message formatting can never roll back a booking. - Tests: NotificationTemplatesTest.confirmationFitsOneGsm7SegmentForLongestSeededNames (GSM-7 characters only, one segment), longestCombinationWithTheLongestAllowedBaseUrlFitsOneSegment, rendersThePublishedExampleExactly, rendersEnglishDayNamesUnderAnyDefaultLocale. - Dates use DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(..., Locale.ENGLISH) in branch.timezone, so 'Tue 27 Oct' never becomes 'Di 27 Okt'. 'Bring ID + docs.' is accurate for every seeded service: each lists an ID document plus supporting documents. The full list is on the confirmation page and in the email. The SMS links only to /manage. The per-reference URL would add about 20 characters and break the one-segment proof. Cancellation: 'Appt cancelled: {service.sms_name}, {branch.sms_name}, {EEE d MMM} {HH:mm} SAST. Ref {reference}. Rebook: {public_base_url}/book'. Example: 'Appt cancelled: FICA update, Sandton Summit, Tue 27 Oct 09:30 SAST. Ref APT-7K3M-9Q2X. Rebook: http://localhost:8080/book' (121 characters; worst case 141 with a 28-septet base). No SMS ever contains a customer field. The demo disclaimer lives in the web app's banners and the privacy notice, not in the 160-character body.

Email template, verbatim

Subject: 'Appointment confirmed - {reference} | {service.name}, {EEEE d MMMM yyyy} at {HH:mm}'. Body (plain text): 'Hi {firstName}, Your appointment is confirmed. Reference: {reference} Service: {service.name} (about {durationMinutes} minutes) Branch: {branch.name} {branch.address.line1}, {branch.address.suburb}, {branch.address.city}, {branch.address.postalCode} When: {EEEE d MMMM yyyy}, {HH:mm} - {end HH:mm} (South African Standard Time) What to bring: - {documentsNeeded[0]} - {documentsNeeded[1]} ... Please arrive 10 minutes early. Need to change or cancel? {manage_url} (You will be asked for your reference and the last 4 digits of your cellphone number.) Your privacy: Fynbos Bank (fictional demo) uses these details only to schedule, confirm and manage this appointment. Access, correction and complaint rights: {public_url}/privacy. Information Regulator: inforegulator.org.za. --- This is a simulated message generated by a demonstration project. It was not sent by any bank.' Dates and times use Locale.ENGLISH in branch.timezone. Cancellation variant: subject 'Appointment cancelled - {reference}'; the body states the cancelled slot and the rebook link. The email never contains the mobile number in any form. Tests: emailListsDocumentsAndSastWindow, emailNeverContainsFullMobileNumber. Templates live in one class (NotificationTemplates), with snapshot files under src/test/resources/templates. The whole channel is Tier 3 and cut by default; unless it ships, none of this is built and the email field is not in the API.

Reference number

APT-XXXX-XXXX (MASTER reference_format): 8 characters from ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789 (31 symbols, no 0/O/1/I/L) drawn with SecureRandom, for example APT-7K3M-9Q2X. Stored uppercase in appointments.reference varchar(13) UNIQUE with CHECK (reference ~ '^APT-[A-HJKMNP-Z2-9]{4}-[A-HJKMNP-Z2-9]{4}$'). Lookup input is trimmed, upper-cased and hyphens are optional. A collision re-runs the booking transaction once. The reference appears in every message, the Location header, the inbox and the manage page. It never appears in any problem body.

Reliability

  • Delivery is at-least-once and idempotent on message id: the id is what a real provider would dedupe on, and provider_message_id is stored for future delivery-receipt callbacks.
  • Claiming.
  • Rows are claimed with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, so two API instances never send the same message.
  • Claim, send and mark share ONE transaction per row, with up to 20 rows per tick.
  • We never claim in one transaction and process in another, which would double-send after the locks drop or self-deadlock on REQUIRES_NEW.
  • Nor do we batch 20 rows into one transaction, because one failed statement aborts a PostgreSQL transaction (25P02) and would roll back marks whose sends had succeeded.
  • A crash re-sends at most the one in-flight message.
  • Poison handling.
  • Gateway exceptions become a retry with backoff inside the row's transaction.
  • Any other failure (mapping, the mark, the database, or an Error thrown by the gateway) rolls back only that pass, and a separate short transaction marks the row FAILED / DISPATCH_ERROR with last_error MARK_FAILED, or deletes a cancellation instead, so no row can loop or block the batch. The MessageGateway port cuts an error code to 500 characters and refuses a provider id that is not 1 to 64 (MessageGatewayTest).
  • Backoff. 2, 4, 8 and 16 s after failures 1 to 4, via next_attempt_at = :now + 2^attempts s.
  • FAILED / DELIVERY_FAILED on the fifth failed attempt, about 35 to 40 s end to end, or on the first refusal the gateway calls permanent, such as AWS's for a number the SMS sandbox has not verified or a monthly spend limit reached. last_error keeps the refusal's fixed code, which outbox.retry and outbox.failed log beside the message id.
  • Time.
  • Every time-dependent statement binds :now from the Clock, so MutableClock tests are deterministic.
  • Ordering.
  • The cancel transaction deletes every confirmation of the appointment, whatever its status, waiting for a row the dispatcher has locked, and then deletes the appointment (V6).
  • Since M6, the dispatcher also asks booking, before it sends a confirmation, whether its appointment is cancelled, and marks it FAILED / SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION instead, which since V6 catches only a confirmation of an appointment cancelled before V6 or set CANCELLED by hand. A retry refuses superseded rows.
  • A confirmation is therefore delivered before its cancellation or never.
  • A cancellation outlives its deleted appointment only until it is final: where a confirmation would be marked SENT or FAILED, the dispatcher deletes it in the same transaction and logs outbox.deleted.
  • Abuse.
  • Where branchbook.sms.recipient-daily-cap is above 0, as on the hosted demo (5), a number outside the demo block that has been written that many SMS in the last 24 hours, counted in the ledger sms_recipient_sends (V7), where the messages a cancel or the dispatcher deleted still count, gets a further confirmation written FAILED / RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP, and a further cancellation reported so in the cancel's response but never stored; neither is sent or retried, and the booking or the cancel stands.
  • The threat and its production controls are documented in ADR-0004.
  • Status.
  • A row never goes to SENT before a send was attempted.
  • Delivery status never changes appointment status: the appointment is CONFIRMED at commit because the seat is what the customer bought, and the message status is a separate fact (stated in README and ADR-0003, and tested by NotificationDispatcherIT.retriesWithBackoffThenMarksFailedAfterFiveAttempts).
  • Replay happens only via POST /messages/{id}/retry or the runbook UPDATE, both guarded to DELIVERY_FAILED and DISPATCH_ERROR rows.
  • Scheduling.
  • The dispatcher runs on its own single-thread scheduler, so a slow gateway cannot starve other scheduled work. 150 ms inside one row's lock is harmless at this scale, and ADR-0003 names the lease-column variant for a slow real provider.
  • Observability.
  • There is no outbox health indicator, because health shows no component details anywhere. The simulated inbox (GET /api/v1/messages) and the SQL in docs/runbook.md show pending and failed messages.
  • There is an outbox_pending gauge (M6), counted once per dispatcher tick and served on /actuator/prometheus under make up; the hosted demo serves only health and info. notifications_dispatched_total is not built. correlation_id is on every row and log line, and recipients appear in no log.
  • Tests: ConfirmationOutboxIT.bookingAndPendingMessagesCommitAtomically, RecipientDailyCapIT.theSmsPastTheCapIsWrittenFailedAndTheBookingStillSucceeds and theDemoBlockIsNeverCapped; RecipientCapLedgerIT.bookingAndCancellingInTurnReachesTheCapThoughEveryMessageIsDeleted; BookAppointmentIT.failureAfterTheSmsIsWrittenRollsTheSmsBackWithTheBooking and NotificationOutboxIT.refusesToWriteOutsideATransaction;
  • NotificationDispatcherIT.marksSentWithProviderMessageId, retriesWithBackoffThenMarksFailedAfterFiveAttempts, twoDispatchersNeverSendTheSameMessageTwice, poisonedRowDoesNotBlockTheBatch, oneTickHandlesAtMostTwentyMessages, gatewayThatThrowsIsTriedAgain, gatewayErrorMarksTheMessageAndTheBatchCarriesOn, and DispatcherLoopIT for the real loop; confirmationOfACancelledAppointmentResetByHandIsSupersededAtTheClaimNotSent from M6; cancelDeletesThePendingConfirmationAndTheSentCancellationIsDeleted from V6; permanentRefusalMarksFailedOnItsFirstAttemptAndLogsItsCode; AwsSmsGatewayTest and RoutingSmsGatewayTest for the real gateway and the demo block;
  • InboxApiIT.listsTheNewestMessagesWithTheirReferencesAndRedactedRecipients and InboxSwitchedOffIT.inboxIsAPlain404WhenSwitchedOff; RetryMessageApiIT.retryOfDeliveryFailedResetsItAndTheNextTickSendsIt and retryOfSupersededConfirmationIsNoOp; InboxReferencesHiddenIT for the hidden references.

How a reviewer sees it work

  1. Demo numbers (also in the README; they are always simulated, even on the hosted demo, which texts every other South African mobile). Tab A: 082 555 0101. Tab B: 082 555 0102. Extra bookings: 082 555 0103. Always-failing SMS: any number ending 0000, e.g. 082 555 0000. Failing email (if the email channel shipped): anything@fail.example. Branches are allocated so runs never collide: DemoDataLoader books at Bellville Kingsway and Umhlanga Ridge; smoke.sh at Sandton Summit; demo-409.sh and Playwright at Polokwane Savannah.
  2. Run make up and open http://localhost:8080 in two tabs, A and B. In both, choose Polokwane Savannah (capacity 1), 'Update personal details (FICA)', the first open day and the SAME time showing '1 left'. Fill the details (A: 082 555 0101, B: 082 555 010
  3. and stop on Review in both tabs. Confirm in A: the confirmation page shows the reference and the SMS 'To 082 *** 0101' marked 'Sending', which becomes 'Sent at HH:MM' within 2 to 4 s, with the SMS text behind 'Show the message'. Switch to B and confirm. B gets 'That time was just taken' with up to three alternatives and its details intact. Pick an alternative and B books. ReviewStep does not consult availability, so B's submit really reaches the server and the 409 is reproducible every time.
  4. Click 'See the SMS in the simulated inbox' (/messages). Both SMS show as SENT with attempts 1. Recipients appear as '082 *** ****', because the inbox is an unauthenticated demo view and never shows digits. DemoDataLoader's three bookings at Bellville Kingsway and Umhlanga Ridge are listed too; the one to a number ending 0000 is FAILED, or still retrying during the first ~40 s after start.
  5. Book any slot with 082 555 0000. /messages shows attempts climbing from 1 to 5 over about 35 to 40 s (2+4+8+16 s of backoff plus 2 s ticks), then FAILED / DELIVERY_FAILED with lastError SIMULATED_FAILURE. The appointment itself stays CONFIRMED. Press Retry: the row returns to PENDING with attempts 0 and fails again, which proves the replay path.
  6. Cancel: open /manage, enter tab A's reference and 0101, and cancel. The page says the appointment is cancelled and its details deleted, and the cancellation SMS appears in /messages, with APT-****-**** as its reference, until it is sent and deleted; opening the reference again in /manage finds nothing. Cancel the 0000 booking while its confirmation is still retrying: the confirmation is deleted with the booking, so it is never sent after the cancellation, and the cancellation to 0000 is tried five times and then deleted. In the 'Book again' form, only the branch and service are pre-filled; the details are not.
  7. Headless, safe to repeat. make smoke runs scripts/smoke.sh, which writes nothing, so it is safe against any instance. It checks the probes, the app, the branch and availability APIs, the error contract and the security headers. It also checks that booking and lookup answer, using requests refused before the database is touched (a booking without an Idempotency-Key, one with a landline, a lookup without X-Mobile-Last4) and a lookup of a reference that cannot exist, which is never counted. scripts/demo-409.sh finds the next Polokwane FICA slot with seatsAvailable 1 at run time, fires 20 parallel bookings from 20 distinct demo numbers between 082 555 0100 and 0199, prints the chosen slot and the 1 x 201 / 19 x 409 split, and exits non-zero on any other split. curl 'localhost:8080/api/v1/messages?limit=20' shows the outbox, and docs/runbook.md has the SQL. To repeat the browser walkthrough, change the last two digits of the numbers. 0102 still holds an upcoming FICA appointment, so reusing it returns DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT.

Calendar invite

Not built in v1 (MASTER out_of_scope). It would cost about an hour that competes with UI states and the three proof tests. SAST correctness is already proven by timestamptz storage, the +02:00 literal test and web tests under two time zones, and the brief asks for a communicated confirmation, not a calendar artefact. It is the first item in the README next steps, with the design fixed so it is on record as decided, not forgotten: METHOD:PUBLISH; UID {reference}@branchbook.example; DTSTART/DTEND with TZID=Africa/Johannesburg and a VTIMEZONE holding one STANDARD component (TZOFFSETFROM/TO +0200, no DST); SUMMARY '{service.name} - {branch.name}'; LOCATION the full address; DESCRIPTION with reference, documents and manage link; STATUS:CONFIRMED (STATUS:CANCELLED with METHOD:CANCEL on cancellation); VALARM -PT1H; SEQUENCE 0. It would be served as GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference}/calendar.ics with X-Mobile-Last4 (fetched by the SPA and saved as a blob) and attached to the email row through an attachments column.

Every step of the confirmation flow
#ActorActionNotes
1DBHolds the committed PENDING notification_messages rows (next_attempt_at <= :now), indexed by the partial index ix_notification_messages_due.Rows exist only because the booking or cancel transaction committed; a rolled-back booking leaves nothing.
2DispatcherDispatcherLoop, a SmartLifecycle, calls NotificationDispatcher.runOnce(now) with a 2 s fixed delay on its own single thread, outbox-1: up to 20 passes per tick, each pass its own TransactionTemplate in which claim, send and mark share one transaction for one row.A fixed-delay task never overlaps itself. The loop owns its thread rather than a TaskScheduler bean, which would replace Spring Boot's default scheduler and move the hourly and daily jobs onto it. In tests branchbook.dispatcher.enabled=false, and tests call runOnce with the instant they want, 2, 4, 8 and 16 s apart, with no sleeps; DispatcherLoopIT runs the real loop.
3DBClaim one row: SELECT ... FROM notification_messages WHERE status='PENDING' AND next_attempt_at <= :now ORDER BY next_attempt_at, id LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED.No row ends the tick. A second API instance skips locked rows, so no message is sent twice (NotificationDispatcherIT.twoDispatchersNeverSendTheSameMessageTwice). Only the message row is locked. Since M6 the dispatcher then asks booking's AppointmentDirectory, by a plain read, whether the appointment is cancelled; if that read fails, the pass rolls back and the row stays PENDING for the next tick.
4DispatcherPuts the row's correlation_id in MDC. It hands an OutgoingMessage (id, channel, recipient, body) to the one MessageGateway. A CONFIRMATION whose appointment is CANCELLED is marked FAILED / SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION without sending (M6).A cancel deletes its booking's confirmations (V6), so the supersede guard now catches only a confirmation of an appointment cancelled before V6 or set CANCELLED by hand (NotificationDispatcherIT.confirmationOfACancelledAppointmentResetByHandIsSupersededAtTheClaimNotSent).
5ChannelSimulatedSmsGateway waits 150 ms (branchbook.dispatcher.simulated-latency) and returns Accepted(providerMessageId 'sim-<uuid>'); SimulatedEmailGateway (Tier 3 only) would do the same. Deterministic failure: an SMS recipient ending 0000 returns Refused(SIMULATED_FAILURE), as would an email at @fail.example (Tier 3).The dispatcher writes one log line per attempt (outbox.sent, outbox.retry or outbox.failed with messageId and attempts, and for a refusal its fixed code), never the recipient, the body or the reference; the gateway logs nothing. Where branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, RoutingSmsGateway sends a number outside the demo block through AwsSmsGateway instead: no SDK retries, a 10 s limit on the call, AWS's MessageId as the provider id, and a refusal recorded as a fixed code such as AWS_THROTTLED, never AWS's message, which can quote the number. A refusal is permanent, and fails the message at once, for any conflict (such as a number the sandbox has not verified), access denied, an invalid request, a missing resource or a monthly spend limit reached; any other is retried. It logs nothing either.
6DBSuccess: an immediate JdbcClient UPDATE sets status='SENT', sent_at=:now, provider_message_id (1 to 64 characters, enforced by MessageGateway.Accepted) and attempts=attempts+1; COMMIT of that row's transaction. A CANCELLATION is deleted instead, in the same transaction (outbox.deleted).The Tier 3 counter notifications_dispatched_total is not built (NotificationDispatcherIT.marksSentWithProviderMessageId).
7DBGateway failure: UPDATE attempts=attempts+1, last_error (truncated to <= 500, no PII), next_attempt_at = :now + 2^attempts seconds (2, 4, 8, 16 s after failures 1-4). The fifth failure, or a refusal the gateway calls permanent, sets status='FAILED' with failure_reason DELIVERY_FAILED; a CANCELLATION is deleted instead. COMMIT.About 30 s of backoff, so FAILED is reached about 35 to 40 s after the first attempt (NotificationDispatcherIT.retriesWithBackoffThenMarksFailedAfterFiveAttempts). The outbox_pending gauge (M6) counts the row while it is PENDING; the Tier 3 outcome counter is not built; the inbox and docs/runbook.md show the row. The appointment row is untouched: delivery status never changes appointment status.
8DispatcherAny other failure (row mapping, the mark statement, the database, or an Error thrown by the gateway) rolls back only that pass. A separate short TransactionTemplate marks the row FAILED / DISPATCH_ERROR with last_error 'MARK_FAILED', or deletes a CANCELLATION still PENDING (the exception class goes to the ERROR log line). If that also fails, the tick stops and resumes in 2 s.No row can loop or block the batch (NotificationDispatcherIT.poisonedRowDoesNotBlockTheBatch, using a test-only trigger that raises on one row's SENT update). A crash re-sends at most the one in-flight message: delivery is at-least-once, deduplicated on message id by a real provider.
9SPAConfirmationPage polls GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference} with X-Mobile-Last4 every 2 s while any notification is PENDING (TanStack refetchInterval).Polling stops at SENT or FAILED, with no time cap: a 0000 recipient reaches FAILED in about 35 s, and meanwhile the page says the booking is already saved. The appointment itself is already shown as CONFIRMED.
10APIReturns AppointmentResponse with notifications[] (status, attempts, sentAt, failureReason, recipientMasked with digits because the caller proved them, body).Each poll is a lookup; successes do not count against LookupAttemptLimiter.
11ClientSees 'To 082 *** 0101' with a 'Sent at 10:05' badge (aria-live polite) with the rendered SMS text, and, if the Tier 3 email channel shipped, the email line likewise. The FAILED copy depends on failureReason. DELIVERY_FAILED: 'We could not send the SMS after 5 attempts. Your booking still stands; keep your reference.' SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION: 'The SMS was not sent, because the appointment was cancelled.' RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP (where the daily cap is on): the general 'We could not send the SMS.', and in the inbox 'Not sent: the number reached its limit of messages for the day'.Links: 'See the SMS in the simulated inbox', or 'See what happened in the simulated inbox' after a failure (/messages) and 'Book another appointment' (/book).
12InboxThe /messages page calls GET /api/v1/messages?limit=50 (only while branchbook.inbox.enabled is true, as in compose.yaml, make dev-api; otherwise, as on the hosted demo, a plain 404, and the page says the inbox is switched off on this deployment) and lists every message newest first, with channel, digit-free recipient, status, failureReason, attempts, nextAttemptAt, lastError and body, under the banner 'Demo operator view. It lists every booking's messages, recipients redacted, and is switched off in production. Here every message is simulated, as on any local run.'. Where branchbook.inbox.redact-references is on, every reference shows as APT-****-****, as plain text rather than a link; a cancellation whose booking the cancel deleted shows it so anywhere, and leaves the list once it is sent or has failed for good.It polls every 2 s while any row is PENDING, so attempts can be watched climbing for a 0000 recipient.
13ClientClicks Retry on a FAILED row.The button appears only when retryable is true: FAILED with DELIVERY_FAILED or DISPATCH_ERROR, and for a confirmation, an appointment neither cancelled nor started (RetryPolicy). The answer is said in a status region in the message's card.
14InboxPOST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry.Runbook fallback: UPDATE notification_messages SET status='PENDING', attempts=0, next_attempt_at=now(), last_error=NULL, failure_reason=NULL WHERE id='...' AND status='FAILED' AND failure_reason IN ('DELIVERY_FAILED','DISPATCH_ERROR'), plus the same guard against a CONFIRMATION of a cancelled or started appointment. The runbook may use now() because it is operator SQL, not application code.
15DBRetryMessageHandler, in one transaction: SELECT ... FROM notification_messages WHERE id=:id FOR UPDATE, waiting up to 2 s for a send in flight (none, as for a cancellation deleted once final: 404 MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND); RetryPolicy checks FAILED with DELIVERY_FAILED or DISPATCH_ERROR and, for a CONFIRMATION, an appointment neither cancelled nor started, read through booking's AppointmentDirectory; then UPDATE ... SET status='PENDING', attempts=0, next_attempt_at=:now, last_error=NULL, failure_reason=NULL WHERE id=:id RETURNING the row. Any other message is returned unchanged with retryable false.Flow returns to step 2 within 2 s. A 0000 recipient fails again by design, so the reviewer sees the retry cycle (RetryMessageApiIT.retryOfDeliveryFailedResetsItAndTheNextTickSendsIt).

DesignBuilt

Domain model

Status. Built: Branch, OperatingHours, ServiceType, PublicHolidayCalendar, BranchCalendar, BookingWindow, ServiceOffer, SlotCalendar, Slot, BookedInterval, SlotProblem, Appointment with cancellation (M6, CancelOutcome; since V6 the cancel deletes the appointment in the same transaction), AppointmentStatus, CustomerDetails, the value objects MobileNumber, CustomerName, AppointmentReference and IdempotencyKey, and the notification messages with delivery attempts, supersession, retry and the daily cap, which counts in its ledger, sms_recipient_sends (V7). The lookup limit's counts live in lookup_attempts (V8). Check-in, completion and no-show stay domain-only, with no endpoint.

States

StateMeaning
CONFIRMEDSeat reserved at commit; confirmation queued in the same transaction. The only state from which the customer can cancel. Set on insert; there is no REQUESTED/HELD state because booking is single-step and transactional. In v1 a CONFIRMED row stays CONFIRMED after its start time (no staff actor, no sweeper); every customer-facing rule is time-relative against the Clock, so such a row is not cancellable (canCancel false), never blocks a new booking of the same service and holds a seat only in the past.
CHECKED_INCustomer arrived and was checked in by branch staff within the check-in window. Still ACTIVE (holds the seat). Reachable only through Appointment.checkIn() in the domain and its tests; no endpoint in v1.
COMPLETEDTerminal. Consultation finished and marked done by staff. Domain-only in v1. Releases the seat for the constraint predicate.
CANCELLEDTerminal, and not kept. Cancelled by the customer before starts_at via the API: Appointment.cancel sets CANCELLED and cancelled_at in memory, and the same transaction deletes every CONFIRMATION of it, whatever its status, writes the CANCELLATION message and deletes the row with its version checked, which frees the seat at once (V6). The 200 response shows the appointment as CANCELLED. Only a row cancelled before V6, or by hand, is ever found CANCELLED, and a cancel then answers its reference as an unknown one: 404, counted as a wrong try.
NO_SHOWTerminal. Customer never checked in by starts_at + 15 min. Set by Appointment.markNoShow(); the 15-minute sweeper job is a documented next step, not built.

Transitions

FromToTriggerGuard
(none)CONFIRMEDPOST /api/v1/appointments -> BookAppointmentHandler.handleIdempotency-Key fresh (or same key + same hash -> replay, or 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED once that booking is cancelled); branch active and branch_services row exists; SlotCalendar.validate(startsAt) == OK; under the per-mobile advisory lock (steps 4-5) no upcoming active appointment for the same mobile and service (else 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT) and no active appointment for the same mobile overlapping [startsAt, endsAt) (else 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP); a free seat exists under the branch_services FOR UPDATE lock (else 409 SLOT_FULL). Side effects in the same transaction: reference generated, ends_at computed, appointment persisted and flushed, PENDING CONFIRMATION rows enqueued, idempotency row completed.
CONFIRMEDCANCELLEDPOST /api/v1/appointments/{reference}/cancel with X-Mobile-Last4 -> CancelAppointmentHandler -> Appointment.cancel(now)reference + last4 match, counted with lookups by the same limiter (else 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, which is also the answer, counted, for a reference already cancelled and so deleted); status == CONFIRMED and now < starts_at (else 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL, appointment left as it was); Appointment.cancel returns CancelOutcome CANCELLED, ALREADY_CANCELLED (only for a row cancelled before V6, or by hand, answered as 404, counted) or TOO_LATE; the version-checked lock resolves concurrent cancels: the second finds the row gone (OptimisticLockingFailureException) and answers 404, counted. Side effects in the same transaction: cancelled_at = updated_at = now in memory; JpaAppointmentRepository.lockForDeletion detaches the entity and locks the row FOR UPDATE with its version; every CONFIRMATION row deleted, whatever its status, waiting for one being sent; the CANCELLATION SMS written, unless the daily cap stops it; the response rendered; the appointment deleted with its version checked, which leaves the cancellation and the idempotency key with no appointment. Built in M6 (CancelAppointmentIT); the delete since V6.
CONFIRMEDCHECKED_INAppointment.checkIn(now) (domain state machine only; staff endpoint out of scope, ADR-0004)starts_at - PT30M <= now <= starts_at + PT15M; status == CONFIRMED. Side effect: checked_in_at = now.
CHECKED_INCOMPLETEDAppointment.complete(now) (domain only)status == CHECKED_IN; no time constraint. Side effect: completed_at = now (checked_in_at kept).
CONFIRMEDNO_SHOWAppointment.markNoShow(now) (domain only; a 15-minute sweeper is a next step)now > starts_at + PT15M and status == CONFIRMED. Side effect: no_show_at = now; seat released by the constraint predicate.
COMPLETED | CANCELLED | NO_SHOW(any)any callcanTransitionTo returns false; domain throws IllegalStateTransition -> 409 (AppointmentStatusTest.terminalStatesRejectEveryTransition)

Entities and value objects

Branch Catalogue aggregate (read-only at runtime, seeded by Flyway). A physical Fynbos Bank branch with its own…

Catalogue aggregate (read-only at runtime, seeded by Flyway). A physical Fynbos Bank branch with its own timezone and per-weekday hours. Owns OperatingHours and BranchService rows. Exposed by GET /api/v1/branches and GET /api/v1/branches/{code}. Read through JdbcClient row mappers into a plain domain record (catalogue tables are not JPA entities, so ddl-auto=validate never has to reconcile their composite keys or array columns).

FieldTypeNotes
idUUIDPK, generated by the application (UUID v4) in the V3 seed as fixed literals
codeString (varchar 8)Immutable business key, ^[A-Z]{2,8}$; seeds SAN, BEL, UMH, GQE, POL, RUS; used in URLs
nameString (varchar 60)Display name, e.g. 'Sandton Summit Mall'
smsNameString (varchar 20)Short label for the SMS template; varchar(20) and the GSM-7 CHECK branches_sms_name_gsm7 keep the SMS under 160 GSM-7; seeds 'Sandton Summit' (14), 'Bellville Kingsway' (18), 'Umhlanga Ridge' (14), 'Gqeberha Baywater' (17), 'Polokwane Savannah' (18), 'Rustenburg Bojanala' (19)
addressLine1String (varchar 120)Plainly fictional street address (e.g. 'Shop 4, 1 Protea Lane'); never a real street number in a real mall
suburbString (varchar 60)Shown in the branch list
cityString (varchar 60)Shown in the branch list
provinceString (varchar 20)One of the nine SA provinces (CHECK)
postalCodeString (varchar 4)Four digits (CHECK); varchar, not char, so no bpchar padding or type mismatch
timezoneZoneId (varchar 40)IANA id, every seed 'Africa/Johannesburg'; SlotCalendar converts wall-clock hours through it, never by adding +02:00
openOnPublicHolidaysbooleanDefault false for every seed; true would let SlotCalendar ignore public_holidays for this branch
activebooleanInactive branch is absent from lists/availability and books 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND; existing appointments stay readable
createdAtInstant (timestamptz)Audit only

Invariants

  • code is unique and immutable once any appointment references the branch
  • timezone must parse as java.time.ZoneId (BranchTest.rejectsUnknownZoneId)
  • opensSunday (GET /branches) is derived: a branch_operating_hours row with weekday = 7 exists
  • Branch holds no object graph: OperatingHours and BranchService are loaded by branch_id (UUID FK columns, never @ManyToOne)
  • Every seeded address, name and number is fictional; nothing names or resembles a real bank
OperatingHours One row per branch per ISO weekday on which the branch trades; absence of a row means closed that day.…

One row per branch per ISO weekday on which the branch trades; absence of a row means closed that day. Branch-local wall-clock times.

FieldTypeNotes
branchIdUUIDFK branches.id; part of PK
weekdayshort (1-7)ISO numbering = java.time.DayOfWeek.getValue(): 1 Monday ... 7 Sunday (OperatingHoursTest.weekdayNumberingMatchesDayOfWeek)
opensAtLocalTime (time)Branch-local; first slot starts here
closesAtLocalTime (time)Branch-local; every slot must satisfy startsAt + duration <= closesAt

Invariants

  • PK (branch_id, weekday): at most one row per branch per weekday
  • CHECK opens_at < closes_at
  • No row = closed: Sunday rows exist only for SAN and UMH (09:00-13:00); RUS Saturday is 08:00-12:00
  • Converted to instants only via Branch.timezone (SlotCalendarTest.producesSameSlotsUnderAnyJvmDefaultZone)
  • Worked last starts, pinned by SlotCalendarTest: weekday 08:00-17:00 -> 15 min 16:45, 30 min 16:30, 45 min 16:15; Saturday 08:00-13:00 -> 15 min 12:45, 30 min 12:30, 45 min 11:45 (12:30 is on the 45-minute grid but ends 13:15); RUS Saturday 08:00-12:00 -> 30 min 11:30, 45 min 11:00 (11:45 ends 12:30); Sunday 09:00-13:00 -> 30 min 12:30, 45 min 12:00
Service Catalogue entity: a bookable branch service with a fixed duration that defines its slot grid, and the…

Catalogue entity: a bookable branch service with a fixed duration that defines its slot grid, and the documents to bring that the confirmation repeats.

FieldTypeNotes

nameString (varchar 60)e.g. 'Update personal details (FICA)'

descriptionString (varchar 300)Plain-language card copy
durationMinutesshortCHECK between 15 and 120 and multiple of 15; seeds FICA 15, CARD 15, OPEN 30, CREDIT 30, ESTATE 30, HOME 45; defines the grid: slots start at opens_at + k x duration
documentsNeededList<String> (text[])PostgreSQL text array, 1-8 non-null entries (CHECK); read with JdbcClient as java.sql.Array, so no JSON FormatMapper (Jackson 2 vs 3) is involved; rendered on the service card, review step, confirmation screen and email; every seeded list names an ID document, which is why the SMS can say 'Bring ID + docs.'
