The design seeds six of these, with 15, 30 and 45-minute durations, so that slot grids differ between services and the capacity-1 branch makes the race easy to reproduce.

Mobile numbers

Required. Client pre-check: strip spaces, hyphens, dots, brackets and '(0)', then match ^(\+27|0027|27|0)(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$. Server: MobileNumber.parse via libphonenumber region ZA, accepted only when the type is MOBILE and the E.164 form matches E164_PATTERN ^\+27(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$ ('082 555 0101', '0825550101', '+27 82 555 0101', '+27 (0)82 555 0101', '27825550101', '0027825550101' -> +27825550101); landlines 01-05, VoIP 087 (including sub-ranges libphonenumber labels MOBILE), 080/086 rejected; stored E.164 only under a CHECK that uses the same pattern, so a parsed number can never raise 23514. 422 MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED, errors[{field:'customer.mobile'}].

Message the customer sees: We need a cellphone number to SMS your confirmation

  • parse accepts '082 555 0101', '0825550101', '+27 82 555 0101', '+27 (0)82 555 0101', '27825550101', '0027825550101' and yields +27825550101 (MobileNumberTest.sixInputFormatsNormaliseToPlus27825550101)
  • Rejects landlines 01-05, VoIP 087, 080/086 and any non-MOBILE type with MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED; the VoIP fixture is one libphonenumber reports as VOIP and the test asserts that type (MobileNumberTest.rejectsLandline021Voip087AndTollFree080)
  • E164_PATTERN equals the appointments_customer_mobile_e164 CHECK read from pg_constraint (SchemaConstraintIT.mobileCheckMatchesMobileNumberPattern)
  • Raw input is never persisted or logged; toString() returns redacted()
Formats people actually type, from the research
  • National format 0XX XXX XXXX (10 digits incl. trunk prefix 0). ICASA's numbering plan: national numbers are 10 digits; mobile numbers start 06, 07 or 08 (e.g. 060-068, 071-074, 076, 078-079, 081-084). Landlines start 01-05; 087 is VoIP (treat as non-SMS); 08x premium/freephone ranges 080/086 are not mobiles.
  • International format +27 XX XXX XXXX - E.164 is +27 followed by the 9 national significant digits (the leading 0 is dropped), 11 digits after the plus, e.g. +27825550101.
  • Country-code-without-plus 27XXXXXXXXX (11 digits) and international-access-code form 0027XXXXXXXXX (13 digits) - both common in copy/paste and CSV data.
  • Formatted variants with spaces, hyphens, dots and brackets: '082 555 0101', '082-555-0101', '(082) 555 0101', '+27 (0)82 555 0101' (the '(0)' must be stripped).
  • Normalisation algorithm (store E.164 only): strip everything except digits and a leading '+'; if starts with '0027' -> '+27' + rest; if starts with '27' and length 11 -> '+' + digits; if starts with '0' and length 10 -> '+27' + digits[1:]; if starts with '+27' and length 12 -> keep; then require ^\+27[6-8]\d{8}$ for the SMS channel. Prefer the libphonenumber port for the stack (libphonenumber-js, google-libphonenumber, PhoneNumbers for .NET, libphonenumber for Java) with region 'ZA' and number type MOBILE, and keep a hand-written fallback regex test suite: phone_normalises_national_to_e164, phone_normalises_0027_prefix, phone_rejects_landline_021, phone_rejects_voip_087, phone_strips_bracketed_zero.
  • Display format back to users: 0XX XXX XXXX (what South Africans recognise); masked display in confirmations: 082 *** 0101.

SA ID numbers: documented, deliberately not collected

Format
13 digits: YYMMDD SSSS C A Z. YYMMDD = date of birth (two-digit year; infer century: if YY > current two-digit year then 19YY else 20YY, and reject impossible dates such as 000230). SSSS = gender sequence: 0000-4999 female, 5000-9999 male. C = citizenship: 0 SA citizen, 1 permanent resident (2 refugee appears in some validators - accept 0 or 1 only). A = legacy race indicator, now almost always 8 (9 seen on older numbers) - validate as a single digit and do not reject on value. Z = Luhn check digit. Regex gate before checksum: ^\d{2}(0[1-9]|1[0-2])(0[1-9]|[12]\d|3[01])\d{4}[01]\d\d$
Checksum
Luhn (mod 10) over all 13 digits: from the right-most digit (the check digit) moving left, double every second digit (i.e. positions 12, 10, 8, ... counted from the left), subtract 9 from any product over 9, sum everything, and the total must be divisible by 10. SARS's worked example 8001015009087: odd positions 8+0+0+5+0+0 = 13; even-position digits 011098 x 2 = 22196, digits sum 2+2+1+9+6 = 20; 13+20 = 33; check = (10 - 3) mod 10 = 7. A passing checksum proves only that the number is well-formed, not that Home Affairs issued it. Tests to write: id_rejects_wrong_checksum, id_rejects_impossible_date_000230, id_accepts_leap_day_000229, id_parses_gender_and_citizenship, id_rejects_non_digits_and_wrong_length.
Synthetic example
9001015000085 - synthetic: born 1990-01-01, sequence 5000 (lowest male sequence), citizen (0), A=8, Luhn check digit 5 (verified by script). The SARS documentation example 8001015009087 is another widely used test value. Use these in seeds, fixtures and README; never use a real person's number.

POPIA

Collect first name, last name and mobile; email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships. No ID number, no consent checkbox, no login. Show the mobile as '082 *** 0101' only to the booker and to a caller who has just proved it, and as '082 *** ****' in the inbox. Logs, MDC and metrics carry no recipient. No problem body reveals another booking's reference. LogRedactionIT runs on real PostgreSQL, so Hibernate, pgjdbc and server logs are checked too. The hosted demo deletes each booking, with its messages and idempotency keys, within the hour after its appointment ends, and its database backups expire after 7 days. In production the bank's records and privacy officers would set the period (docs/production-notes.md). In every deployment a cancel deletes the booking at once: only its cancellation SMS outlives it, until it is sent or has failed for good, beside two fingerprints kept for about 24 hours, the Idempotency-Key's unkeyed SHA-256 of the request (pseudonymised, not anonymous) and, where the daily cap is on, its HMAC-SHA256 of the number in sms_recipient_sends.

POPIA by construction: only name and mobile are stored (email only with FR-37); the mobile is masked or redacted outside the domain. No personal data appears in logs, problem bodies or fixtures beyond synthetic numbers, including database-side logs: pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false, Hibernate's SQL error log (org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error) OFF, and PostgreSQL started with log_error_verbosity=terse and log_min_error_statement=panic. LogRedactionIT reads the application's formatted log and PostgreSQL's container log. Data stays in the local container, or, on the hosted demo, in RDS for PostgreSQL in Cape Town (af-south-1), where each booking is deleted within the hour after its appointment ends and backups expire after 7 days.

Research notes on POPIA obligations
  • Lawful basis (s9, s11): booking an appointment is processing needed 'to carry out actions for the conclusion or performance of a contract to which the data subject is party' / at the data subject's request (s11(1)(b)), so NO consent checkbox is needed for the booking itself. Consent (s11(1)(a)) is only required for optional extras such as marketing. Do not gate the booking behind a 'I consent to everything' tick box - reviewers in a bank will notice.
  • Purpose specification (s13): collect 'for a specific, explicitly defined and lawful purpose' and make the data subject aware of it. State on the form: 'We use these details only to schedule, confirm, remind you of and manage this branch appointment.'
  • Minimality (s10): information must be 'adequate, relevant and not excessive'. Collect first name, last name, mobile (mandatory), email (optional, needed for the .ics), service, branch, slot. Make ID number optional ('helps the consultant prepare; required for FICA/card services'); never collect physical address, income, account numbers or date of birth separately (DOB is derivable from the ID).
  • Notification at collection (s18): before collection make the person aware of: what is collected, the responsible party's name and address, the purpose, whether fields are voluntary or mandatory and the consequence of not supplying them, recipients, the right of access/correction, the right to object (s11(3)), and the right to complain to the Information Regulator with its contact details. Implement as a short inline privacy notice + /privacy page, not a 40-page policy.
  • Retention (s14): records 'must not be retained any longer than is necessary for achieving the purpose'; once no longer needed they must be destroyed, deleted or de-identified 'in a manner that prevents its reconstruction in an intelligible form'. Rule: a scheduled job de-identifies client PII 90 days after the appointment's end (keeps branch/service/slot/status for statistics, nulls name, contact and ID fields, keeps only a salted hash for duplicate detection). Document the period in the README. Built instead, for the hosted demo only: each booking is deleted, with its messages and idempotency keys, within the hour after its appointment ends, and backups that could still hold it expire after 7 days. In every deployment a cancel deletes the booking at once, keeping only its cancellation SMS until it is sent or has failed for good. Nothing is de-identified, and a local copy keeps its data until make down.
  • Security safeguards (s19): 'appropriate, reasonable technical and organisational measures' to prevent loss, damage, unauthorised destruction, unlawful access or processing; identify foreseeable risks, establish safeguards, verify and update them, with 'due regard to generally accepted information security practices'. Concretely: TLS everywhere, ID number encrypted at rest (AES-256-GCM, key from env/KMS) with a separate HMAC index column for lookups, masked rendering (900101*******), PII excluded from logs and error messages, least-privilege DB role, rate limiting on lookup endpoints, dependency scanning in CI, no PII in test fixtures except the synthetic IDs.
  • Security compromise notification (s22): where there are reasonable grounds to believe PII was accessed or acquired by an unauthorised person, notify the Information Regulator and the affected data subjects 'as soon as reasonably possible' using the Regulator's prescribed form SCN1 (guidelines published 2022). Put a one-paragraph incident runbook in the README/ops docs.
  • Direct marketing by electronic communication (s69): prohibited unless the data subject consented or is an existing customer being marketed similar products with an opt-out at collection and in every message; each message must identify the sender and give an opt-out address. Transactional confirmations and reminders are service messages, not direct marketing, so they need no consent - but keep the optional 'send me offers' checkbox separate, unticked by default, and never reuse appointment contact details for marketing.
  • Data subject participation (s23-s24): the client may request access to and correction/deletion of their PII. Provide the self-service manage link (view, reschedule, cancel) and a 'delete my details now' action after the appointment is terminal; log the request as an AppointmentEvent.
  • Unique identifiers and special information (s26, s57): the ID number is not 'special personal information' but it is a unique identifier; do not use it as the only key to retrieve an appointment (require reference + last 4 digits of mobile or ID), do not link it to other data sets, and do not repurpose it.
  • Accountability and the regulator (s8, s55-s56, s74): the responsible party must register an Information Officer with the Information Regulator (inforegulator.org.za, which has issued enforcement notices for unregistered officers and accidental disclosures); complaints go to the Regulator under s74. In a demo, name a fictional responsible party (the design uses 'Fynbos Bank (fictional demo)') and its Information Officer contact in the privacy notice, never a real bank.
  • Cross-border transfers (s72): keep data in South Africa or document the transfer basis; in a demo, run the database locally/in-region (the hosted demo keeps its database in AWS af-south-1, Cape Town) and avoid third-party SMS/email SaaS - which is one more reason the 'simulated' gateway is the right interpretation. As built, it stays simulated locally and for the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 0199; since 27 October 2026 the hosted demo texts every other number through AWS End User Messaging SMS from af-south-1, and a protect configuration allows SMS to South Africa only. While AWS keeps the account in its SMS sandbox, only numbers verified on it receive the text; AWS refuses any other for good, so the message is FAILED on its first attempt and the booking still stands.

Time

timestamptz for every instant. branch.timezone converts wall-clock hours. A java.time.Clock bean is injected everywhere and application SQL binds :now from it, never calling now(). TZ=UTC in the JVM, containers and CI, with the API's unit and API tests run again under TZ=America/New_York. Response records format instants at the branch offset themselves rather than trusting a Jackson setting. Web tests run under two zones. The bookable window comes from the server, and AvailabilityApiIT.rendersStartsAtWithJohannesburgOffset pins the literal '+02:00'.

All instants are timestamptz. Wall-clock hours are converted through branch.timezone. java.time.Clock is injected, and application SQL binds :now from it (NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest), with DEFAULT now() only on audit columns. TZ=UTC in the JVM, images and CI, and CI runs the API's unit and API tests again under TZ=America/New_York. Response records emit +02:00 via ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone with no Jackson time-zone setting, and web tests pass under TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York.

BuildBuilt

Front end

Status. Built in M4 and M6: the five booking steps at /book with the choices in the URL, the confirmation page with the SMS's delivery status polled every 2 s and Book this service again, /manage, the simulated inbox, and the landing, privacy, not-found and error pages, to the mockups recorded in web/README.md under UI design, plus cancelling and Retry (Tier 2), built from the designed parts but not drawn yet; the booking, appointment, manage, inbox and privacy pages load on first use. 214 Vitest tests run in CI under TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York against a fake API in MSW, and Playwright drives the app in Chromium against the compose stack in CI, with axe and a CSP check. Not built: the Tier 3 email field; skeleton rows and offline detection were dropped (a recorded departure).

One SPA served from the same origin as the API. The landing page, the shell and the error and not-found pages are in the first download; every other page, with the libraries only it uses, is fetched the first time it is opened (lazy routes in web/src/routes.tsx). It has one linear customer journey (the /book stepper) plus manage, simulated inbox and privacy pages. Server state lives only in TanStack Query over the generated client. Wizard state lives in the URL, so back, forward and refresh all work. The only personal data the client holds is the details the customer typed, the Idempotency-Key with a hash of the request, and the last four digits of each appointment the tab booked or looked up, all in sessionStorage (the details and the key until the 201, the digits until the tab cancels the appointment). For an appointment it cancelled, the tab keeps only when it was cancelled and its branch, service and time, never the name or the number. The device clock and zone are never used for logic: the bookable date window comes from the server and every time is formatted in Africa/Johannesburg. Every async view has loading, empty, error and stale states. Every error code in docs/errors.md has copy in api/problemCopy.ts; IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED and IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED, which only a bug could cause because the app always sends a key and mints a new one when the request changes, get the generic 'Something went wrong on our side' with the correlation id to quote, and no conflict copy ever reveals another booking's reference or time. Layout is 390 px first, with 44 px targets, WCAG 2.2 AA text contrast and 3:1 edges on every enabled input, button, tile and pager (disabled ones are exempt under WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.4.11). The theme is the fictional Fynbos Bank, with a line on every page saying the bank is fictional; the UI never names a real bank.

RoutePurposeKey elements
/Landing page: what this is, two calls to action, demo disclosure.
  • The shell's banner ('Fynbos Bank is fictional. BranchBook is an engineering assessment, not a real bank's service. The hosted demo texts the number you give, so use only your own; the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 082 555 0199 are never texted. While AWS keeps the demo in its SMS sandbox, only numbers verified on its account receive the text; any other shows Not delivered, and the booking still stands.'), then the header: a leaf mark, 'BranchBook', 'by Fynbos Bank' and a Demo tag
  • h1 'Book a branch appointment', one sentence, then 'How it works' in three steps and 'Before you visit'
  • Primary Button 'Book an appointment' -> /book; secondary 'Manage an appointment' -> /manage
  • Footer links: Simulated inbox (/messages), Privacy (/privacy)
/book?branch=&service=&date=&slot=&step=The booking stepper. The current step is derived from the query params: no branch -> BranchStep; branch without service -> ServiceStep; service without step -> DateTimeStep (DateStrip + TimeGrid), where a chosen ?slot= is repeated above Continue, which sets step=details; step=details -> DetailsStep; step=review -> ReviewStep. A URL that skips ahead opens the first step still missing an answer. BookPage corrects a URL it cannot follow and says why in the API's copy: an unknown branch shows the branch list (BRANCH_NOT_FOUND), a service the branch does not offer shows its services (SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH), and a review without the details this tab saved shows the details step.
  • Stepper nav (aria-current='step') with step titles Branch, Service, Date and time, Your details, Review; at 390 px a Back link naming the previous step, 'Step 3 of 5' and five segments
  • BranchStep: cards from GET /api/v1/branches (name, suburb/city, province, 'Open Sundays' badge), a search over name, suburb and city with a live count, a loading line, and a ProblemAlert with Try again on error
  • ServiceStep: services from GET /api/v1/branches/{code} (name, durationMinutes, expandable 'What to bring'), plus a summary of the branch address and hours
  • DateStrip: the server's bookable window (bookableFrom..bookableTo, 30 dates) in 7-day pages. Closed days are disabled and captioned 'Closed' or 'Holiday'; a line under the strip and the date's accessible name give the reason from closedDates ('Public holiday: Local Government Elections'). Default date is the first open day in the window.
  • TimeGrid: GET /api/v1/branches/{code}/availability?service=&date= rendered in SAST, with the zone stated once in the group's legend, in Morning and Afternoon groups of four columns. Full slots show 'Full' and are disabled; one free seat shows a '1 left' badge. 'Times as of 10:41' comes from generatedAt, with a Refresh button. A closed day says 'The branch is closed on Wednesday 4 November' with the reason; a day with no free time offers a button to the next open date.
  • DetailsStep: firstName, lastName, mobile (email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships), an inline privacy notice linking to /privacy, Continue
  • ReviewStep: what, where, when (SAST, written out) and who, with an Edit link per step. 'Confirm appointment' Button (reads 'Confirming…' and is aria-disabled while pending). Idempotency-Key from useIdempotencyKey, kept in sessionStorage with a hash of the request.
  • Error surfaces: An alert on the review step (409 SLOT_FULL with alternatives[] buttons and a link to all times; Confirm stays disabled until a new time is chosen). DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT links to /manage and CUSTOMER_OVERLAP to the times; neither shows a reference or time. A 422 for the time or date stays on the review step with a link to choose another; a refused name or number goes back to the details step with the error on its field.
  • On 201: seed the appointment's cache, remember the last four digits for the reference in sessionStorage, clear the details and the key, navigate(/appointments/${reference}, {replace: true})
/appointments/:referencePersistent confirmation and self-service view of one appointment. Reached right after booking or after a successful lookup on /manage; the SMS links to /manage, not here.
  • Needs last4: read from sessionStorage, otherwise 'Open your appointment' asks once for the 'Last four digits of your cellphone number' for a fresh tab or a shared link. Calls GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference} with X-Mobile-Last4. 404 -> 'We couldn't find an appointment with those details.' with a link to /manage.
  • h1 'Your appointment is confirmed' (focused on mount); reference in large monospace with a Copy button
  • Summary card: service, branch name + address, 'Tuesday 27 October 2026' with '09:30 to 09:45 SAST' under it, What to bring list, 'Please arrive 10 minutes early.'
  • DeliveryStatus per notification: 'To 082 *** 0101' with a badge, 'Sending' -> 'Sent at 10:42' (polling every 2 s for as long as it is PENDING) -> or 'Not delivered' with 'We could not send the SMS after 5 attempts. Your booking still stands; keep your reference.'. Once sent, the message shows as a phone bubble that can be hidden. The 0101 mask appears here only because this caller booked or has just proved the number.
  • Actions: 'Book this service again' -> /book?branch=&service=, which opens the date-and-time step; 'Book another appointment' -> /book; 'See the SMS in the simulated inbox' -> /messages, unless the latest SMS failed, when its card links there instead; and, while the appointment is CONFIRMED and canCancel is true, 'Cancel appointment' (CancelSection, Tier 2), which opens an inline step to confirm
  • Cancelled state (Tier 2): the server deletes the appointment as it cancels it, so the page stops asking for it once the cancel is sent and gives way to a final view: h1 'This appointment was cancelled', 'Its details have been deleted. A cancellation SMS is on its way to 082 *** 0101.', and a card with the service, branch, date and time under a 'Cancelled' badge, when it was cancelled, in SAST, and that its time is free again, then Book this service again and Book another appointment; no reference, no delivery status and no Cancel. The tab remembers the cancellation without the name or the number (rememberedCancellation), so a refresh shows the same view, saying the SMS was sent to the number booked with; any other tab finds nothing
  • Past state (now >= startsAt): the API's canCancel is false, so Cancel is not offered, with no line saying why; a cancel sent as the appointment starts gets 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL, 'This appointment has already started. It can no longer be cancelled.', and the page fetches the appointment again
/manageFind an appointment by reference + last 4 digits. The SMS manage link lands here.
  • The form: reference input (any case, hyphens optional, sent as APT-XXXX-XXXX), last4 input (inputmode numeric), 'Find appointment' button. Shape is checked client-side first, so a malformed value never reaches the API (the server's 400 is not counted as a failed attempt).
  • 404 -> an alert above the form with the copy in docs/errors.md, 'We couldn't find an appointment with those details.' (the same for wrong digits); a line under the form says 'Can't find your SMS? The demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 082 555 0199 are never texted, so open your appointment here to read it, or see the simulated inbox on a local run.', with the inbox linked. 429 -> an alert with the copy in docs/errors.md, 'Too many attempts. Try again later.', saying lookups for that reference pause for up to an hour, even with the right digits; a 429 with Retry-After: 1, which means another lookup is still being checked, is retried once after a second.
  • 200 -> write {reference, last4} to sessionStorage, navigate to /appointments/:reference
/messagesSimulated inbox: every notification message the outbox still holds; a cancel deletes its booking's confirmations, and a cancellation goes once it has been sent or has failed for good. It is an unauthenticated demo operator view that exists only while branchbook.inbox.enabled is true: on under make up and make dev-api, off in production and on the hosted demo, where the page says the inbox is not available here.
  • Banner (plain text, not a live region): 'Demo operator view. It lists every booking's messages, recipients redacted, and is switched off in production. Here every message is simulated, as on any local run.'
  • GET /api/v1/messages?limit=50, polled every 2 s while any row is PENDING, otherwise not polled. Each message card shows its kind and channel, redacted recipient ('082 *** ****'), subject, body (SMS as a bubble), a PENDING/SENT/FAILED status badge, attempts, lastError, createdAt/sentAt in SAST, and the reference: a link to its appointment, or plain text where the server redacts it (APT-****-****, while branchbook.inbox.redact-references is on, and for a cancellation whose booking a cancel has deleted).
  • Each FAILED message the API marks retryable has a Retry button (Tier 2), which calls POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry. Its accessible name names the message, and its answer is said in a status region in the card, such as 'Queued to send again. Its status updates here.' Each stopped row gives its reason in plain words: 'Delivery failed after 5 attempts', 'Not sent: the appointment was cancelled first' or 'Not sent: the number reached its limit of messages for the day'. With no messages: 'No messages yet' and a button to book. Where the inbox is switched off, a plain 404: 'The simulated inbox is switched off on this deployment', with a link to /manage.
/privacyPOPIA s18 notice in plain language.
  • Responsible party 'Fynbos Bank (fictional demo)'; purpose 'to schedule, confirm and manage this appointment'; what is collected (three fields, none optional, never an ID number), with 'Use only your own cellphone number: the hosted demo texts it.' and that the demo numbers are never texted; who sends the SMS (on the hosted demo, AWS End User Messaging, in Cape Town, as the operator, at most five messages a day to one number, counted by a keyed fingerprint of the number, never the number, kept for about a day after each message); retention ('The hosted demo keeps a booking, with your name and number, until its appointment is over, and deletes it within the hour after it ends.', and backups that could still hold it expire after 7 days; a copy run locally keeps them until deleted; a booking the customer cancels is deleted at once, all but the cancellation SMS, kept until it has been sent or has failed for good; and a one-way fingerprint of the booking's details is kept for about a day, so the same booking sent twice is booked once); rights of access and correction; Information Regulator contact; no marketing
*Not found.
  • h1 'Page not found', links to / and /book

Fynbos Bank: a calm, plain-spoken retail-bank look, not a startup dashboard. The screens are drawn as mockups (phone first, at 390 px): the main Tier 1 screens and states, and a Foundations board, recorded in web/README.md under UI design. Tokens in web/src/ui/tokens.css: --colour-primary #1E6B52 (buttons, links, focus ring, the chosen date and time), --colour-primary-strong #175541 (hover; text on the primary tint), --colour-primary-tint #E6F1EC (Sent badge, What to bring), --colour-accent #B8405E (invalid-field borders and destructive actions only), --colour-accent-strong #9A2F4A (error text and icons, Failed badge; 6.2:1 on its tint, where the accent is only 4.5:1), --colour-accent-tint #F8E8EC (error alerts), --colour-warn #775C10 with tint #FBF3DC (Pending, 1 left, stale times, the banner and the Demo tag; 5.7:1, where the earlier #8A6D1C was 4.4:1), --colour-text #2B2B2B, --colour-text-muted #5B615C, --colour-surface #F6F7F5 (page), --colour-card #FFFFFF, --colour-border #DDE2DC (card edges, dividers and other decorative edges, 1.3:1), --colour-control #7D867F (input and pager borders, 3.5:1 on the page and 3.8:1 on white) and --colour-control-soft #87908A (time and date tiles, 3.1:1 on the page and 3.3:1 on white); radius 6 px; a soft shadow (0 1px 2px at 6%) on list cards, the review summary and the reference card only, and 0 -2px 8px at 6% cast upwards by the time step's action bar; spacing scale 4/8/12/16/24/32. Typography: Nunito Sans (400/600/700) via @fontsource, system-ui fallback; base 16 px, h1 28/32, h2 20/28, alert title 18/24, date 18/24, card heading 16/24, body 16/24, small 14/20, meta 13/18, badge 13/18, label 12/16, caption 11/14; the reference in a 26/32 monospace; times in tabular-nums. Layout: single column, max-width 640 px centred, 16 px side gutter; cards in a list 8 px apart and sections 16 px (24 px on the landing page); lists indented 24 px; the slot grid shows 4 columns at every width, in Morning and Afternoon groups, and the date strip 7; wider screens keep the one column, at most 640 px wide. Own wordmark: a small inline SVG leaf (#1E6B52 with a #4FA383 inner leaf), 'BranchBook' in 700 weight and a muted 'by Fynbos Bank', with a Demo tag in warn on its tint; no logo files; no real bank's brand colours; light theme only, no dark mode (recorded as a non-goal).

Components

ComponentResponsibility
Shell (web/src/ui/Shell.tsx)Skip link (shown on focus), the fictional-bank banner first, then the header with the wordmark and the Demo tag, the <main> landmark, and a footer with the inbox and privacy links; wraps every route.
Banner (in Shell)Persistent notice, first in every page as an aside labelled 'About this site': 'Fynbos Bank is fictional. BranchBook is an engineering assessment, not a real bank's service. The hosted demo texts the number you give, so use only your own; the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 082 555 0199 are never texted. While AWS keeps the demo in its SMS sandbox, only numbers verified on its account receive the text; any other shows Not delivered, and the booking still stands.' That last sentence goes once AWS takes the account out of the SMS sandbox. It names no real bank.
StepperRenders the five steps as segments with a Back link naming the previous step and 'Step 3 of 5', and marks aria-current; PageHeading moves focus to each step's heading and sets document.title.
BranchStepLists active branches from useBranches, filters client-side, writes ?branch= on select.
ServiceStepLists the services offered at the branch from useBranch(code), with expandable documents; writes ?service=.
DateStripRenders exactly the server's bookableFrom..bookableTo window in 7-day pages, using closedDates; closed days are disabled with their reason; writes ?date=. Never derives 'today' from the device.
TimeGrid (in DateTimeStep)A fieldset of native radio inputs from useAvailability, its legend naming the day and the zone, in Morning and Afternoon groups of four columns; SAST formatting; free, '1 left', selected and full states; generatedAt + Refresh; writes ?slot=.
DetailsStepreact-hook-form + zod details form (detailsForm.ts): checks each field when it is left by the API's rules, saves the details to sessionStorage, shows the server's field errors that the review step hands back, writes ?step=review.
Privacy notice (in DetailsStep)Two-line s18 notice linking to /privacy, rendered inside the details form (no checkbox).
ReviewStepSummary with edit links. Owns the useBookAppointment mutation, the persisted Idempotency-Key, and the 409/422/503 recovery routing.
SLOT_FULL alert (in ReviewStep)On the review step: 409 SLOT_FULL copy 'That time was just taken', with alternatives[] as buttons that set ?slot= and rotate the key, a link to all times, and the chosen time marked 'No longer free'; Confirm stays disabled until a new time is chosen.
ConfirmationPageReference with Copy, the confirmation SMS with its delivery status and text, the appointment summary, Book this service again, Book another appointment, the cancel step while the appointment can be cancelled, the final cancelled view that replaces all of it once the cancel succeeds (shown again after a refresh from what the tab remembers), and the last-four form for a tab that has not seen the appointment.
DeliveryStatusAn aria-live badge per notification: Sending, Sent at a time, Not delivered, or Not sent for a confirmation a cancel stopped, which is not a failure. useAppointment refetches every 2 s while any message is PENDING, with no cap; it never polls a cancelled appointment, and the confirmation page switches it off once a cancel is sent.
DigitsForm (in ConfirmationPage)Collects the last 4 digits when a fresh tab or shared link arrives without sessionStorage; stores them on success.
CancelSection (in ConfirmationPage)Tier 2. 'Cancel appointment', a secondary button in accent, opens an inline step, not a modal, that names the service, branch, date and time and says 'Cancelling releases the time for someone else to book. It cannot be undone.' Nothing is sent until 'Yes, cancel it'; 'Keep my appointment' and Escape close it. Calls useCancelAppointment with the digits the page was opened with. A refusal is worded by code inside the step, which stays open to try again; TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL replaces the step with its copy.
ManagePage + useLookupAppointmentReference + last4 form for /manage, with normalisation and the 404/429 copy. The lookup is a mutation, so a wrong try is never repeated by itself.
InboxPage / useInbox / useRetryMessageInbox list with conditional polling, status badges, redacted recipients, references as links or, where the server redacts them, plain text, reason text for failed, superseded or capped rows, a Retry button on each retryable message with its answer in a status region in the card, and empty and disabled states.
PrivacyPageStatic POPIA notice content.
ProblemAlertRenders an ApiProblem using problemCopy.ts copy, a network failure as 'We couldn't reach BranchBook'; the correlationId to quote for 5xx; an optional Try again.
ErrorPageThe router's error element, inside the shell: a page that fails to render, or whose code cannot be fetched (say after a new release replaced it), shows 'Something went wrong on our side' with 'Start a booking again', which reloads, and a link home; never shows stack traces.
ui primitives (Button, ButtonLink, Field, Alert, Badge, Card, PageHeading, PageLoading, LeafMark, icons)Token-driven, 44 px targets, visible focus rings, aria wiring built in; the only place where raw CSS classes for controls live. Button and ButtonLink take destructive, which draws them in accent, as on Cancel; PageLoading is the shell's 'Loading the page…' line.
api/client.tsThe openapi-fetch client typed by schema.d.ts, and unwrap, which returns a success's body or throws an ApiProblem (built in M2). It also reads Retry-After (seconds or an HTTP date) and Idempotent-Replayed.
api/queries.ts, api/inbox.tsuseBranches, useBranch, useAvailability, useAppointment, useBookAppointment and useCancelAppointment in queries.ts, and useInbox and useRetryMessage in inbox.ts, with the query keys and policies below; useLookupAppointment is in pages/ManagePage.lookup.ts.
api/problemCopy.tsCode-to-copy map for all 21 codes in docs/errors.md (the two Idempotency-Key codes get the generic copy with the correlation id), plus fieldErrors, which maps errors[] to form fields; a response without a code gets the generic copy, never the server detail. No entry interpolates a reference, time or number from another booking.
lib/time.tsIntl.DateTimeFormat with {timeZone: 'Africa/Johannesburg'}, read through formatToParts, and fixed English day and month names, behind formatDateLong, formatDateNoYear, formatDateShort, formatTime and formatTimeRange; calendar dates are handled as text, never as a Date in the device's zone. A range or a time in a sentence reads '09:30 to 09:45 SAST'; a time tile shows the bare time under a legend that names the zone; dates read 'Tuesday 27 October 2026', 'Tuesday 27 October' under the week's range, or 'Tue 27 Oct' where space is short.
lib/mobile.tsparseSaMobile, which gives the E.164 form to send and '082 555 0101' to show, and last4.
booking/detailsForm.tsThe details schema and normaliseName: trim, NFC, map U+2019/U+2018/U+02BC to an ASCII apostrophe, collapse internal whitespace (the same steps as the server's CustomerName).
booking/useIdempotencyKey.tsUUID kept with a 53-bit hash (cyrb53) of the booking request, as {fingerprint, key} in sessionStorage 'branchbook.idempotency', so the record holds no name or number. Survives a refresh, rotates when the payload changes, is reused on retry, and is cleared on 201.
booking/bookingSession.ts, lib/storage.tsTyped sessionStorage access for the details, each reference's last four digits, which a cancel forgets (forgetLookup), each appointment the tab cancelled ('branchbook.cancellations': when it was cancelled, the branch's and the service's codes and names, and the time, never a name or a number) and the key's record, over JSON helpers that wrap every call in try/catch.
lib/zodConfig.tsSets zod to parse without compiling (jitless), because the CSP refuses the new Function probe zod 4 makes and the browser reports each probe as a violation.

State and data

Server state: TanStack Query only, no global store. Keys and policies: ['branches'] staleTime 5 min. ['branches', code] staleTime 5 min; the response carries bookableFrom/bookableTo and closedDates, computed from the server Clock, which drive the DateStrip. ['availability', code, service, date] keeps the app's defaults, staleTime 30 s with no refetch on window focus or on a timer, so times do not change under the customer's finger; the time step offers Refresh, and a booking or a SLOT_FULL marks every availability stale. ['appointment', reference, last4] takes last4 from sessionStorage and sends it as the X-Mobile-Last4 header (in the in-memory cache key, never in the URL or any log); refetchInterval 2000 while any notification.status === 'PENDING' and the appointment is not CANCELLED, otherwise off; the query is switched off while a cancel is pending and once it has succeeded. ['messages'] staleTime 0 and refetchInterval 2000 while any row is PENDING, otherwise off. Mutations: useBookAppointment POSTs /api/v1/appointments with Idempotency-Key. Its onSuccess (fresh 201 or Idempotent-Replayed: true, treated the same) seeds ['appointment', reference, last4] with the body, remembers the digits in sessionStorage and marks every availability stale; the review step then clears the details and the key and navigates. useCancelAppointment (Tier 2) POSTs /api/v1/appointments/{reference}/cancel with X-Mobile-Last4. Its onSuccess removes every cached copy of the appointment, whatever digits opened it, since the server has deleted it, forgets the tab's digits for it (forgetLookup), remembers the cancellation without the name or the number (rememberCancellation), and marks every availability and the inbox stale; a TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL refetches the appointment. useRetryMessage (Tier 2) POSTs /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry, puts the answer in the cached inbox, then refetches the inbox. Retry policy: useBookAppointment retries once on 503 TRY_AGAIN after Retry-After, capped at 5 s, with the same key, which replays the 201 if the first attempt won; after a network error the customer's next tap sends the same key. It does not retry other HTTP responses. useCancelAppointment retries a 503 once the same way, since a 503 TRY_AGAIN wrote nothing; a cancel that succeeded is never sent again, as the server has deleted the appointment and a repeat would get 404. Queries retry once, only for a network failure or a 5xx, never a 4xx. Wizard state: URL query params branch, service, date, slot (the start as HH:MM in SAST) and step, derived rather than stored, so back, forward and refresh work. Draft details: saved to sessionStorage 'branchbook.details' when the details step is submitted, with the key in 'branchbook.idempotency' as {fingerprint, key}; both cleared on 201. A 409 round trip or a refresh after a timed-out POST therefore keeps both the customer's details and the same key, which is the commonest real duplicate. 'Book this service again', on every appointment, cancelled or not, prefills only branch and service (/book?branch=&service=, which opens the date-and-time step), because the draft was cleared at the original 201 and the server returns only masked data. The last four digits of each reference the tab booked or looked up live in sessionStorage 'branchbook.lookups' so refreshing the confirmation page works, until the tab cancels the appointment; a cancellation lives in 'branchbook.cancellations', so a refresh shows the cancelled view again. Types: web/src/api/schema.d.ts is generated by npm run generate:api (openapi-typescript docs/openapi.yaml); there are no hand-written response types, and npm run typecheck plus npm run check:api in CI keep it honest. client.ts (built in M2) turns an error response into ApiProblem {status, code, correlationId, problem}, taking the correlation id from the problem or the X-Correlation-Id response header. A body that is not a problem, such as a proxy's HTML page, gives an ApiProblem without a code, and a network failure rejects with the browser's own error, so a screen can tell offline from refused. ApiProblem also carries retryAfterSeconds from Retry-After. Time: every instant from the API is a string with +02:00 and is formatted only through lib/time.ts. The device zone and date are never used for logic.

Forms and validation

One zod schema per form, mirroring the server rules. Details: firstName/lastName run through normaliseName (trim, NFC, curly apostrophes U+2019/U+2018/U+02BC -> ', internal whitespace collapsed), then 1 to 60 characters matching /^\p{L}[\p{L}\p{M} '\-]*$/u. Mobile goes through parseSaMobile, which repeats the steps libphonenumber takes for South Africa in the API's MobileNumber.parse, ignoring spaces, hyphens, dots, brackets and '(0)', and accepts only an E.164 form matching /^\+27(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$/, failing with the server's copy 'We need a cellphone number to SMS your confirmation.'. Email (only if the Tier 3 email channel ships) is optional, trimmed, lower-cased, max 254, z.string().email(). Lookup: the reference is trimmed, upper-cased and stripped of hyphens/spaces, must match /^APT[A-HJKMNP-Z2-9]{8}$/, and is re-hyphenated before the request; last4 must match /^\d{4}$/. react-hook-form uses mode 'onTouched', reValidateMode 'onChange' and zodResolver; a lookup's button is disabled while it is pending; when a submit fails, react-hook-form focuses the first invalid field, whose error is linked to it. The server is authoritative. Response mapping: 400 VALIDATION_FAILED -> back to the details step, with errors[] mapped by fieldErrors (customer.firstName -> firstName) and applied with setError under an alert; 422 MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED -> setError('mobile'); 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED, only where a deployment takes only the demo numbers -> back to the details step with 'This demo accepts only 082 555 0000 to 082 555 0199, for example 082 555 0101.' on the cellphone field; 422 SLOT_OFF_GRID, SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS, SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY, SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME, DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW -> an alert on the review step with the code's copy and a link to choose another time (?slot and ?step removed) or, for a closed day or a date outside the window, another date; 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH -> service step with an alert; 409 SLOT_FULL -> the review step's alert with up to three alternatives[] on the same day (choosing one sets ?slot= and useIdempotencyKey rotates), details preserved; 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT -> 'You already have an upcoming appointment for this service.' with a link to /manage (no reference, branch or time); 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP -> 'You already have an appointment at that time.' with a link to choose another time; 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED -> the generic 'Something went wrong on our side' with the correlation id, since only a bug could cause it: the app mints a new key whenever the request changes (docs/errors.md); 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL -> the cancel step gives way to 'This appointment has already started. It can no longer be cancelled.', which takes focus, and the appointment is fetched again, so Cancel is no longer offered; 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND -> uniform copy from docs/errors.md ('We couldn't find an appointment with those details.'); 429 LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED -> 'Too many attempts. Try again later.', saying lookups for that reference pause for up to an hour, and a 429 with Retry-After: 1 retried once after a second; 503 TRY_AGAIN -> one automatic resubmission with the same key after Retry-After, capped at 5 s, then an alert with the correlation id, and Confirm sends the same key again; 404 MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND, from an inbox Retry only -> 'We couldn't find that message. Refresh the inbox and try again.' in that message's card; 500 INTERNAL_ERROR -> alert 'Something went wrong on our side' with correlationId. All copy lives in api/problemCopy.ts keyed by code; the UI never string-matches detail text.

Accessibility

  • Every control has a visible <label>; hints and errors are linked with aria-describedby; invalid fields set aria-invalid; a placeholder is never the only label
  • Stepper is <nav aria-label='Booking steps'> with aria-current='step'; on each step change focus moves to the step's h1 (tabIndex -1) and document.title becomes 'Step 3 of 5: Date and time - BranchBook'
  • TimeGrid is a <fieldset> whose <legend> reads 'Available times on Tuesday 27 October' with 'In South African Standard Time (SAST)'. Each slot is a native radio inside its label, so the browser gives one tab stop, the arrow keys and the announcement, with no roving tabindex to build. Full slots are disabled radios named '08:30, Full' and the last seat '08:45, 1 left'; the tile's visible words are aria-hidden, so 'Selected' is never read, because the radio is already announced as checked; the tile shows the focus ring of its radio.
  • DateStrip buttons carry aria-pressed and an aria-label such as 'Thursday 24 September, closed: public holiday, Heritage Day'; the week pager buttons are labelled 'Previous 7 days' / 'Next 7 days' and are aria-disabled at the edges of the server window, so they stay focusable
  • Live regions: the DeliveryStatus badge sits in a role='status' region present from the start; every error alert, ProblemAlert and the SLOT_FULL alert included, is role='alert'; the branch count, the stale-times banner, Copy and the answer to an inbox Retry, in its message's card, announce through status regions; inbox polling updates are not announced
  • Confirmation page: the h1 takes focus when the page is reached from within the app; the reference is set in monospace with a 'Copy reference' button that then reads 'Copied', while a status region says 'Reference copied.'
  • The cancel step (CancelSection, Tier 2) is a region named by its heading, 'Cancel this appointment?', which takes focus when the step opens; Escape and 'Keep my appointment' close it and give focus back to 'Cancel appointment'; while the cancel is out both buttons are aria-disabled rather than disabled, so the focused one keeps focus and a refusal is read from there; once cancelled, the page's h1 takes the focus. It is not a modal <dialog>, so nothing traps focus: jsdom, where the tests run, does not implement showModal()
  • Skip link to #main as the first focusable element; <html lang='en-ZA'>; header/main/footer landmarks; headings in order
  • Contrast verified against the tokens (WCAG 2.2): white on #1E6B52 6.4:1, #2B2B2B on #F6F7F5 13.2:1, #5B615C 5.1:1 or more wherever it is used, #B8405E on white 5.3:1, error text #9A2F4A 6.2:1 on its tint, #775C10 5.7:1 on its tint; inputs and pagers edged in #7D867F (3.5:1 on the page) and tiles in #87908A (3.1:1). Focus ring 2 px #1E6B52 with a 2 px offset, visible on every control. No information is conveyed by colour alone (status badges carry text).
  • Touch targets at least 44x44 px; no hover-only content; prefers-reduced-motion cuts animations, such as the Sending spinner, and transitions
  • Checks in CI: eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y-x (strict, errors not warnings), Testing Library tests that query by role or text, never by test id, and, with Playwright since M6, axe scans of the review step and the confirmation page in book-appointment.spec.ts, at 390 and 1280 px, that fail on any serious or critical WCAG 2.2 AA violation

UX details

  • Branch picker: cards with name, suburb and city, a province chip and an 'Open Sundays' badge; the search matches name, suburb or city, ignoring case and accents, with a live count; all six seeded branches fit on one screen at 390 px; selecting one advances automatically
  • Service picker: cards show the duration ('About 15 minutes') and an expandable 'What to bring' list, so the customer sees the documents before choosing a time; services the branch does not offer are not shown
  • Date strip: the 30 bookable dates the server reports, 7 per page, starting at bookableFrom. Closed days are greyed, captioned 'Closed' or 'Holiday', with the reason in a line under the strip; a day with no remaining slots (for example, late afternoon today) says there are no times left to book, or that every time is taken, and offers the next open date; today's tile says 'Today'; the first open day is preselected.
  • Slot grid: times in SAST, with the zone stated once in the group's legend ('In South African Standard Time (SAST)'); four columns in Morning and Afternoon groups; a 15/30/45-minute grid per service; 'Full' slots disabled; a '1 left' badge when seatsAvailable = 1; 'Times as of 10:41' with a Refresh button; no refetch on window focus or on a timer; after 10 minutes without a tap or key press, a 'Times may have changed. Refresh to see the latest.' banner with Refresh, inside a role=status region that is present, and empty, before the times go stale; choosing a time does not move on, Continue does
  • Details form: the mobile accepts any SA format and is shown as '082 555 0101' on the review step; names typed on an iPhone (curly apostrophes, for example D’Oliveira) are accepted; email, only if the Tier 3 email channel ships, is marked optional with 'We will also email the confirmation'; the privacy notice sits under the fields; there is no consent checkbox; the draft and its key survive refresh and 409 round trips
  • Review step: one card per fact, with 'Edit' links back to that step. 'Confirm appointment' reads 'Confirming…' and is aria-disabled while pending. A network retry, a 503 retry or a refresh-and-confirm reuses the same Idempotency-Key, so a double tap can never book twice.
  • 409 SLOT_FULL, on the review step: 'That time was just taken', up to three later times as buttons and a link to all times; availability is refetched, details are preserved, Confirm waits for a new time, and a new key is minted when a different slot is chosen
  • Confirmation page is a real screen, not a toast. It shows the reference (APT-XXXX-XXXX) large with Copy; service, branch, and date and time in the review's order, and what to bring; 'Please arrive 10 minutes early.'; a delivery badge that moves from 'Sending' to 'Sent at 10:42' within about 2 s; the rendered SMS text; links to the inbox, to book this service again and to book another appointment, and Cancel appointment until the appointment starts. A refresh keeps working through sessionStorage.
  • Failed delivery copy keeps the customer calm: 'We could not send the SMS after 5 attempts. Your booking still stands; keep your reference.' with a link to the simulated inbox, where, if the inbox is switched on, Retry can queue the message again. With 082 555 0000, which the simulated gateway always refuses, it appears after about 35 s, while the page is still polling. A refusal AWS gives for good, such as for a number its SMS sandbox has not verified, is FAILED on the first attempt, so the copy reads 'after 1 attempt' within a few seconds.
  • Cancel (Tier 2): 'Cancel appointment' opens an inline step that restates the service, branch, date and time and says 'Cancelling releases the time for someone else to book. It cannot be undone.', with 'Yes, cancel it' and 'Keep my appointment'. Success shows 'This appointment was cancelled' and 'Its details have been deleted.', with the masked number the cancellation SMS is on its way to, then the service, branch, date and time under a 'Cancelled' badge, when it was cancelled, in SAST, that the time is free again, and the two ways to book again; there is no reference, no delivery status and no second Cancel, because the server has deleted the appointment. From the start time Cancel is not offered; a cancel the API refuses as too late says 'This appointment has already started. It can no longer be cancelled.'
  • Manage: reference and last-4 form; wrong digits and an unknown reference show identical copy. The SMS manage link lands on /manage. /appointments/:reference asks for the last 4 digits only when sessionStorage is empty (a fresh tab or shared link).
  • Simulated inbox: newest first; an SMS is rendered as a phone bubble with the redacted recipient ('082 *** ****'), an email (Tier 3 only) with subject and body; status badges, attempts and last error are visible; a plain reason on failed, superseded or capped rows, and a Retry button on each message that may still be sent; a reference the server redacts is plain text, not a link; the banner, 'Demo operator view.', says it lists every booking's messages, recipients redacted, is switched off in production, and that every message there is simulated, as on any local run
  • Loading: a muted line such as 'Loading branches…' or 'Opening your appointment…', 'Loading the page…' in the shell after 300 ms while the code of a page opened from a link or a refresh is fetched, and 'Confirming…' on the Confirm button; skeleton rows that match the final layout are not built
  • Empty states: a closed day in the slot grid with its reason, 'No branch matches that search' for the filter, 'No messages yet' in the inbox
  • Error states: ProblemAlert with plain-language copy, Try again where asking again could help, and the correlationId for 5xx ('If it keeps happening, quote reference 6f1c…'). A network failure reads 'We couldn't reach BranchBook'; offline detection that pauses polling is not built.
  • Demo visibility: the fictional-bank banner on every page and a Demo tag in the header; the footer reads 'Fynbos Bank is fictional. Branch hours are representative, not official.' beside the inbox and privacy links; no real bank is named anywhere in the UI
  • CSP-safe by construction: CSS Modules and tokens only, no <style> injection, no inline scripts; fonts are self-hosted and never inlined as data: URIs, and zod parses jitless, since its eval probe would be reported as a violation; a real-browser pass over every screen at 390 and 1280 px, on the jar under the policy, found no violations. Since M6, every Playwright end-to-end test also fails on any securitypolicyviolation event, uncaught error or console error it did not declare

BuildBuilt

Back end

Status. Built: every package of the modular monolith, the eight migrations and the test support. M6 added cancellation, the retry, the demo data, the two meters and the switches for the demo-numbers rule and the hosted demo's retention job. Since then: the real SMS gateway, the per-recipient daily cap, and the retention job's new rule, which deletes each booking once its appointment is over; then a cancel that deletes the booking (V6), the cap's ledger of keyed fingerprints (V7) and its key, permanent SMS refusals with their codes logged, demo data skipped where SMS is real, and the lookup counts in PostgreSQL (V8), so every instance shares the limit. There are no Spring profiles: application.yml holds every setting, and each deployment sets environment variables. Not built: the email channel and the Tier 3 dispatch counter.

One modular monolith in api/ with top-level packages catalogue, scheduling, booking, notification and shared under za.co.fynbos.branchbook. Each module splits into api (controllers + request/response records), application (handlers that own transactions, plus ports and queries), domain (plain Java; JPA annotations allowed on the Appointment aggregate only; no org.springframework imports) and infrastructure (JdbcClient adapters, the one JPA repository, gateways and schedulers; the two meters sit beside what they count, BookingOutcomeMeter in booking/api and OutboxPendingGauge in notification/application). Dependency arrows: scheduling -> catalogue; booking -> catalogue, scheduling (booking implements scheduling's BookedIntervalsPort); notification -> booking (it implements booking's ConfirmationOutbox, AppointmentNotifications and NotificationEraser ports, asks booking's AppointmentDirectory for an appointment's reference, start and whether it is cancelled, takes booking's Confirmation and Cancellation values and masks the recipient with MobileNumber; it reads nothing from catalogue, because booking hands it the branch's and the service's SMS names); every module -> shared; shared -> nothing. DemoDataLoader lives in booking's infrastructure package, so the demo data needs no arrow of its own. There is no booking -> notification arrow. A reviewer enters the booking path through five files, AppointmentController -> BookAppointmentHandler -> Appointment/SlotCalendar -> AppointmentRepository -> NotificationOutbox. BookAppointmentHandler.handle runs its private book method, which reads top to bottom as ten numbered one-line steps; the adapters those steps reach (JdbcIdempotencyKeyStore, JdbcBookingLocks, JpaAppointmentRepository, ConstraintViolationTranslator) are listed in docs/architecture.md. The only asynchronous element is NotificationDispatcher polling the outbox. Everything else is synchronous REST under /api/v1 with RFC 9457 errors. Time rule: Java decides 'now' through the injected Clock and binds it as :now into SQL; no business statement calls the database's now().

Package layout

  • api/pom.xml, api/mvnw, api/.mvn/wrapper/maven-wrapper.properties. There is no api/Dockerfile: the single root Dockerfile builds web, then api.
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/BranchBookApplication.java (@SpringBootApplication, @EnableScheduling, @ConfigurationPropertiesScan)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/catalogue/api/BranchController.java (GET /api/v1/branches, GET /api/v1/branches/{code}), BranchListResponse.java, BranchDetailResponse.java (address, timezone, operatingHours[], services[], closedDates[], bookableFrom, bookableTo)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/catalogue/application/BranchQueries.java (activeBranches(); branchDetail(code), with closedDates and the bookable window [today, today + horizon) computed from the Clock), BranchCalendarProvider.java (BranchCalendar calendarFor(code) and calendarFor(Branch); offerFor(branch, serviceCode), which refuses a service the branch does not offer), BranchCalendar.java, in catalogue/domain (a final class over the Branch, its hours by DayOfWeek and the PublicHolidayCalendar: hoursOn, closure, closuresIn), ServiceOffer.java, in catalogue/domain (record: serviceId, code, smsName, durationMinutes, capacity; the SMS name travels with the offer, so the outbox needs no catalogue query while the seat lock is held), HolidayCoverageCheck.java (a WARN at start-up and every day at 06:00 SAST from 60 days before the booking window reaches the end of the holiday data), CatalogueLookup.java (branch(id) and service(id), active or not, so AppointmentViews can show an appointment's names, address, duration and documents; the templates get their SMS names from booking, not from here), CatalogueReadPort.java (port implemented in infrastructure)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/catalogue/domain/Address.java, Branch.java, OperatingHours.java, ServiceType.java, PublicHoliday.java, PublicHolidayCalendar.java, BranchCalendar.java, ClosedDay.java, BookingWindow.java (immutable records read through JdbcClient, not JPA entities, so ddl-auto=validate covers only appointments), PublicHolidayCalendar.java (pure: isHoliday(date) = stored OR Monday after a stored Sunday, s2(1)), Province.java (enum)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/catalogue/infrastructure/JdbcCatalogue.java (implements CatalogueReadPort: JdbcClient row mappers for branches, branch_operating_hours, services with documents_needed text[] read as java.sql.Array -> List<String>, branch_services and public_holidays; no JSON mapping at all)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/scheduling/api/AvailabilityController.java (GET /api/v1/branches/{code}/availability), AvailabilityResponse.java (with the nested SlotResponse; startsAt and endsAt are TimeFormat.isoOffset strings)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/scheduling/application/AvailabilityQueries.java (read-only: checks the branch, the service and the date in that order, loads the calendar, the offer and the booked intervals, and returns DayAvailability), BookedIntervalsPort.java (List<BookedInterval> activeBetween(branchId, serviceId, from, to); implemented by booking, so scheduling never reads booking's table), AlternativeSlots.java (M3: next three free slots on the same day for 409 SLOT_FULL, computed after rollback outside the booking transaction), DayAvailability (a record nested in AvailabilityQueries), DateOutsideBookingWindowException.java
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/scheduling/domain/SlotCalendar.java (window(); slotsOn(date, booked); validate(startsAt) -> Optional<SlotProblem> with fixed precedence DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW > SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY > SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS > SLOT_OFF_GRID > SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME; closed days come from BranchCalendar.closure), Slot.java (record startsAt, endsAt, seatsAvailable), BookedInterval.java (record seatNo, startsAt, endsAt; half-open overlaps(), as in the exclusion constraint), SlotProblem.java (enum SLOT_OFF_GRID, SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS, SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY, SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME, DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW), ClosedDay.Reason, in catalogue/domain (CLOSED_DAY, PUBLIC_HOLIDAY), BookingWindow.java, in catalogue/domain (record firstDate, lastDate; startingOn(today, horizon) gives 30 bookable dates). Scheduling has no infrastructure package.
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/booking/api/AppointmentController.java (POST /api/v1/appointments, GET /api/v1/appointments/{reference}, POST /api/v1/appointments/{reference}/cancel; headers Idempotency-Key, X-Mobile-Last4), BookAppointmentRequest.java (record with Bean Validation and the nested CustomerRequest; startsAt is a string checked by @IsoOffsetDateTime and the names by @PersonName, which report OFFSET_REQUIRED and NAME_INVALID), AppointmentResponse.java (AppointmentResponse.from(AppointmentView): MobileNumber.masked() for callers who just proved the number; times via TimeFormat), NotificationResponse.java (from NotificationSummary), BookingOutcomeMeter.java (M6: an interceptor that counts each POST /api/v1/appointments once its response is written, as appointments_booked_total by outcome, so it also sees the 400s Spring gives before the controller runs). There is no AppointmentResponseMapper; AppointmentResponse, its customer, NotificationResponse and CustomerRequest print a redacted toString()
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/booking/application/BookAppointmentHandler.java (handle(BookAppointmentCommand) runs the private book method inside one TransactionTemplate at READ COMMITTED, each step one line calling a named private method or a port: (1) IdempotencyKeyStore.reserve; (2) load active branch + ServiceOffer; (3) SlotCalendar.validate(startsAt), on a calendar built with now; (4) BookingLocks.lockCustomer(mobile); (5) DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT then CUSTOMER_OVERLAP pre-checks; (6) BookingLocks.lockSeats(branchId, serviceId) -> capacity; (7) lowest free seat or SLOT_FULL; (8) AppointmentRepository.insert (persist + flush); (9) ConfirmationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation (JdbcClient INSERT); (10) IdempotencyKeyStore.complete. One whole-transaction re-run on a reference collision, 40P01 or 40001: a second conflict is 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2, a second collision a 500; SLOT_FULL alternatives come from AlternativeSlots after the rollback, and the response is read back through AppointmentViews), BookAppointmentCommand.java, BookingResult.java (sealed: Created(AppointmentView) | Replayed(AppointmentView)), AppointmentViews.java (renders AppointmentView, the appointment with its branch, service and messages, read after the commit), Confirmation.java (what the SMS needs: id, reference, recipient, start, zone, and the branch's and the service's SMS names), ReferenceGenerator.java (port; SecureReferenceGenerator in infrastructure), BookingOutcome.java (M6: the counter's seven outcomes, from switches over every result and every ProblemCode with no default, so a new code does not compile until it has one), CancelAppointmentHandler.java (counts the try with the limiter in a transaction of its own, then, in one transaction: loads the appointment and checks the last four digits, Appointment.cancel(now) in memory, AppointmentRepository.lockForDeletion, enqueueCancellation, which deletes every confirmation and writes the cancellation SMS, the response rendered from memory, and AppointmentRepository.delete with the version checked; then it settles the count. A cancel that finds the row gone, deleted by another cancel or the purge, is 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, counted as a wrong try), LookupAppointmentHandler.java, LookupAttemptLimiter.java (counts in lookup_attempts (V8) through the LookupAttemptStore port, so every instance shares one limit and the counts survive a restart; each begin and end is a short REQUIRES_NEW transaction of its own that locks the reference's row; begin counts a lookup as in flight before the database read, end turns it into a failure for wrong digits or releases it, and never throws; five failures block the reference until an hour after the first wrong lookup, and a lookup refused only because failures and lookups in flight reach five gets Retry-After: 1; a lookup never settled stops counting once its 30 s lease runs out), LookupAttemptStore.java (port: lockWindow, insert, update, delete, purgeClosed), Window.java (record: one reference's window, its failures, its lookups in flight and their lease), AppointmentRepository.java (port: insert, findByReference, findLowestFreeSeat(branchId, serviceId, startsAt, endsAt, capacity), hasUpcomingActiveForService(mobile, serviceId, now), hasOverlappingActive(mobile, startsAt, endsAt)), BookingLocks.java (port: lockCustomer(MobileNumber) = pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(:mobileE164, 0)); int lockSeats(branchId, serviceId) = SELECT capacity ... FOR UPDATE), IdempotencyKeyStore.java (port: reserve(key, hash, now) -> Reservation{New, Replay(appointmentId), Cancelled, Reused}, Cancelled for a key whose booking a cancel has deleted; complete(key, appointmentId), which also stores status 201; purgeExpired(now)), ConfirmationOutbox.java (port: enqueueConfirmation(Confirmation); enqueueCancellation(Cancellation), built in M6, which from V6 deletes every confirmation of the appointment and returns the cancellation's NotificationSummary for the response), AppointmentNotifications.java (port: List<NotificationSummary> forAppointment(appointmentId)), NotificationSummary.java (record channel, kind, status, attempts, sentAt, failureReason, recipientMasked, body), no RequestHash.java: BookAppointmentCommand.requestHash() is the SHA-256 of branch, service, the startsAt instant, mobileE164, firstName and lastName after parsing, so the same details typed another way hash alike; and, from M6, Cancellation.java (what the cancellation SMS needs), AppointmentAccess.java (the limiter, the load and the constant-time digit check that lookup and cancel share, so a wrong cancel counts as a wrong lookup), RetentionPurge.java (deletes ended appointments with their messages and keys, in batches of up to 500, each one transaction that locks them with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED), RetentionProperties.java (branchbook.retention: enabled, after-end PT0S, never negative) and NotificationEraser.java (port: the purge deletes an appointment's messages through notification). AppointmentRepository gained lockEndedNoLaterThan and deleteAllById, and, since a cancel deletes the booking (V6), lockForDeletion and delete in place of M6's update; IdempotencyKeyStore gained deleteForAppointments
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/booking/domain/Appointment.java (@Entity, the only one, where a null @Version marks a new row, so it is persisted, never merged; @Version Integer version, null until the first persist; confirm(...) factory; cancel(now), which returns CancelOutcome (CANCELLED, ALREADY_CANCELLED or TOO_LATE); canCancel(now); computes endsAt), AppointmentStatus.java (enum CONFIRMED, CHECKED_IN, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_SHOW; canTransitionTo; ACTIVE set plus ACTIVE_SQL constant used by every native predicate), CustomerDetails.java (@Embeddable firstName, lastName, mobile E.164, email only with Tier 3), AppointmentReference.java (value object: generate(SecureRandom), parse(input), PATTERN; toString() returns APT-****-****, because a reference is half of what opens an appointment), MobileNumber.java (value object over libphonenumber ZA, accepting only the MOBILE type that also matches the storage regex ^\+27(6[0-9]|7[0-9]|8[1-5])[0-9]{7}$; e164(), display(), masked() '082 *** 0101', redacted() '082 *** ****', last4(); toString() returns redacted()), CustomerName.java (trim, NFC, U+2019/U+2018/U+02BC -> ASCII apostrophe, 1-60 of Unicode letters, combining marks, space, hyphen, apostrophe), IdempotencyKey.java (record), no BookingProblems.java: the refusals are separate classes in booking/application (SlotFullException(alternatives), DuplicateAppointmentException, CustomerOverlapException, SlotNotBookableException, MobileNumberRequiredException, IdempotencyKeyRequiredException, IdempotencyKeyReusedException, AppointmentNotFoundException, LookupRateLimitedException(retryAfter), all extending shared ProblemException, plus ReferenceCollisionException and TransactionConflictException, which trigger the re-run; TooLateToCancelException (M6) and DemoNumberRequiredException; none carries a reference or a time of another booking)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/booking/infrastructure/JpaAppointmentRepository.java (implements AppointmentRepository: EntityManager.persist + flush() inside ConstraintViolationTranslator; native generate_series(1,:capacity) NOT EXISTS lowest-free-seat query; upcoming and overlap pre-check queries; the cancel's lock and delete are plain SQL with the version checked, SELECT ... WHERE id = :id AND version = :version FOR UPDATE and DELETE ... WHERE id = :id AND version = :version, with the appointment detached first, so its cancelled state is never flushed), JdbcBookingLocks.java (implements BookingLocks), JdbcIdempotencyKeyStore.java (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the existing row has expired, RETURNING key, so an expired key is taken over in the same statement and a key is honoured for exactly 24 hours, purged or not; follow-up SELECT, which reads a completed key with no appointment, its booking cancelled and deleted, as Cancelled; UPDATE with appointment_id and 201; DELETE WHERE expires_at <= :now), JdbcBookedIntervals.java (implements scheduling's BookedIntervalsPort: read-only query over appointments WHERE status IN ACTIVE_SQL and the half-open overlap, plus starts_at > from - LONGEST_APPOINTMENT (120 minutes, the services_duration_grid limit), so it reads one day of ix_appointments_slot_lookup instead of every past active appointment (BR-25); on 32,400 rows a sequential scan of 681 buffers became an index range of 3; JdbcBookedIntervalsIT ties the constant to the schema's CHECK), ConstraintViolationTranslator.java (reads PSQLException.getServerErrorMessage().getConstraint() and getSQLState(), never the message text; maps to application exceptions (booking's, and catalogue's ServiceNotOfferedAtBranchException), and 40P01/40001 to TransactionConflictException, so handlers never depend on infrastructure; it logs one INFO line with only the SQLSTATE and, for a mapped constraint, its name), IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob.java (@Scheduled fixedDelay 1 h -> IdempotencyKeyStore.purgeExpired(clock.instant())), JdbcLookupAttemptStore.java (implements LookupAttemptStore: SELECT ... FOR UPDATE of a reference's row, INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING for two first lookups at once, every instant bound from the caller, and no ConstraintViolationTranslator, so a deadlock is a 503 rather than a 500), LookupAttemptPurgeJob.java (@Scheduled every hour from an hour after start-up -> LookupAttemptStore.purgeClosed(clock.instant()), which deletes closed windows, logging only the count), RetentionPurgeJob.java (present only while branchbook.retention.enabled is true; @Scheduled every hour from a minute after start-up -> RetentionPurge.purge(clock.instant()), logging only the three counts), DemoDataLoader.java and DemoDataProperties.java (where branchbook.demo-data.enabled is on, which only compose.yaml sets: once the application is ready, on a virtual thread of its own, books three fixtures through BookAppointmentHandler if appointments is empty, at the first free slot of the next open day at Bellville Kingsway and Umhlanga Ridge, never Polokwane or Sandton; synthetic numbers outside the demo block, the third ending 0000 so its SMS fails; one correlation id 'sys-<uuid>'; skipped with one INFO line where branchbook.booking.demo-numbers-only is on or branchbook.sms.gateway is aws, so no fixture ever sends a real SMS; logs and carries on after any error)
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/notification/api/InboxController.java (present only while branchbook.inbox.enabled is true, otherwise the path is a plain 404; GET /api/v1/messages?limit=, 1 to 200, default 50, newest first; POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry, built in M6; where branchbook.inbox.redact-references is true, both show every reference as APT-****-****), InboxResponse.java (recipientMasked = MobileNumber.redacted(), '082 *** ****', or the redacted email; a redacted toString())
  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/notification/application/NotificationOutbox.java (implements booking.application.ConfirmationOutbox. Renders the SMS from the Confirmation booking passes, which carries the branch's and the service's SMS names, so it reads nothing from catalogue, and persists a PENDING row in the caller's transaction (Propagation.MANDATORY, so it refuses to run outside one), tagged with the request's correlation id; SMS always, EMAIL only with the Tier 3 email channel. Where branchbook.sms.recipient-daily-cap is above 0, it applies the per-recipient daily cap: when the number, outside the demo block, already has that many rows in the cap's ledger, sms_recipient_sends, written in the last 24 h under its keyed fingerprint (RecipientFingerprints), the new message is FAILED with failure_reason RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP; otherwise it is written PENDING and the ledger gains a row in the same transaction, which outlives the message, so booking and cancelling in turn cannot pass the cap. enqueueCancellation first deletes every CONFIRMATION of that appointment, whatever its status, waiting for one the dispatcher is sending, then writes the cancellation; a capped cancellation is reported in the cancel's response and never stored), NotificationDispatcher.java (runOnce(Instant now): up to 20 due messages, each in its own TransactionTemplate that claims one row with SKIP LOCKED, sends it through MessageGateway, and marks it with an explicit UPDATE; backoff 2/4/8/16 s from the instant given, and FAILED with DELIVERY_FAILED on the fifth refusal, or on the first that the gateway calls permanent, with the refusal's code in last_error and in the log line; a gateway exception counts as a retryable refusal (GATEWAY_ERROR); a failed mark, or a gateway Error, is followed by a separate transaction setting FAILED with failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR and last_error 'MARK_FAILED'; from M6 it asks booking's AppointmentDirectory before sending a confirmation and marks one of a cancelled appointment SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION, which since V6 catches only an appointment cancelled before cancels deleted their bookings, or by hand; it deletes a cancellation, whose appointment is gone, once it is sent or has failed for good, instead of marking it; and it refreshes OutboxPendingGauge after each tick), MessageQueries.java (implements booking.application.AppointmentNotifications), RetryMessageHandler.java (FAILED with failure_reason DELIVERY_FAILED or DISPATCH_ERROR -> PENDING, attempts 0, next_attempt_at :now, last_error and failure_reason NULL; a no-op for PENDING, SENT, SUPERSEDED_BY_CANCELLATION and RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP rows; MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND for unknown ids), MessageGateway.java (port: Delivery send(OutgoingMessage), where the sealed Delivery is Accepted(providerMessageId, 1 to 64 characters) or Refused(errorCode, permanent), built with Refused.retryable or Refused.permanent), OutgoingMessage.java (id, channel, recipient, body), DispatcherProperties.java, InboxQueries.java (the inbox listing, naming appointments through booking's AppointmentDirectory); from M6, RetryPolicy.java (the one rule behind both the inbox's retryable flag and the retry), InboxProperties.java (branchbook.inbox: enabled, redact-references), MessageEraser.java (implements booking's NotificationEraser, Propagation.MANDATORY), OutboxPendingGauge.java (the outbox_pending gauge: one count per tick, NaN until the first), SmsProperties.java (branchbook.sms: gateway, recipient-daily-cap, recipient-cap-key, which its toString masks, and the aws settings, validated at start-up), and, since the cap's ledger (V7), RecipientFingerprints.java (HMAC-SHA256 of the number's E.164 form under branchbook.sms.recipient-cap-key, in lower-case hex;
