  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/notification/domain/NotificationMessage.java (outbox row as a plain domain object mapped by a JdbcClient row mapper, not a JPA entity: kind, channel, recipient, subject, body, status, attempts, nextAttemptAt, lastError (truncated to 500), failureReason, providerMessageId (truncated to 64), correlationId, createdAt, sentAt; no mark methods: the dispatcher marks a row through the repository's UPDATEs, markSent, markRetry, markFailed and markDispatchError; superseding is an UPDATE, and a capped row is created FAILED by NotificationMessage.cappedSms), NotificationStatus.java (PENDING, SENT, FAILED), NotificationKind.java (CONFIRMATION, CANCELLATION), NotificationChannel.java (SMS, EMAIL), NotificationTemplates.java (pure; DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(..., Locale.ENGLISH) at branch.timezone; confirmationSms(serviceSmsName, branchSmsName, startsAt, zone, reference) <= 160 GSM-7 with service sms_name, branch sms_name, SAST time, reference and the /manage link only; longestConfirmationSeptets(publicBaseUrl), the worst case the start-up check uses; cancellationSms, built in M6, with longestCancellationSeptets; the email variant is Tier 3 and not built), Gsm7.java (charset check and septet count), no SmsBudget.java: NotificationTemplates.longestConfirmationSeptets renders the worst case (18- and 20-character names, a two-digit day, a full reference and the base URL) from the template itself, no GatewayException.java (RetryPolicy, in notification/application, is the retry endpoint's rule, not the backoff): the backoff after failures 1-4 (2/4/8/16 s, FAILED on the fifth) is NotificationDispatcher.backoffAfter, and a gateway that throws counts as a refusal

Configuration

No Spring profiles. Every setting is in application.yml, and each place that runs the jar sets environment variables: make dev-api (./mvnw spring-boot:test-run, on a throwaway Testcontainers PostgreSQL) and the tests switch the inbox on; compose.yaml sets the database, PUBLIC_BASE_URL, BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_ENABLED=true and BRANCHBOOK_DEMO_DATA_ENABLED=true, with TZ=UTC in the image; the hosted demo's Fargate task sets DB_URL with sslmode=require, the database credentials from Secrets Manager, PUBLIC_BASE_URL, BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_ENABLED=false, BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES=true, BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED=true, BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws with the region, the protect configuration, BRANCHBOOK_SMS_RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP=5, the cap's key BRANCHBOOK_SMS_RECIPIENT_CAP_KEY from Secrets Manager and the dry-run switch, MANAGEMENT_ENDPOINTS_WEB_EXPOSURE_INCLUDE=health,info and SERVER_FORWARD_HEADERS_STRATEGY=native. It leaves BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY at its default, false, since it sends real SMS. The inbox is switched by the setting branchbook.inbox.enabled (BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_ENABLED), false by default and true in compose.yaml, make dev-api and the tests. Environment variables, with placeholders in .env.example and no defaults in application.yml: DB_URL (jdbc:postgresql://db:5432/branchbook in compose), DB_USER (branchbook), DB_PASSWORD (compose default 'local-dev-only-change-me', labelled dev-only; no real secret exists in the repo), APP_PORT (compose host port, default 8080, bound to 127.0.0.1), PUBLIC_BASE_URL (sets branchbook.notification.public-base-url; http://localhost:8080 by default; used in the SMS /manage link and validated at startup against the 160-septet budget, so at most 28 characters), BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_LEAD_TIME (PT2H), BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_HORIZON (P30D, half-open: 30 bookable dates), BRANCHBOOK_DISPATCHER_ENABLED (true; the interval and the simulated latency are in application.yml), BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_ENABLED (false, as on the hosted demo; true in compose.yaml), BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES (false), BRANCHBOOK_DEMO_DATA_ENABLED (false; true in compose.yaml), BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY (false), BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED (false) and BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_AFTER_END (PT0S), BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY (simulated), BRANCHBOOK_SMS_RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP (0, off), BRANCHBOOK_SMS_RECIPIENT_CAP_KEY (empty: a random key at start-up, so the cap's counts restart with the application; at least 32 characters when set), the BRANCHBOOK_SMS_AWS_* settings (region, protect configuration, configuration set, origination identity, max price 0.20 and dry run, read only for the aws gateway), TZ=UTC (Dockerfile ENV; the web tests set TZ per step). Fixed properties in application.yml: spring.threads.virtual.enabled=true; spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=validate (covers appointments, the only JPA-mapped table); spring.jpa.open-in-view=false; spring.flyway.fail-on-missing-locations=true; spring.jpa.properties.hibernate.jdbc.time_zone=UTC; spring.datasource.hikari.connection-timeout=3000, data-source-properties.options="-c lock_timeout=2000 -c statement_timeout=5000", data-source-properties.logServerErrorDetail=false (pgjdbc keeps server DETAIL lines, which carry key values such as a mobile, out of exception messages; the constraint name stays available on ServerErrorMessage); spring.jackson.deserialization.fail-on-unknown-properties=true (property name verified against Boot 4 / Jackson 3 on scaffold day and guarded by AppointmentApiIT.postWithUnknownJsonFieldReturns400ValidationFailed); instants are serialised by TimeFormat as strings, so no Jackson date/time-zone property is relied on; server.shutdown=graceful; spring.lifecycle.timeout-per-shutdown-phase=20s; logging.pattern.correlation, which prefixes each line written during a request with its correlation id; logging.level.org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error=OFF (Hibernate 7's category for its SQL error log); logging.level.software.amazon.awssdk=WARN, so the AWS SDK never logs a request, which carries a number and a reference; management.endpoints.web.exposure.include=health,info,prometheus; management.endpoint.health.probes.enabled=true; management.endpoint.health.show-details left at never in every environment; springdoc: /v3/api-docs in OpenAPI 3.1 and Swagger UI on in every environment, paths-to-match /api/**, writer-with-order-by-keys=true and override-with-generic-response=false, so the document is the same on every run and each operation lists only its own errors. Tests: there is no test profile, so the ITs run with the production lock_timeout 2000 and statement_timeout 5000. The race tests run with the production 2 s too, after a warm-up booking; only a booking held by BookingPause raises its own lock_timeout, to 10 s, and BookAppointmentIT.lockTimeoutReturns503TryAgainWithRetryAfter pins the 503 mapping. @IntegrationTest sets branchbook.dispatcher.enabled=false (tests call runOnce with the instant they want) and branchbook.inbox.enabled=true. Typed properties come from BookingProperties, NotificationProperties, DispatcherProperties, SmsProperties, InboxProperties, RetentionProperties and DemoDataProperties (@ConfigurationProperties; a malformed max price or base URL, or a negative cap or after-end, stops start-up, and so does a cap key under 32 characters, which RecipientFingerprints refuses without quoting it). Secrets: none in images or git; .gitignore (first commit) excludes .env, target/, node_modules/, web/dist, api/src/main/resources/static and playwright-report/.

Error handling

One @RestControllerAdvice (shared.problem.ProblemDetailHandler extends ResponseEntityExceptionHandler), with ErrorDispatchController for failures outside Spring MVC, produces application/problem+json {type: urn:branchbook:problem:<CODE>, title, status, detail (plain language, no PII, no class names, no SQL), instance (urn:branchbook:request:<correlationId>, never the path), code, correlationId, errors[{field, code, message}]?, alternatives[{startsAt, endsAt}]?}, with Cache-Control no-store and Retry-After on 429/503. No problem body ever contains an appointment reference, a start time or a recipient belonging to another booking. Mapping: MethodArgumentNotValidException / HandlerMethodValidationException / ConstraintViolationException (params) -> 400 VALIDATION_FAILED with one errors[] entry per field (path customer.mobile etc.); HttpMessageNotReadableException (malformed JSON, or an unknown property, reported as UNKNOWN_FIELD) -> 400 VALIDATION_FAILED; a startsAt that is not an ISO-8601 date and time with an offset fails @IsoOffsetDateTime (OFFSET_REQUIRED) and an invalid name @PersonName (NAME_INVALID); missing or non-UUID Idempotency-Key -> 400 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REQUIRED; X-Mobile-Last4 missing or not 4 digits -> 400 VALIDATION_FAILED (field X-Mobile-Last4), not counted by the limiter because a shape check reveals nothing; wrong digits -> 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND; a reference that does not match the pattern after normalisation -> 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND without touching the database; MobileNumber parse failure -> 422 MOBILE_NUMBER_REQUIRED errors[{field: customer.mobile}]; a South African mobile outside 082 555 0000 to 0199 while branchbook.booking.demo-numbers-only is on -> 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED errors[{field: customer.mobile}], before anything is written; CustomerName invalid -> 400 VALIDATION_FAILED; BranchNotFound -> 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND; missing branch_services row, or an unknown or inactive service code -> 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH; SlotProblem from SlotCalendar.validate (exactly one code, by the fixed precedence) -> 422 DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW | SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY | SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS | SLOT_OFF_GRID | SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME. Customer and seat rules are decided under the locks in a fixed order, so the code never depends on which index PostgreSQL checks first: an upcoming active appointment for the same mobile and service (ends_at > :now) -> 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT ('You already have an upcoming appointment for this service. Use the reference in your SMS to manage it.'); an active appointment for the same mobile overlapping [startsAt, endsAt) -> 409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP (generic detail, no time or branch); no free seat -> 409 SLOT_FULL with alternatives[] (up to three, computed by AlternativeSlots after rollback). IdempotencyKeyStore.Reused -> 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED, and so is Cancelled, a key whose booking a cancel has deleted, with a fixed detail saying so, so a retry never books it again. Replay -> 201 with the current AppointmentResponse re-rendered from appointment_id, the original Location and header Idempotent-Replayed: true. Cancel at/after startsAt -> 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL. LookupAttemptLimiter exceeded -> 429 LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED + Retry-After seconds (what is left of the hour from the first wrong lookup, or 1 when refused only because failures and lookups in flight reach five). Unknown message id on /messages/{id}/retry, including a cancellation deleted once final, -> 404 MESSAGE_NOT_FOUND; retry of a PENDING, SENT, superseded or capped row, or of a FAILED confirmation of an appointment that is cancelled or has started -> 200 no-op returning the row unchanged; a cancel whose appointment the retention purge deleted -> 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, never a 500. Database: a violation surfaces at the flush that follows persist (step 8) or at the JdbcClient statement itself (steps 9 and 10), inside ConstraintViolationTranslator, which reads ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and getSQLState() (23505 unique, 23P01 exclusion, 23503 foreign key, 23514 check), never the message text. Mapping: appointments_seat_no_overlap -> SlotFullException (409 SLOT_FULL; a backstop that should never fire while the row lock is held); appointments_customer_no_overlap -> CustomerOverlapException (409 CUSTOMER_OVERLAP; a backstop behind the pre-check); appointments_reference_key -> ReferenceCollisionException, on which the handler re-runs the whole transaction once, then 500 INTERNAL_ERROR; appointments_branch_service_fkey -> 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH; any other name passes through untranslated -> 500 INTERNAL_ERROR, logged at ERROR with the exception's types and stack frames but never its messages; a mapped name logs one INFO line with only the constraint and SQLSTATE. ConstraintNamesIT proves every mapped name exists. Concurrency and availability: 40P01 deadlock_detected and 40001 serialization_failure -> one automatic re-run of the transaction, then 503 TRY_AGAIN; CannotAcquireLockException / 55P03 (lock_timeout) / QueryTimeoutException / 57014 (statement_timeout) / CannotGetJdbcConnectionException / CannotCreateTransactionException (Hikari 3 s inside TransactionTemplate) -> 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2 (Retry-After: 5 for the two pool timeouts) and metric outcome try_again. Cancel loads the Appointment without a lock (started -> 409 TOO_LATE_TO_CANCEL; already CANCELLED, which only one cancelled before cancels deleted their bookings, or by hand, can be -> 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND), locks its row with the version checked, and deletes it with the version checked after writing the cancellation SMS; a concurrent second cancel, or one whose appointment the purge deleted after it was loaded, finds the row gone (OptimisticLockingFailureException) and answers 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND, counted as a wrong try, as does any later cancel or lookup of the reference. Spring's own 404/405/406/415 for unknown API routes keep the standard ProblemDetail plus correlationId and neither code nor type (not part of the catalogue, by design; the Problem schema marks both optional). Everything else -> 500 INTERNAL_ERROR, detail 'Something went wrong on our side. Quote the correlation id if you contact us.', logged at ERROR with the stack trace server-side only; with logServerErrorDetail=false that trace carries no key values. Never serialised: stack traces, exception messages, submitted input. docs/errors.md lists every code with its status, when it happens and what the UI shows; the document is kept in step with the ProblemCode enum by ProblemCodeCatalogueTest.

Logging

Spring Boot's plain console pattern everywhere, with no JSON format configured; logging.pattern.correlation prefixes each line written during a request with [correlationId]. MDC holds one key, correlationId, set by CorrelationIdFilter and cleared in a finally block; it never carries a reference, a mobile, a mask of one, or an email. Log lines name an appointment by its id, never by its reference, which is half of what opens it. Named events, built in M3: booking.confirmed {appointmentId, branch, service, seat}, booking.replayed {appointmentId}, booking.refused {code}, booking.rerun {cause}, booking.conflict {sqlState}, booking.constraint {sqlState, constraint}, outbox.enqueued {messageId, kind, channel} and idempotency.purged {count} at INFO, and lookup.blocked {failures, until} at WARN, which names no reference. Built in M5, each on the message's own correlation id: outbox.sent {messageId, channel, attempts} and outbox.retry {messageId, attempts, retryInSeconds} at INFO, outbox.failed {messageId, attempts} at WARN, and outbox.dispatch_error {messageId, cause} at ERROR. Built in M6 and with real SMS: cancel.refused {code}; outbox.superseded {messageId} from the dispatcher; outbox.capped {messageId, kind, channel, cap} at WARN; outbox.retry_reset and outbox.retry_unchanged {messageId, ...}; retention.purged {appointments, messages, keys}, with retention.batch_failed at WARN; demo_data.loaded, demo_data.skipped and, at WARN, the fixture failures by branch and service. Since a cancel deletes the booking: cancel.deleted {appointmentId} and cancel.gone {cause}, in place of cancel.cancelled, cancel.already_cancelled and cancel.rerun; outbox.confirmations_deleted {appointmentId, count} from the cancel, in place of its outbox.superseded; outbox.deleted {messageId, kind} for a cancellation deleted once final; and outbox.capped with stored=false for a capped cancellation. Since then: outbox.retry and outbox.failed also give code, the refusal's fixed code, such as AWS_THROTTLED, never a provider's message; demo_data.skipped gives reason=real_sms_gateway where the gateway is aws; lookup.count_not_settled {cause} at WARN; and lookup_attempts.purged {count} and cap_ledger.purged {count}, one line per hourly purge. ERROR only for unexpected exceptions (types and stack frames, never messages) and dispatcher pass failures. The refusal line carries the problem's code; the BookingOutcome labels are the tags of the appointments_booked_total counter. Never logged: request or response bodies, names, email, any form of the mobile, an appointment reference (the simulated gateway logs nothing; the dispatcher names a message by its id), last-4 digits, Hibernate SQL (show-sql off), Tomcat access log (off). The database layer is closed off too: pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false keeps DETAIL lines (for example 'Key (customer_mobile, ...)=(+27...)') out of exception messages; org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error, the category Hibernate 7 logs failed statements under, is OFF, so Hibernate does not re-log driver messages; ConstraintViolationTranslator logs only the constraint name and SQLSTATE; PostgreSQL itself runs with -c log_error_verbosity=terse -c log_min_error_statement=panic in compose and in Testcontainers, so docker compose logs db holds no DETAIL lines and no statements. LogRedactionIT, an ordinary integration test on real PostgreSQL, captures the application's formatted log while one synthetic customer is booked, replayed, refused (key reused, 400, landline, off the grid, both customer 409s, SLOT_FULL), looked up right and wrong until 429, stopped by the exclusion constraint and failed with a forced 500, and LogRedactionIT.cancelAndRetryLogsContainNoCustomerNameMobileOrReference does the same for a cancel and a retry. It fails if the customer's name, email, number in any form, masked or not, or the appointment's reference appears, and it runs the same check over the PostgreSQL container's log; both logs must show the events happened. Hibernate and HikariCP at WARN; org.springframework.web at INFO; springdoc at WARN.

Health and operations

Actuator exposes only: /actuator/health (status only: no component details in any environment, so there is no outbox indicator; the inbox and docs/runbook.md show the outbox), /actuator/health/liveness and /actuator/health/readiness (probes enabled; readiness includes the db check), /actuator/info (build version and time), and /actuator/prometheus with Spring Boot's meters and the application's two, appointments_booked_total and outbox_pending (M6); the hosted demo's task exposes only health and info. Everything else (env, beans, mappings, heapdump, loggers) is not exposed. Docker: the root Dockerfile has three stages. (1) node:24.22.1-alpine runs npm ci with a BuildKit cache mount for the npm cache and builds web/dist. (2) eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jdk-alpine with the Maven wrapper copies pom.xml and runs ./mvnw -B -ntp -DskipTests package with a --mount=type=cache for ~/.m2, then a layered extract, with dist copied to src/main/resources/static; tests run in CI, never inside docker build, where there is no Docker for Testcontainers and no docs/. (3) eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jre-alpine runtime, USER 10001, ENV TZ=UTC, ENTRYPOINT java -XX:MaxRAMPercentage=75 -XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError, HEALTHCHECK --interval=5s --timeout=3s --start-period=90s --retries=3 CMD wget -q -O /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:${PORT:-8080}/readyz || exit 1. Every base tag is checked with docker manifest inspect for linux/arm64 on scaffold day; if alpine aarch64 is missing, fall back to the Ubuntu eclipse-temurin:25-jre (or 21-jre) tag with curl in the HEALTHCHECK. compose.yaml: db postgres:17.11-alpine with command ['postgres', '-c', 'log_error_verbosity=terse', '-c', 'log_min_error_statement=panic'], a pg_isready healthcheck and no published port; app depends_on db condition service_healthy, ports '127.0.0.1:${APP_PORT:-8080}:8080', env from .env. Neither the demo database nor the unauthenticated inbox is reachable from the local network. make up = docker compose up --build --detach --wait --wait-timeout 300, then prints the URL. Graceful shutdown: server.shutdown=graceful with a 20 s phase, so in-flight bookings commit; the dispatcher's own scheduler, owned by DispatcherLoop, has waitForTasksToCompleteOnShutdown=true and awaitTermination 10 s, so the current one-row pass finishes, or its lock is released by rollback and at most that one message is re-sent. Schedulers: DispatcherLoop runs NotificationDispatcher.runOnce with a 2 s fixed delay on its own thread outbox-1 (never the request pool or the default scheduler); IdempotencyKeyPurgeJob hourly, LookupAttemptPurgeJob hourly from an hour after start-up, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJob hourly from a minute after start-up, RetentionPurgeJob hourly from a minute after start-up where retention is switched on, and HolidayCoverageCheck daily at 06:00 SAST, all on the default scheduler (scheduling is switched on in BranchBookApplication). Ops actions (docs/runbook.md): list PENDING/FAILED messages (inbox page or SQL); replay one (Retry in the inbox, POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry, where the inbox is on; elsewhere the guarded UPDATE in the runbook: UPDATE notification_messages SET status='PENDING', attempts=0, next_attempt_at=now(), last_error=NULL, failure_reason=NULL WHERE id=... AND status='FAILED' AND failure_reason IN ('DELIVERY_FAILED','DISPATCH_ERROR') AND NOT (kind='CONFIRMATION' AND appointment_id IN (SELECT id FROM appointments WHERE status='CANCELLED' OR starts_at <= now())); now() is acceptable in an operator's ad-hoc SQL); trace a correlation id (docker compose logs app | grep <id>); check the backlog (the inbox or the runbook's SQL; health shows no details; under make up, the outbox_pending gauge on /actuator/prometheus); reset the demo (make down && make up; the loader re-seeds); force a failed delivery (book with any number ending 0000, which reaches FAILED in about 35 s). Makefile targets: up, down, logs, psql, smoke, demo-409, dev-api, dev-web, test, test-api, test-web, test-docs, contract, fmt, hooks and secrets-scan; make help lists them.

BuildPartly built

Security and observability

Status. Built: the security headers with the Swagger UI exception, the stateless configuration, terse PostgreSQL logging, the lookup limiter, redacting value types, LogHygieneIT and LogRedactionIT, the web app under the strict CSP (M4), and from M6 the Playwright checks for CSP violations and console errors, cancel and retry with their log proof, inbox references hidden where a deployment asks, and two Prometheus metrics whose scrape holds no personal data; after M6, real SMS to South Africa only with a cap of five a day per number, and each hosted booking deleted once its appointment is over, behind WAF (/actuator blocked after decoding, 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes, AWS managed rules), HTTPS, Secrets Manager, a TLS-only database, 7-day logs and three alarms; since then, every cancelled booking deleted at once (V6), the daily cap counted by keyed fingerprints of the numbers (V7), with the key in Secrets Manager on the hosted demo, and the lookup counts shared by every instance in PostgreSQL (V8). Not built: a one-time code or sign-in, the Tier 3 dispatch counter, tracing, an outbox alarm, and a metrics scrape on the hosted demo, whose task serves only health and info.

ConcernMitigation
Browser-side injection and framing of a bank-themed pageSecurityConfig sets Content-Security-Policy "default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'" on the SPA and the API. Self-hosted Nunito Sans and Vite's hashed bundles satisfy it: there are no inline scripts and no style-injecting CSS-in-JS. /swagger-ui/** alone gets style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline', with the reason written beside it. Also set: X-Content-Type-Options nosniff, Referrer-Policy no-referrer, Permissions-Policy camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=(), payment=(), usb=(); X-Frame-Options DENY, and Cache-Control no-store on /api/**; over HTTPS, as on the hosted demo, Spring Security's default Strict-Transport-Security as well. SecurityHeadersIT asserts each header on an API response and an SPA response, and swaggerUiAloneMayUseInlineStyles; scripts/smoke.sh checks that Swagger UI is served; A real-browser pass over every screen at 390 and 1280 px, on the jar under this policy, reports no violation; zod parses jitless (web/src/lib/zodConfig.ts), because its new Function probe would be reported as one. The Playwright tests (M6) fail on any securitypolicyviolation event, uncaught error or console error they did not declare, at 390 and 1280 px; none opens Swagger UI.
Cross-origin access to the APINo CORS configuration exists. The SPA is served by the same jar at the same origin and Vite's dev server proxies /api, so browsers block foreign-origin reads by default. SecurityHeadersIT.corsPreflightFromForeignOriginIsNotAllowed proves a preflight gets no Access-Control-Allow-Origin. The README states this under Security notes.
CSRFDisabled, with the reason in SecurityConfig: SessionCreationPolicy.STATELESS, no cookies, no sessions and no Basic auth, so a cross-site request carries no ambient credential. Every mutation requires a JSON body plus custom headers (Idempotency-Key, X-Mobile-Last4), which a simple cross-site form cannot send.
Malformed or oversized input reaching domain or SQLBean Validation on request records (lengths, patterns, startsAt required as an ISO-8601 string with an offset, checked by @IsoOffsetDateTime); strict JSON (unknown properties -> 400); There is no in-app body-size limit in v1 (the request-body size filter is cut in the delivery plan; the gateway/WAF owns body limits in production). Idempotency-Key must be a UUID. The reference regex is applied after normalisation, before any query. X-Mobile-Last4 must be exactly 4 digits (400, not counted). X-Correlation-Id is limited to [A-Za-z0-9-]{1,64} or replaced. limit is clamped to 1-200; date must be ISO and inside the server's half-open window. Value objects (MobileNumber, which requires the libphonenumber MOBILE type and the storage regex; CustomerName, NFC-normalised; AppointmentReference) reject anything the edge missed, and the V2 CHECK constraints are the last line.
Brute force of the second factor and lookup abuseLookupAttemptLimiter (per reference, 5 failures per hour, counted in PostgreSQL in lookup_attempts from V8, whose closed windows an hourly job deletes, so spraying unknown references leaves about an hour of rows) counts each lookup as in flight before the database read, so lookups that arrive together cannot all pass the check (LookupAttemptLimiterIT.lookupsArrivingTogetherAdmitOnlyAsManyAsTheLimitLeaves), and returns 429 LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED with Retry-After once five wrong lookups fall in the hour from the first (Retry-After: 1 when it refuses only because failures and lookups in flight reach five), for GET from M3 and for cancel from M6, whose wrong digits count towards the same five. The counts are shared by every instance, as the hosted demo's two tasks need, and kept across a restart (LookupAttemptLimiterIT.countsAreSharedBetweenInstances); a lookup in flight holds its place for a 30-second lease at most, so one never settled stops counting after that. last4 is compared with MessageDigest.isEqual. Malformed headers are 400 and not counted, because a shape check reveals nothing. Documented residual risk: five wrong attempts lock the real customer out of that one reference for an hour, which requires the attacker to know the reference. Documented as a demo-scale control that gateway/WAF limits replace in production. Tests: getWithWrongLast4Returns404NotForbidden and sixthWrongLast4WithinHourReturns429.
Unauthenticated SMS sending: SMS pumping (toll fraud), harassment of a third party's number, slot squatting with invented numbersBuilt after M6, as Tier 3 (BR-17): NotificationOutbox enforces a per-recipient daily cap (branchbook.sms.recipient-daily-cap, off by default and 5 on the hosted demo, per rolling 24 h, counted in the ledger sms_recipient_sends (V7) by an HMAC-SHA256 fingerprint of the number, never the number, exactly under the booking's per-mobile advisory lock and softly on the cancel path). Cancellations count towards it, and so do the messages a cancel or the dispatcher has deleted, for their 24 hours; capped messages do not, and the demo block is never capped. A confirmation over the cap is stored as FAILED with failure_reason RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP and a cancellation over it is reported so but never stored; neither is handed to a gateway, while the booking or cancel still stands, so delivery never decides appointment status. Book-cancel-book loops then stop producing SMS. The per-mobile rules bound live rows per number, but invented valid numbers are free, so slot squatting is stated honestly as unsolved in v1. Where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws, as on the hosted demo since 27 October 2026, the confirmation and the cancellation are real SMS through AWS End User Messaging SMS in af-south-1, and the demo block, 082 555 0000 to 0199, stays simulated. The hosted demo adds a protect configuration that lets AWS text South Africa only and blocks every voice and MMS destination, WAF's limit of 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes, a maximum price of US$0.20 a message, AWS's monthly SMS spend limit, whose refusal, like the sandbox's, fails each message at once rather than after five attempts, and, while the account is in the SMS sandbox, delivery to verified numbers only. The task takes the fingerprint key from Secrets Manager (branchbook/sms-cap-key), so a restart or the second task keeps every count. The residual risk: anyone can make the app text any South African number, up to the cap a day. Production answer, written in ADR-0004 and docs/production-notes.md and deliberately not built: OTP verification of the mobile before an appointment becomes CONFIRMED (or booking only inside the authenticated banking app), per-IP and per-number-prefix gateway limits, premium-prefix blocking, provider-side geo and fraud controls, a CAPTCHA on POST, SMS cost alerting, and slot-hold limits per verified identity. Tests: RecipientDailyCapIT.theSmsPastTheCapIsWrittenFailedAndTheBookingStillSucceeds, RecipientCapLedgerIT.bookingAndCancellingInTurnReachesTheCapThoughEveryMessageIsDeleted and RoutingSmsGatewayTest.theDemoBlockIsAlwaysSimulated.
Booking endpoint abuse (floods, duplicates)A required Idempotency-Key (400 without it) absorbs retries. The per-mobile rules (one upcoming active appointment per service, checked under the advisory lock; no overlapping active appointments via appointments_customer_no_overlap) bound how many live rows one number can create. MobileNumber must be a valid ZA mobile; seat capacity bounds writes per slot; statement_timeout and lock_timeout cap resource use. Per-IP throttling is explicitly a gateway concern, not an in-app one: on the hosted demo, AWS WAF allows 10 POSTs under /api/v1/appointments, bookings and cancels together, per IP in 5 minutes, then applies AWS managed rules.
Over-collection and exposure of personal information (POPIA s10, s19)Only first name, last name and mobile are collected (plus an optional email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships); no ID number, address or date of birth. The mobile is stored only as E.164. It is rendered as 082 *** 0101 only to a caller who has just proved the number (the POST 201, and GET/cancel after the last-4 check), as 082 *** **** in the inbox, and not at all in logs, MDC, metrics or health. Names, numbers and references never reach any log line, including Hibernate, pgjdbc and the PostgreSQL server log (logServerErrorDetail=false, Hibernate's org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error log OFF, terse server logging; log lines name appointments by id; LogRedactionIT checks the application's formatted log and the container log). ProblemDetail never echoes input. idempotency_keys stores no response body, so no second copy of the name exists; it holds an unkeyed SHA-256 of the request's details, which someone who guesses them could confirm, so a key is pseudonymised, not anonymous. Test fixtures use synthetic numbers. There is an inline s18 notice and a /privacy page, no marketing field, and a retention schedule for production in docs/production-notes.md. Where BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED is set, as on the hosted demo only, an hourly job deletes each appointment within the hour after it ends, with its messages and idempotency keys (RetentionPurgeIT.anAppointmentGoesOnceItHasEndedNotBefore); the database's backups, which could still hold it, expire after 7 days, and /privacy says both. Everywhere, a cancel deletes its booking at once, with the name, the number and every confirmation SMS (BR-14); only the cancellation SMS, until it is sent or has failed for good, and the idempotency key, for the rest of its 24 hours, outlive it, with no appointment, and /privacy names both (CancelAppointmentIT.cancelReturnsTheCancelledAppointmentWithOnlyItsCancellationAndDeletesIt). Where the daily cap is on, its ledger holds a keyed fingerprint of the number, never the number, for 24 hours; the key is at least 32 characters and never logged (RecipientCapLedgerIT.theLedgerHoldsOnlyTheNumbersKeyedFingerprint). Where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws, the number and the text of each SMS go to AWS End User Messaging SMS in af-south-1, and a refusal is stored as a fixed code, never AWS's message (AwsSmsGatewayTest.eachRefusalBecomesItsFixedCodeAndNeverQuotesTheMessage). Accepting only the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 0199 (BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY, 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED) is built, and off on the hosted demo, which texts the number given.
Reading or cancelling someone else's appointmentAccess needs possession of the reference (31^8 space from SecureRandom) plus the last 4 digits of the booking mobile, sent in X-Mobile-Last4 on GET and cancel. The digits never appear in URLs, logs, MDC, problem bodies or the inbox, which shows '082 *** ****'. The SPA keeps them in sessionStorage only. No login by design (ADR-0004), with the OIDC production path documented.
Reference enumeration and oracle responsesAn unknown reference, a malformed reference, wrong digits and a cancelled reference, whose appointment the cancel deleted, all return the same 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND body, so a cancelled booking cannot be told from one never made, and the digits are compared in constant time (MessageDigest.isEqual). Never 403. The limiter counts every wrong lookup of a well-formed reference, known or not; a malformed one is refused before the database is read and never counted, because it can never match. Booking conflicts are not an oracle for references: 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT and CUSTOMER_OVERLAP carry no reference, branch or start time (AppointmentApiIT.noProblemBodyEverContainsAnAppointmentReference), so knowing someone's cellphone number never yields their reference. Accepted residual, recorded in ADR-0004: those two 409s still reveal that some booking exists for a known number; production closes this with OTP or OIDC-verified identity.
Vulnerable or drifting dependencies and imagesExact versions everywhere (Boot BOM pinned, package-lock.json with npm ci, postgres:17.x-alpine exact tag, Actions by major tag). Every pinned component is inside its support window on submission day. Dependabot runs weekly for maven, npm, docker and github-actions, with weekly, grouped version-update PRs for every ecosystem; security alerts stay on. npm audit --audit-level=high is informational in the web job. A Trivy image scan is optional, only if everything is green at hour 30 (it is a toolchain addition). No latest tags anywhere.
Container privilege and runtime surfaceRuntime image eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jre-alpine with USER 10001, no build tools, TZ=UTC and a HEALTHCHECK on readiness. Compose runs the app with no privileged flags. Both published ports are bound to 127.0.0.1 (only the app, on ${APP_PORT:-8080}; the database publishes no port), so on shared Wi-Fi neither the demo database with its dev password nor the unauthenticated inbox is reachable from other machines.
Secrets in git, images or logs.gitignore and .env.example are in commit one. Compose uses ${DB_PASSWORD:-local-dev-only-change-me}, labelled dev-only. No API keys exist: the simulated gateways need none, and the AWS gateway uses the ECS task role. On the hosted demo, AWS generates the database credentials into Secrets Manager (branchbook/database), and the SMS daily cap's HMAC key, 48 letters and digits, into branchbook/sms-cap-key, and injects both into the task. The demo asks for no sign-in, so it has no other credential. The submission-day sweep runs git log -p | grep -iE 'password|secret|token|key=' and the README lists the result. The actuator env endpoint is not exposed.
SQL injectionAll data access goes through JdbcClient named binds and JPA (persist, find, and one JPQL query with a named parameter), including the bound :now; no SQL is built from input (only constants such as AppointmentStatus.ACTIVE_SQL are formatted into it). The translator reads constraint names from ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and compares them with constants; nothing is executed from them. Reference and codes are regex-validated before any query.
Header, template and message injectionCustomerName allows only Unicode letters, combining marks, space, hyphen and apostrophe (after NFC normalisation and apostrophe mapping), so no CR/LF or control characters reach the SMS/email templates. Names appear only in the email greeting; the SMS carries no customer field. Subjects and bodies are built by NotificationTemplates from seeded catalogue strings and validated values, with Locale.ENGLISH, and the Gsm7 check rejects non-GSM-7 output. The Location header is built from the validated reference. X-Correlation-Id is echoed only when it matches the allow-list pattern. No .ics is generated (out of scope), so there is no iCalendar injection surface.
Demo-only and management surfaces leaking to productionGET /api/v1/messages exists only while branchbook.inbox.enabled is true: off by default, on in compose.yaml, make dev-api and the tests, and off, as on the hosted demo, it is a plain 404 (InboxSwitchedOffIT, which starts a second Spring context with it off). The POST retry, built in M6, exists only with it and can only make a failed message due again (InboxSwitchedOffIT.retryIsAPlain404WhenSwitchedOff). The README and the page banner label the inbox as an unauthenticated demo operator view that lists every booking's messages and does not exist in production; recipients are redacted there, and where BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES is set, as on the hosted demo should its inbox be switched on, every reference shows as APT-****-**** (InboxReferencesHiddenIT). DemoDataLoader runs only where BRANCHBOOK_DEMO_DATA_ENABLED is set, which only compose.yaml does, and books nothing where only the demo numbers may book or where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY is aws, so its fixtures never send a real SMS; Swagger UI and /v3/api-docs are on in every environment on purpose: the API is public, and the document describes nothing that is not already in the repository. Actuator is limited to health, info and prometheus (SecurityHeadersIT.operationalEndpointsBeyondHealthInfoAndPrometheusAreClosed), and health shows no component details (ApplicationStartsIT.healthRevealsNoComponentDetails); on the hosted demo the task serves only health and info, and WAF blocks /actuator outright (403), after URL-decoding and lower-casing the path.

Observability

AspectApproach
Correlation idCorrelationIdFilter accepts a well-formed X-Correlation-Id or generates a UUID. It stores the id in MDC, echoes it on every response header and inside every ProblemDetail (correlationId), and persists it on notification_messages.correlation_id; the dispatcher sets it on MDC per row. A message written outside a request (by DemoDataLoader) is tagged 'sys-<uuid>' by NotificationOutbox, so the NOT NULL column always has a value; the hourly key purge writes no message and logs without an id. The SPA sends no id of its own: the server generates one per request and returns it, so docker compose logs app | grep <id> shows the booking and its message delivery as one story.
Structured logsSpring Boot's plain-text format, the same everywhere, with the correlation id in every line written during a request (logging.pattern.correlation). Each line written during a request carries correlationId, the only MDC key; lines name an appointment by its id, never by its reference. Named events built in M3: booking.confirmed, booking.replayed, booking.refused{code}, booking.rerun, booking.conflict, booking.constraint, outbox.enqueued, idempotency.purged and lookup.blocked (a WARN that names no reference). Built in M5: outbox.sent, outbox.retry, outbox.failed and outbox.dispatch_error, each naming the message by id under the message's own correlation id; after M6, outbox.retry and outbox.failed also give the refusal's fixed code. Built in M6 and after: cancel.deleted, cancel.gone and cancel.refused; outbox.confirmations_deleted, outbox.deleted, outbox.superseded, outbox.capped, outbox.retry_reset and outbox.retry_unchanged; lookup.count_not_settled; and retention.purged, cap_ledger.purged and lookup_attempts.purged, which log counts only. The metric's outcome labels come from the BookingOutcome enum. No bodies, names, mobiles in any form, references or last-4 digits, and the database layer cannot re-introduce them (logServerErrorDetail=false, Hibernate's org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error log OFF, terse PostgreSQL logging); LogRedactionIT checks the application's formatted log and the container log.
Metrics (Micrometer -> /actuator/prometheus)Two custom metrics, the Tier 2 pair, built in M6: appointments_booked_total{outcome=confirmed|replayed|slot_full|duplicate|customer_overlap|validation|try_again} (labels from BookingOutcome, counted once the response is written, every series present from start-up at 0) and the outbox_pending gauge (refreshed once per dispatcher tick from one COUNT query, NaN until the first tick). The Tier 3 notifications_dispatched_total is not built. MetricsIT.scrapeHoldsNoPersonalData checks the scrape for the name, the number and the reference. On the hosted demo no one reads them: its task serves only health and info. Boot's defaults cover the rest: http_server_requests_seconds, hikaricp_connections_* and jvm_*; the dispatcher's thread, outbox-1, is private to DispatcherLoop, not a scheduler bean. There is no outbox health indicator: the simulated inbox and the runbook SQL show failed and pending messages; v1's six other custom metrics are cut for budget.
Health and readiness/actuator/health has the db indicator and no outbox indicator: health shows no component details, so the simulated inbox and docs/runbook.md show the outbox (ADR-0003 Notes). Holiday coverage is not a health detail: HolidayCoverageCheck logs a WARN at start-up and every day at 06:00 SAST from 60 days before the booking window outruns the holiday data. A production alert would trigger on the age of the oldest PENDING message, on FAILED messages or on that WARN. The hosted demo has three CloudWatch alarms, published to an SNS topic: 5 or more 5xx answers in 5 minutes, no healthy task for 3 minutes, and less than 2 GB of database storage left; its application logs go to CloudWatch (/branchbook/app, kept 7 days), and PostgreSQL's own log is not exported. Liveness and readiness groups are enabled. Readiness (db) is the Docker HEALTHCHECK, the compose --wait gate used by make up and the CI stack job, and the hosted demo's load-balancer health check (/readyz). Health shows no component details over HTTP (ApplicationStartsIT.healthRevealsNoComponentDetails).
Outbox lag and failure visibilityThe same facts appear in three places: the gauge and counter above; the simulated inbox (/messages), showing status, attempts, nextAttemptAt, lastError, the Retry button and the superseded/capped reasons; and the live delivery badge on the confirmation page. A recipient ending 0000 reaches FAILED in about 35 s (five attempts with 2+4+8+16 s of backoff between them, plus the 2 s ticks), so the reviewer can watch the attempts climb in the inbox and in docker compose logs app while the confirmation page, which polls every 2 s until no message is PENDING, turns to Not delivered. Because the dispatcher commits each row on its own, a crash or a database error re-sends at most the one message in flight.
Database and timeout behaviourHikariCP connectionTimeout 3 s, lock_timeout 2 s and statement_timeout 5 s turn a stuck lock or a down database into a fast 503 TRY_AGAIN (metric outcome try_again, WARN log with correlationId) instead of a hung request. 40P01/40001 get one automatic re-run before a 503. HikariCP pool metrics expose saturation: under load, bookings for other branches never wait on the branch_services lock, but they can wait up to 3 s for a pool connection. There is no IT profile: the ITs run with these production timeouts, ProblemDetailHandlerTest.transientDatabaseFailureBecomes503WithRetryAfterTwo pins the 503 mapping, and the M5 race tests run under them too: only the test-only BookingPause trigger raises lock_timeout, to 10 s, for the booking it holds. Tomcat graceful shutdown lets in-flight transactions commit.
Build and version identity/actuator/info exposes the build version and time (build-info). On the hosted demo WAF blocks it, with the rest of /actuator. The pipeline's deploy job deploys the hosted demo from main and, once the live smoke test passes, records that commit in the SSM parameter /branchbook/release and reads it back.
What the reviewer sees in docker logsdocker compose logs -f app shows, in order: Flyway applying V1-V8; Tomcat on 8080; DemoDataLoader booking three fixtures at Bellville and Umhlanga (booking.confirmed x3 with appointment ids, never references, correlation ids sys-...); the dispatcher sending two and retrying the 0000 recipient (outbox.retry attempts=1..4, then outbox.failed attempts=5); then one booking.* line per request with its correlationId. No line contains a name or any form of a mobile number. docker compose logs db shows only terse server lines. The runbook shows the grep commands and a sample line for each event.
Runbookdocs/runbook.md covers: the health checks (/livez, /readyz, make smoke and make demo-409); what happened to a message, from the inbox or SQL, with each state and failure reason (082 555 0000 always fails); replaying a FAILED message, through the retry endpoint where the inbox is on and with guarded SQL where it is off (both clear last_error and failure_reason, and refuse a confirmation of a cancelled or started appointment); what the AWS refusal codes mean, and letting the hosted demo send real SMS; what a cancel leaves behind; the daily cap's ledger; whether a reference is blocked; whether the retention purge ran; reading the metrics; and following one correlation id through the logs.
Front-end diagnosticsProblemAlert shows the correlationId to quote for a failure on our side, a 5xx included (problemCopy.ts). The app writes nothing to the console and has no telemetry vendor. The Playwright tests (M6) fail on console errors they did not declare, uncaught errors and CSP violations, and a failed CI run keeps the Playwright report, traces and compose logs as artifacts, so a flaky e2e run is diagnosable from them.

BuildPartly built

Testing

Status. Built: every unit, integration, contract and architecture test through M5, and from M6 CancelAppointmentIT, RetryMessageApiIT, InboxReferencesHiddenIT, DemoDataLoaderIT, MetricsIT, RetentionPurgeIT, DemoNumbersOnlyIT, RecipientDailyCapIT and the AWS gateway's unit tests; since then LookupAttemptLimiterIT, RecipientCapLedgerIT, RecipientFingerprintsTest, BookAppointmentHandlerTest and the two new purge jobs' tests (LookupAttemptPurgeJobTest and LookupAttemptPurgeJobIT, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJobTest and RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJobIT); 214 Vitest tests in 31 files under TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York; and four Playwright specs with axe at 390 and 1280 px, run in CI against the compose stack between two smoke runs. Not built: coverage in the CI job summary, and the Tier 3 slot-taken spec.

One named test per domain rule and per error code, placed at the lowest layer that can prove it. Test names read as requirements and are registered in docs/domain-rules.md (BR -> test); ProblemCodeCatalogueTest pins docs/errors.md; scripts/check-test-refs.sh fails CI if a rule marked proven, or a README row not marked planned, cites a Class.method that does not exist. Pure unit tests (no Spring, no Docker) cover SlotCalendar, PublicHolidayCalendar, the value objects, the state machine, templates, the SMS budget and the gateway's result types; the retry schedule is proven against the database in NotificationDispatcherIT. Testcontainers PostgreSQL 17 covers everything the database guarantees: the race, the bypass, idempotency, outbox atomicity, dispatcher behaviour, constraint names, the lookup limit's counts shared between instances and the daily cap's ledger. It runs as one @ServiceConnection container bean, shared through one cached context (@IntegrationTest), with a MutableClock bean, support/ResetClockExtension puts it back at 2026-10-26T06:00:00Z before every IT, and support/DatabaseReset truncates the three transactional tables, the daily cap's ledger and the lookup counts, and forgets scripted references before and after each test of a class that books through the API, so no IT depends on run order. Tests named 'concurrent' prove real interleaving with latches and support/BookingPause (a test-only trigger holds a booking at step 10 on an advisory lock, and pg_stat_activity shows the waits), not by luck of timing. The rest: MockMvc contract tests for status codes and ProblemDetail shape per endpoint; ArchUnit for structure; OpenAPI drift checks on both sides; Vitest/RTL/MSW component tests run twice (TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York); Playwright journeys against the running stack, built in M6: booking and cancelling at 390 px and 1280 px, booking by keyboard and a failed delivery at 1280 px, with axe on the review step and the confirmation page, failing on any CSP violation or undeclared console error; scripts/smoke.sh and scripts/demo-409.sh as shell-level proof. Every script and spec can be rerun, and each keeps to its own branches (DemoDataLoader BEL/UMH, smoke SAN, demo-409 POL). DemoDataLoader books only on an empty database, and scripts/smoke.sh writes nothing. scripts/demo-409.sh races twenty demo numbers from 082 555 0100 to 0199, starting at a random one. The specs pick a random demo number from 082 555 0100 to 0199 (the failed-delivery spec always uses 082 555 0000) and a random branch, service, date and free time from the API, and book again with another on a 409. No getter tests, no all-mocked service tests, no coverage gate. Surefire runs *Test without Docker in under 20 s (./mvnw test). LogRedactionIT and the contract check OpenApiContractIT are ordinary ITs, and SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest guards the split: no *Test may start the application or a container, and every test that starts the application must end in IT. Failsafe runs *IT (make test = test-api + test-web + test-docs). CI and make test-api then run the unit tests and the *ApiIT tests again under TZ=America/New_York, so slots cannot depend on the JVM's zone. make e2e runs the Playwright tests against the running app.

Coverage stance. Never gated. The design put JaCoCo and Vitest summaries in the CI job summary, quoted in the README; that was not built. Today ./mvnw verify writes JaCoCo's report (api/target/site/jacoco/index.html), and nothing else reports coverage. Expected: about 85% line coverage on the booking, scheduling and notification domain and application packages; about 75% for api/ overall (configuration and infrastructure adapters excluded from the expectation); about 80% statements in web/src/booking, web/src/pages and web/src/lib. The binding target is a complete rule-to-test table: every BR, every SlotProblem code (and their precedence), every ProblemCode and every mapped constraint name has at least one named test, enforced by scripts/check-test-refs.sh.

Backend unit

JUnit Jupiter 6 (Boot BOM) + AssertJ; Surefire *Test; no Spring context, no Docker; MutableClock for 'now'. Mockito stubs ports in a few unit tests, where an answer or a failure must come on cue: LookupAppointmentHandlerTest, so the database can fail and a failed read is shown not to be counted; CancelAppointmentHandlerTest and BookAppointmentHandlerTest, for a cancel that finds its row gone and a replay whose booking a cancel deleted before it was read; HolidayCoverageCheckTest, OutboxPendingGaugeTest and the three purge jobs' tests; the ITs exercise handle() end to end.

SlotCalendar: grid, hours, holidays, lead time, horizon (half-open, 30 dates), seats, validate precedence and JVM-zone independence. PublicHolidayCalendar s2(1) rule with dated cases. AppointmentStatus transitions (parameterised). Appointment.cancel guard. MobileNumber six-format matrix, rejections (with the libphonenumber type asserted for each rejected example) and libphonenumber's ZA example mobile against the storage regex. AppointmentReference alphabet and parsing. CustomerName NFC, curly apostrophes and combining marks. NotificationTemplates GSM-7/160-char, SAST and English-locale rendering. The NotificationProperties start-up check, which takes the worst case from NotificationTemplates.longestConfirmationSeptets (there is no SmsBudget). MessageGateway results: a provider id must fit its 64-character column, and a long error code is cut to fit. LookupAttemptLimiter over an in-memory store: a settle that fails never throws and logs neither the reference nor the message, and a try that could not be settled holds its place only for its 30 s lease; the window, lookups in flight, a burst arriving together and stale lookups are proven on PostgreSQL in LookupAttemptLimiterIT. RecipientFingerprints: the HMAC of the E.164 number under the key, a key under 32 characters refused without being quoted, and a blank key's random key with one WARN while the cap is on. BookAppointmentHandlerTest: a replay whose appointment a cancel deletes before it is read is refused as cancelled. The two new purge jobs' schedules and their one log line (LookupAttemptPurgeJobTest, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJobTest). HolidayCoverageCheck warnings at 60 days and past the data. Structural guards: NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest (scans every string literal and text block in the main sources for now(), CURRENT_TIMESTAMP and the other clock functions, and proves the scan on a sample), SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest. The inbox is switched by a setting, not a profile, and InboxSwitchedOffIT proves it.

Backend integration (real PostgreSQL)

JUnit Jupiter 6 + Spring Boot test + Testcontainers 2 postgres:17-alpine (@ServiceConnection, one static container in support/TestcontainersConfiguration, terse server logging), Failsafe *IT, no Spring profile (every IT, the race tests included, uses the production lock_timeout 2000; @IntegrationTest switches the dispatcher's loop off and the inbox on), DatabaseReset before and after each booking test, MutableClock reset before every IT, ScriptedReferenceGenerator, BookingPause; race tests send their requests through MockMvc from twenty threads, so the transactions, locks and connection pool are the real ones and the HTTP layer is not what is under test

The race at capacity 1 (Polokwane Savannah) and capacity 3 (Sandton Summit FICA): one warm-up booking first, then 20 threads with 20 distinct synthetic mobiles released by a CountDownLatch, asserting exactly the 201 count, zero 503s, one appointment row per seat and one SMS row per booking. Also covered: the raw-JDBC bypass of the handler; idempotency reserve/replay/reuse/rollback/purge, including the concurrent same-key pair forced by BookingPause; the per-mobile rules (an upcoming check that ignores past rows, a concurrent same-mobile pair at two branches); outbox atomicity, rollback, supersession, a cancel's deletion of every confirmation whatever its status, and the daily cap (RecipientDailyCapIT), counted in its ledger of keyed fingerprints even after a cancel deletes the messages (RecipientCapLedgerIT); the dispatcher's sent, backoff, FAILED, permanent-refusal, SKIP LOCKED, poisoned-row, batch-of-20, failing-gateway and deleted-cancellation cases with runOnce(now) and MutableClock advanced 2/4/8/16 s (no sleeps), the retry endpoint (RetryMessageApiIT), and the real loop on its own thread in DispatcherLoopIT; cancel freeing the seat, deleting the appointment and its confirmations and keeping only the cancellation, a later lookup or cancel answered 404 and counted, and two cancels at once giving one 200 and one counted 404; the retention purge (RetentionPurgeIT), the demo data (DemoDataLoaderIT), the lookup limit counted in lookup_attempts and shared between instances (LookupAttemptLimiterIT), and the two hourly purges (LookupAttemptPurgeJobIT, RecipientSendLedgerPurgeJobIT); constraint names and the active-status predicate read from pg_constraint; schema constraints firing on raw inserts; application start with Flyway, the probes, the holiday-coverage schedule and a health response with no component details; the booked-intervals read at the edges of its day. LogRedactionIT (an ordinary IT) reads the application's formatted log and the container log. Further cases: CancelAppointmentIT.cancelFreesSeatForNewBooking, cancelReturnsTheCancelledAppointmentWithOnlyItsCancellationAndDeletesIt, lookupAndRepeatCancelAfterACancelReturn404AndAreCounted, twoConcurrentCancelsGiveOne200AndOne404ThatIsCounted, cancelDeletesTheConfirmationWhetherPendingFailedOrSent; IdempotencyIT.sameKeyDifferentPayloadReturns422, sameKeyWithTheNumberAndTimeTypedAnotherWayReplays, sameKeyAfterItsBookingIsCancelledReturns422AndNeverBooksAgain; IdempotencyKeyPurgeJobIT.keyIsHonouredForTwentyFourHoursThenBooksAnewEvenBeforeThePurge, purgeRemovesOnlyKeysThatHaveExpired; BookAppointmentIT.failureAfterTheSmsIsWrittenRollsTheSmsBackWithTheBooking (the rollback proof); ConfirmationOutboxIT.bookingAndPendingMessagesCommitAtomically; NotificationDispatcherIT.oneTickHandlesAtMostTwentyMessages, gatewayThatThrowsIsTriedAgain, gatewayErrorMarksTheMessageAndTheBatchCarriesOn, retryWaitsForTheSendInFlightThenResetsTheFailedMessage (M6), cancelDeletesThePendingConfirmationAndTheSentCancellationIsDeleted, confirmationInFlightWhenTheCancelArrivesIsSentBeforeTheCancellation, permanentRefusalMarksFailedOnItsFirstAttemptAndLogsItsCode, poisonedRowDoesNotBlockTheBatch (a test-only trigger raises only on that row's SENT mark: every other row is sent exactly once, and the poisoned row ends FAILED with failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR and last_error 'MARK_FAILED'); SchemaConstraintIT.rawInsertViolatingEachNamedConstraintIsRejected; ApplicationStartsIT.migrationsEnableBtreeGistForTheDoubleBookingConstraints, readinessProbeIsUpWhenTheDatabaseAnswers

Architecture

ArchUnit 1.5 (archunit-junit5), Surefire

The four module and layer rules from backend.dependency_rule, run on every build without Docker

Contract pinning (OpenAPI drift and docs drift)

OpenApiContractIT runs with the other ITs: it fetches /v3/api-docs.yaml and compares it exactly with docs/openapi.yaml, and -Dopenapi.update=true (make contract) rewrites the file instead; there is no YAML normalisation code. The web CI step runs npm run check:api, which fails when src/api/schema.d.ts is not what openapi-typescript generates. scripts/check-test-refs.sh runs in CI. All three are hard gates.

The committed spec equals the running API. The generated TypeScript client equals the committed spec. Every ProblemCode appears in the Problem schema's code enum (OpenApiContractIT.problemCodeListsEveryProblemCode), so the client's type for a code is the exact set; docs/errors.md is kept in step by ProblemCodeCatalogueTest. Every Class.method cited by a proven rule in docs/domain-rules.md, or by a README row not marked planned, exists under api/src/test.

  • OpenApiContractIT.committedContractMatchesTheRunningApi
  • OpenApiContractIT.everyPatternIsAnchoredSoClientsMatchTheWholeValue
  • OpenApiContractIT.realResponsesHaveExactlyThePropertiesTheirSchemasDeclare
  • OpenApiContractIT.problemCodeListsEveryProblemCode
  • OpenApiContractIT.specDeclaresTheIdempotencyKeyHeader and specDeclaresTheMobileLast4HeaderAndRetryAfter (M3, with the booking and lookup endpoints)
  • web CI: npm run check:api (the generated client matches docs/openapi.yaml)
  • CI scripts/check-test-refs.sh (every cited test exists)

Frontend component

Vitest 5 + React Testing Library 16 + MSW 3 (network-level mocks over the real openapi-fetch client), jsdom, user-event; run twice in CI, with TZ=UTC and TZ=America/New_York, with the system date mocked where the test is about dates

Built in M4 with 181 tests in 31 files; M6 brought them to 208 in the same 31 files, adding cancelling, Book again, the inbox's Retry and redacted references; the cancel that deletes the booking brought them to 214, still in 31 files, adding the final cancelled view, which stops asking once the cancel is sent and shows again after a refresh from what the tab remembers, and the record of a cancellation that keeps no name or number. Every step of /book (BranchStep, ServiceStep, DateTimeStep with the date strip inside the server's window and the time grid, DetailsStep, ReviewStep) and the whole flow (BookingFlow); the recovery paths (409 alternatives, a 503 retried with the same key, a new key for changed details); ConfirmationPage polling, Copy and the new-tab last-four form; ManagePage normalisation; InboxPage states; the SAST helpers (lib/time.ts); the copy by code (problemCopy.ts); useIdempotencyKey; the query hooks; the shell, headings and focus; and the fake API's own handlers

  • ReviewStep.test.tsx sends a refused detail back to its field on the details step
  • ReviewStep.test.tsx offers the later times when the time was just taken, and books the one chosen
  • ReviewStep.test.tsx words DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT by its code, with where to go next
  • ReviewStep.test.tsx retries a TRY_AGAIN once with the same key, and books
  • useIdempotencyKey.test.tsx keeps one key for the same request across renders and a refresh
  • useIdempotencyKey.test.tsx mints a new key when anything in the request changes, so the API never sees a key reused
  • BookingFlow.test.tsx books the FICA update at Sandton Summit Mall and shows the SMS going from Sending to Sent
  • ConfirmationPage.test.tsx shows the booking, and moves the SMS from Sending to Sent by polling
  • ConfirmationPage.test.tsx asks a new tab for the last four digits once, and remembers them
  • PageHeading.test.tsx takes focus when it arrives with an in-app navigation
  • InboxPage.test.tsx lists every message newest first, with its status, attempts and failure in words (recipients as 082 *** ****)
  • ConfirmationPage.test.tsx cancels with the remembered digits, then says the details are deleted and the SMS is on its way
  • ConfirmationPage.test.tsx shows the cancellation again after a refresh, from what this tab remembers
  • bookingSession.test.ts remembers a cancellation without the name or the number, and forgets the digits
  • ConfirmationPage.test.tsx books the same service at the same branch again, from the date and time step
  • InboxPage.test.tsx retries a failed message, says so, and shows it queued again
  • time.test.ts shows an instant on its SAST date and clock, wherever the device is

End-to-end

Playwright 1.64 (Chromium) in its own npm project, e2e/, against a running app: in CI the stack job's docker compose up --build --wait, locally make e2e after make up; projects mobile (390x844, twice the pixel density) and desktop (1280x800), both in the New York time zone; @axe-core/playwright 4.13; a shared fixture that fails a test on any securitypolicyviolation event, uncaught error or console error it did not declare; fixtures.ts picks a random demo number from 082 555 0100 to 0199 and a random branch, service, open date and free time from the API, and books again with another on a 409; one worker, since the tests share one database; retries: 1 on CI, with the trace kept on failure; Chromium cached by the e2e lockfile; the report, traces and compose logs uploaded on failure

Four specs, built in M6, each asserting through the real API, database and dispatcher; axe on the review step and the confirmation page. There is no slot-taken spec and no Swagger UI spec.

  • book-appointment.spec.ts (mobile, desktop): a customer books from the landing page through every step, a landline is refused on its field, the confirmation shows the APT- reference and the SMS going from Sending to Sent within 15 s, the name and number never appear in the address, and /manage answers wrong digits with the not-found copy and opens the booking with the right ones
  • book-appointment.spec.ts: axe finds no serious or critical WCAG 2.2 AA violation on the review step or the confirmation page
  • cancel.spec.ts (mobile, desktop): Keep my appointment sends nothing and gives focus back; Yes, cancel it shows 'This appointment was cancelled', which takes focus, and 'Its details have been deleted' with the cancellation SMS on its way, and no second Cancel; availability shows the seat free again; a refresh shows the same from what the tab remembers; and the API then answers 404 APPOINTMENT_NOT_FOUND even to the right last four digits
  • keyboard.spec.ts (desktop): a booking made with the keyboard alone, the skip link the first stop, each step's heading taking focus and every control showing a focus outline
  • failed-delivery.spec.ts (desktop): a booking for 082 555 0000 shows Not delivered within 60 s, once the dispatcher's five attempts are spent, and says the booking still stands

Smoke and demo scripts

bash + curl + grep; scripts/smoke.sh runs by make smoke and twice in the CI stack job, before and after the end-to-end tests, to prove it can be rerun, and once more in the deploy job, against the hosted demo after cdk deploy and before the release is recorded; scripts/demo-409.sh runs once, after the end-to-end tests

Headless proof for reviewers who never open a browser; each script discovers its slot from the API and uses its own branch and fresh random mobiles

  • scripts/smoke.sh as built in M2 and M3: readiness and liveness, the app and its routes, the branch list and detail, Sandton FICA availability on its last bookable date with +02:00 offsets, 422 DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW and 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH, the booking and lookup endpoints through refusals that write nothing (no Idempotency-Key, a landline, no X-Mobile-Last4, a reference that cannot exist), the OpenAPI 3.1 document at /v3/api-docs, Swagger UI, problem codes, the correlation-id echo, closed operational endpoints and the security headers; it writes nothing, so it is safe against any instance
  • scripts/smoke.sh since M6: /actuator/prometheus serves appointments_booked_total and outbox_pending, and a 403 from an edge firewall, such as the hosted demo's WAF, counts as a closed endpoint; with EXPECT_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY=1 it also checks that a number outside the demo block is refused with DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED
  • scripts/demo-409.sh (make demo-409) finds a Polokwane FICA slot with seatsAvailable=1, searching back from the last bookable date, fires 20 parallel curls with 20 distinct demo numbers (082 555 01xx from a random start), prints the chosen slot and the 1 x 201 / 19 x 409 split, and exits non-zero on any other split

DeliverPartly built

Delivery plan

Status. M0 to M6 are done, with a few M0 items deferred (see Build status): M6 built the whole Tier 2 slot (cancel, retry, demo data, two meters), four Playwright specs in CI and the walkthrough PNGs, and real SMS and deleting each hosted booking once it is over followed. Since then a cancel deletes its booking at once, the pipeline deploys the hosted demo from main once every check is green, and the demo runs two tasks. M7 is built: the clean-clone rehearsal passed, the final documentation pass is done, with docs/tour.md and the AI note, and v1.0.0 is tagged on 0991304. The README walkthrough was not written.

Checkpoint. End of M4 (hour 19). From a clean clone, a reviewer can run make up (health-gated, Swagger UI live) and book in the browser at 390 px: branch, service, a date strip showing closed reasons, a slot grid in SAST, details with the POPIA notice, then review. They land on a persistent confirmation page with the APT-XXXX-XXXX reference, the SAST window, the documents to bring and the composed SMS text, already committed as a PENDING outbox row in the same transaction as the booking. Replaying the same Idempotency-Key with curl returns Idempotent-Replayed: true. A duplicate booking returns a 409 that reveals no reference, and lookup with X-Mobile-Last4 works with 404-not-403. The +02:00 contract test, the holiday rule tests and SchemaConstraintIT run in CI, and docs/openapi.yaml plus the generated client are committed. The checkpoint can be captured in screenshots. Not there yet: the 20-thread race test and the concurrent idempotency proofs (the raw-SQL bypass test was built in M1), the dispatcher with backoff and FAILED, and the live delivery badge (all M5, hour 25); the Playwright spec (M6); cancel (Tier 2). The UI states, accessibility and web tests planned for M6 came with the screens in M4.

M2 (day 2-3, hours 7.5-10.5) SlotCalendar and availability API 3 h
  • SlotCalendar as plain Java, given the instant now (read once from the injected Clock by AvailabilityQueries), computing in branch.timezone. Slot(startsAt, endsAt, seatsAvailable). validate(startsAt) returns exactly one code in this precedence: DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW > SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY > SLOT_OUTSIDE_HOURS > SLOT_OFF_GRID > SLOT_WITHIN_LEAD_TIME
  • GET /api/v1/branches/{code}/availability?service=&date= with timezone, generatedAt, closedReason|null and slots[]. Offsets are formatted explicitly with ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone (no reliance on Jackson time-zone properties). 400 VALIDATION_FAILED, 404 BRANCH_NOT_FOUND, 422 SERVICE_NOT_OFFERED_AT_BRANCH, 422 DATE_OUTSIDE_BOOKING_WINDOW, checked in that order. BookingProperties for lead time and horizon

AvailabilityApiIT includes rendersStartsAtWithJohannesburgOffset and the 422 codes

M3 (day 3, hours 10.5-15.5) Booking transaction, outbox row, lookup 5 h
  • Value objects: MobileNumber.parse (libphonenumber ZA, type MOBILE only; six-format matrix; masked(), redacted() '082 *** ****', last4()); AppointmentReference; Appointment with AppointmentStatus.canTransitionTo and an @Version that is null until persist (built in M1)
  • BookAppointmentHandler.handle, one TransactionTemplate at READ COMMITTED, each step one line calling a named private method (aiming for under 60 lines). (1) idempotency INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE ... WHERE the existing row has expired, RETURNING key (a key is honoured for exactly 24 hours, purged or not; the hourly purge only keeps the table small). (2) load branch and branch_services. (3) SlotCalendar.validate. (4) pg_advisory_xact_lock on the mobile. (5) DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT pre-check (ends_at > :now), then the CUSTOMER_OVERLAP pre-check. (6) branch_services FOR UPDATE through the booking-owned BookingLocks. (7) lowest free seat, or 409 SLOT_FULL with three alternatives. (8) EntityManager.persist + flush. (9) NotificationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation writing the SMS row from NotificationTemplates with a JdbcClient INSERT. (10) complete the idempotency row
  • ConstraintViolationTranslator via ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and SQLSTATE; reference collision or 40P01/40001 re-runs once; a second conflict is 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2, a second collision a 500. No problem body carries a reference. Every time-sensitive SQL statement binds :now (NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest, already built in M2). Hikari options lock_timeout=2000 / statement_timeout=5000 and connectionTimeout 3 s -> 503 TRY_AGAIN with Retry-After: 2 through ProblemDetailHandler (5 for the pool timeout); there is no test profile, so the ITs run with the production timeouts
  • Log hygiene: pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false, logging.level.org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error=OFF (the category Hibernate 7 logs failed statements under, not SqlExceptionHelper), the translator logs only constraint name and SQLSTATE, MDC holds only the correlation id, and log lines name appointments by id, never by reference. LookupAppointmentHandler with X-Mobile-Last4 (404 on any mismatch) and LookupAttemptLimiter (Caffeine, bounded size, each lookup counted as in flight before the read; five failures in the hour from the first wrong lookup -> 429; since V8 the counts live in PostgreSQL, in lookup_attempts, shared by every instance, and Caffeine is gone). AppointmentController POST (201 + Location, Idempotent-Replayed) and GET
  • Tests: AppointmentApiIT core cases (201 with Location and a PENDING SMS, 400 missing key, 422 landline, 422 SLOT_ON_CLOSED_DAY, 409 SLOT_FULL with alternatives, 409 DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT without a reference, noProblemBodyEverContainsAnAppointmentReference, getWithWrongLast4Returns404NotForbidden, sixthWrongLast4WithinHourReturns429, responsesNeverContainUnmaskedMobile, everyProblemCarriesCodeAndCorrelationId); BookAppointmentIT.pastConfirmedAppointmentDoesNotBlockSameServiceBooking; LogRedactionIT (an ordinary IT on real PostgreSQL: the application's formatted log plus the container's, across 201, replay, key reuse, 400, 422s, both customer 409s, SLOT_FULL, lookups up to 429, the exclusion constraint and a forced 500; the cancel path is added with the Tier 2 cancel); MobileNumberTest; AppointmentStatusTest; AppointmentReferenceTest; NotificationTemplatesTest.confirmationFitsOneGsm7SegmentForLongestSeededNames

Done when: A curl booking returns APT-XXXX-XXXX with a masked mobile and a PENDING SMS in notifications[]. Replaying the same key returns Idempotent-Replayed: true. A DUPLICATE body contains no reference. A past appointment does not block rebooking. LogRedactionIT proves that no name, mobile or reference reaches the app, Hibernate, pgjdbc or Postgres logs on a 409. docs/errors.md lists every code used so far.   • NotificationDispatcher, run every 2 s by DispatcherLoop on its own single thread outbox-1 (no scheduler bean, which would replace Boot's default): up to 20 messages per tick, each in its own transaction (claim LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED with :now, send, JdbcClient UPDATE, commit; a failed mark becomes FAILED with failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR and last_error 'MARK_FAILED'); backoff 2/4/8/16 s with FAILED on the fifth failed attempt (since 27 October 2026, at once on a refusal the gateway marks permanent); runOnce(Instant now) for tests; SimulatedSmsGateway (150 ms, recipients ending 0000 refused with SIMULATED_FAILURE); a gateway exception counts as a refusal; no OutboxHealthIndicator, because health shows no component details: the simulated inbox, GET /api/v1/messages behind branchbook.inbox.enabled, and docs/runbook.md show the outbox
  • NotificationDispatcherIT (MutableClock, no sleeps): marksSentWithProviderMessageId, retriesWithBackoffThenMarksFailedAfterFiveAttempts, twoDispatchersNeverSendTheSameMessageTwice (the gateway double blocks on a latch), poisonedRowDoesNotBlockTheBatch (a database-level failure injected by a test-only trigger that raises on that row's SENT mark; a 65-character providerMessageId is rejected by MessageGateway.Accepted inside the gateway, which counts as a refusal, so it cannot poison a mark), oneTickHandlesAtMostTwentyMessages, gatewayThatThrowsIsTriedAgain, gatewayErrorMarksTheMessageAndTheBatchCarriesOn; DispatcherLoopIT runs the real loop. ConfirmationPage (built in M4) polls to SENT or FAILED in a polite status region; inbox polls every 2 s
  • scripts/smoke.sh, which writes nothing so it is safe against any instance (a departure recorded in M3), and scripts/demo-409.sh (make demo-409) (Polokwane, slot discovered at runtime, 20 distinct mobiles, exits 1 on any split other than 1/19); docs/runbook.md with the retry SQL as the Tier-1 replay path; ADR-0002 and ADR-0003; README simulated-confirmation and testing sections
