Scope cuts

CutWhyIn README
Customer login (JWT, OIDC, OTP)Not in the brief; a one-week implementation is security theatre. Reference + last-4 with 404-not-403 and a per-reference limiter is proportionate. OTP-verified mobile or OIDC bound to a customer id is the production path (ADR-0004).yes
Staff or admin endpoints and UI (check-in, complete, no-show sweeper, listings, API keys)The brief asks for a customer feature, not a platform, and staff transitions need an actor the brief does not include. The state machine and its tests exist. The endpoints and a 15-minute NO_SHOW job (which is also what closes past appointments) are described in next steps; the per-service rule only counts upcoming appointments, so their absence locks nobody out.yes
Reschedule endpointHonest trade-off: today a change of time means cancel, then rebook. The CancelDialog warns that cancelling releases the time, and only branch and service are pre-filled. The atomic seat swap (acquire the new seat, release the old one, same transaction, same reference) is written up in ADR-0002 consequences as the next feature.yes
Reminder messages 24 h beforeA second scheduler plus time-travel tests for marginal demo value. The outbox kind column already accommodates REMINDER.yes
Ad-hoc branch_closures table with cascade cancellationPublic holidays and per-branch hours already prove closed-day logic. The cascade reuses the same outbox and is described, not built.yes
SA ID number collection, Luhn validation, encryption at rest, HMAC lookupPOPIA s10 minimality: the booking does not need it and identity is verified in branch. ADR-0004 records the decision; docs/production-notes.md, written in M6, does not carry a validator or an encryption design.yes
Retention/de-identification job, append-only audit table with trigger, split database rolesBank-grade on paper, but each costs an hour the UI and tests need. docs/production-notes.md asks for a retention schedule agreed with the bank (POPIA s14). One deletion job was built, for the hosted demo: RetentionPurgeJob, off by default, deletes each appointment within the hour after it ends, with its messages and idempotency keys. Since V6 a cancel deletes its booking at once, everywhere.yes
.ics calendar attachmentRoughly an hour that competes with UI states. SAST correctness is already proven by the +02:00 literal test and the UI. First item in next steps.yes
Real SMS/email providers, Mailpit/MailHog SMTP, WhatsAppThe brief says simulated. A real adapter is one MessageGateway class plus configuration, and one was built after M6: where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws, as on the hosted demo, AwsSmsGateway texts every number outside the demo block through AWS End User Messaging SMS in af-south-1, and the demo block stays simulated. Email, Mailpit and WhatsApp stay cut: Mailpit adds a container and is still not SMS, and WhatsApp needs Meta template approval.yes
Kafka/RabbitMQ, microservices, Spring Modulith, Kubernetes manifests, Terraform, API gatewayDisproportionate for one deployable and one consumer. The dispatcher's claim loop is the documented Kafka/MSK seam (ADR-0003) and the Dockerfile is the EKS seam.yes
Public live demo on free-tier hostingA sleeping instance makes the reviewer's first click look broken, and a fake bank collecting real phone numbers outside South Africa is a bad look. Compose, the screenshots and the walkthrough carry the local demo; the hosted demo runs instead at https://branchbook.click on AWS in Cape Town, always on and open to anyone with the address (ADR-0006).yes
Per-IP rate limiting (Bucket4j), request-body size filter, bulkheads, WAF, Trivy image scans, coverage gates, mutation and load testsGateway/platform concerns, or theatre for a take-home. Only the lookup limiter is in the app; the hosted demo puts AWS WAF in front of its load balancer, with 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes and AWS managed rules. Body-size and rate limits are listed under production adds. Trivy is optional only if everything is green at hour 30 (it is a toolchain addition).yes
Dark mode, i18n, nearest-branch geolocation, maps, month-end hours overrides, HELD/REQUESTED states, waiting listsNo reviewer value inside 35 hours. Booking is single-step and transactional, so no hold timer is needed.yes
Overall cap of N active appointments per customerWithout OTP, numbers are free, so a cap per number proves nothing. The per-service upcoming rule (under the per-mobile lock) and the customer-overlap EXCLUDE constraint already bound what one number can hold. Recorded in docs/domain-rules.md, not the README.no
Tier 2 (built only if M5 is green by hour 25): the cancel flow (POST .../cancel, CancelDialog, CancelAppointmentIT, confirmation-superseded-by-cancellation rule). The read-only /manage lookup (ManagePage) is not in this cut: it is Tier 1 work in M4, because every confirmation SMS links to /manageNot in the brief's wording ('schedule' and 'communicate a confirmation'). It is the first thing built in the Tier-2 slot because it completes the manage story. If it is not built, the GET lookup and the confirmation page still exist, and the README scope row says 'not built'. Built in M6.yes
Tier 2: POST /api/v1/messages/{id}/retry and the inbox Retry buttonThe runbook UPDATE is the Tier-1 replay path. The endpoint is a convenience, built after cancel in the Tier-2 slot. Built in M6, with the inbox's Retry; where the inbox is off, the runbook UPDATE is still the replay path.yes
Tier 2: DemoDataLoader (Bellville and Umhlanga only, never Polokwane)The walkthrough books its own data. The loader only makes the first screen and the inbox interesting on first load. Built in M6: three bookings on a fresh compose stack, none where only the demo numbers may book or where SMS are real (BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws).no
Tier 2: custom Micrometer meters appointments_booked_total{outcome} and outbox_pending; Tier 3: notifications_dispatched_total and any further metersThe simulated inbox and the runbook's SQL already show pending and failed messages (there is no OutboxHealthIndicator: health shows no component details), and Boot's default meters stay at /actuator/prometheus. Custom meters are production polish, not proof. The Tier 2 pair was built in M6; the Tier 3 counter, notifications_dispatched_total, was not.yes
Tier 3 (cut by default): email channel (SimulatedEmailGateway, EMAIL rows, the optional email field)SMS alone proves the outbox, the port and the retries. If the channel is not built, the optional email field is removed from the form and the request record in the same commit (POPIA s10: collect nothing unused), and the README says so. Build it only if M5 finished early and the Tier-2 slot is done.yes
Tier 3: Playwright slot-taken and cancel specsscripts/demo-409.sh, ConcurrentBookingIT and the BookingFlow 409 Vitest test already prove the race and its recovery. One book spec at two viewports with axe is the e2e proof. Built in M6: cancel.spec.ts at both viewports, and keyboard and failed-delivery specs at 1280 px; the slot-taken spec was not built.no
Tier 3: README GIFThe PNGs captured by page.screenshot() in the Playwright spec, one per step at each viewport in docs/assets/walkthrough/, are the baseline. The GIF costs about 45 minutes of ffmpeg/gifsicle work.no
Tier 3: per-recipient SMS daily cap (RECIPIENT_DAILY_CAP)The threat model and production controls (OTP, per-recipient and per-prefix caps, CAPTCHA, cost alerts) are written in ADR-0004 and the README. Building the cap competes with Tier-1 proofs. Built after M6 with real SMS (BR-17): off by default, five a day on the hosted demo, counted in sms_recipient_sends (V7) under a keyed fingerprint of the number, whose key the hosted demo takes from Secrets Manager.yes
ArchUnit beyond the four rules in ArchitectureTest, Mockito handler unit tests beyond LookupAppointmentHandlerTest (where a stub must make the database fail on cue), a prod-profile Spring context testThey prove plumbing, not behaviour. The ITs exercise handle() end to end, and ProblemCodeCatalogueTest covers the catalogue.no
PR ceremony beyond three feature PRsAbout an hour of overhead. Three PRs (booking transaction, outbox, web) carry the narrative and everything else goes straight to main. As built, every change since 26 October 2026 reaches main through a pull request, and since 27 October 2026 main requires the api, web, stack, infra and gitleaks checks, with no review.no

DeliverPartly built

Repository and workflow

Status. Built: make up, the Makefile with make contract and make e2e, Dockerfile, compose, CI (api, web, stack and infra jobs; the stack job runs smoke, the Playwright tests, smoke again and demo-409), continuous deployment of the hosted demo and the release record from main once all four pass, Dependabot, the git hooks, the ADRs, and docs/runbook.md, docs/porting.md, docs/production-notes.md and docs/tour.md; the hosted demo at https://branchbook.click is deployed from the CDK app by the pipeline. Not built: coverage in the CI job summary, Dependabot for e2e/, and the README walkthrough with its screenshots, which M7 adds.

One command

make up = docker compose up --build --detach --wait --wait-timeout 300, then prints the URL. Prerequisites: Docker Desktop or Engine 24+ with Compose v2 and about 4 GB free; nothing else needs installing. A cold build takes about three minutes on a typical laptop and connection (the README promises under five minutes cold and under one minute warm). BuildKit caches the Maven dependency layer and npm. --wait returns only when db pg_isready and app /readyz are green, so the printed URL always works. The app port binds to 127.0.0.1 only, and the database publishes none. The landing page shows six branches. /messages already lists the three DemoDataLoader bookings at Bellville and Umhlanga, one of them FAILED (its number ends 0000) about 30 seconds after start-up. make down = docker compose down -v. Without make (Windows): docker compose up --build --wait. On a port clash, set APP_PORT in .env.

Repository layout

  • README.md (reviewer map, under 300 lines, grown in every milestone)
  • LICENSE (MIT)
  • Makefile (targets: up, down, logs, psql, smoke, e2e, demo-409, dev-api, dev-web, test, test-api, test-web, test-docs, contract, fmt, hooks, secrets-scan, secrets-scan-history; raw command printed beside each target for Windows; up prints elapsed time and the URL)
  • Dockerfile (three-stage, BuildKit cache mounts for ~/.m2 and the npm cache: node:24.22.1-alpine web build -> eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jdk-alpine runs ./mvnw -DskipTests package and a layered extract with web/dist copied to src/main/resources/static -> eclipse-temurin:25.0.4.1_1-jre-alpine, uid 10001, TZ=UTC, HEALTHCHECK /readyz; tests run in CI, never in docker build)
  • compose.yaml (app on 127.0.0.1:${APP_PORT:-8080}; db postgres:17.11-alpine, with no published port, with command postgres -c log_error_verbosity=terse -c log_min_error_statement=panic; pg_isready; depends_on condition service_healthy)
  • .env.example (APP_PORT, DB_USER, DB_PASSWORD placeholders only)
  • .gitignore (first commit: .env, target/, node_modules/, web/dist, .vite, playwright-report/, api/src/main/resources/static/)
  • .editorconfig
  • .nvmrc (24)
  • .github/workflows/ci.yml (jobs api, web, stack and infra, then deploy), secret-scan.yml and aws-connection-check.yml
  • .github/dependabot.yml (weekly maven, npm, docker, github-actions, grouped minor/patch; weekly version updates with deliberate major pins ignored, each explained in the file)
  • .github/pull_request_template.md (Intent / Proof / Risk / Review focus; used by the three feature PRs)
  • api/pom.xml, api/mvnw, api/mvnw.cmd, api/.mvn/ (Surefire runs *Test and Failsafe *IT, both with their defaults; SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest keeps every *Test free of Docker)

  • api/src/main/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/booking/{api,application,domain,infrastructure} (application.BookingLocks port; infrastructure holds the branch_services FOR UPDATE, the lowest-free-seat query and the BookedIntervalsPort implementation)

  • api/src/main/resources/db/migration/{V1__enable_btree_gist.sql, V2__schema.sql, V3__reference_data.sql, V4__public_holidays_2026_2027.sql, V5__notification_messages_recipient_index.sql, V6__cancel_deletes_the_booking.sql, V7__sms_recipient_sends.sql, V8__lookup_attempts.sql}
  • api/src/test/java/za/co/fynbos/branchbook/{*Test beside their packages, *IT, ArchitectureTest, SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest, OpenApiContractIT, LogRedactionIT, NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest, support/TestcontainersConfiguration, support/MutableClock, support/Bookings, support/DatabaseReset, support/ScriptedReferenceGenerator}
  • web/{package.json, package-lock.json, vite.config.ts (proxy /api, /actuator, /readyz and /livez -> :8080; also the Vitest config), tsconfig.json (strict), eslint.config.js (typescript-eslint strict, jsx-a11y-x strict, TSDoc), .prettierrc.json, index.html}

  • scripts/{smoke.sh, demo-409.sh, check-test-refs.sh}

CI pipeline

StageWhat it runs
triggerOn push to main, pull_request and a manual run. Concurrency group per ref, with cancel-in-progress except on main, where runs queue so a deployment is never interrupted. permissions contents: read; only deploy adds id-token: write. TZ is set per web test step. The four check jobs run in parallel (no needs); deploy needs all four. Targets: api job under 6 minutes, full pipeline under 15.
apiubuntu-24.04; actions/checkout; actions/setup-java (temurin at the Versions-table JDK, cache maven); ./mvnw -B -ntp verify in api/. That runs spotless:check, Surefire unit tests, ArchitectureTest (four rules), SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest, NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest, then Failsafe *IT, OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT among them, on one shared Testcontainers postgres:17.11-alpine (@ServiceConnection, no profile). Then the unit tests and the API tests (*ApiIT) run again with TZ=America/New_York, and scripts/check-test-refs.sh (fails if a proven rule, or a README row not marked planned, cites a Class.method missing from src/test). JaCoCo writes its report during verify; no coverage figure reaches $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY yet (never a gate). Surefire/Failsafe reports are uploaded on failure.
webactions/setup-node (24, cache npm); npm ci; npm run check:api (a hard gate); npm run lint (ESLint 10 + typescript-eslint + jsx-a11y-x + jsdoc); npm run format:check; npm run typecheck (tsc -b); TZ=UTC npm test; TZ=America/New_York npm test; npm run build. Vitest coverage in the job summary is not built yet.
stackdocker compose up --build --detach --wait --wait-timeout 300 (fails fast if readiness never turns green). Run scripts/smoke.sh, which writes nothing. Then the Playwright tests in e2e/: npm ci, Chromium from actions/cache keyed on the e2e lockfile, and npx playwright test at 390 px and 1280 px, failing on any CSP violation or undeclared console error (retries: 1, trace kept on failure). Then scripts/smoke.sh again and scripts/demo-409.sh (1/19 split at Polokwane or exit 1), and a check that the app and database logs mention no credential. On failure, upload the Playwright report, its traces and docker compose logs --no-color. Always docker compose down --volumes.
infraactions/setup-node (24, cache npm on infra/package-lock.json); npm ci; npm run lint (ESLint with TSDoc); npm run build (type-check); npm test -- --ci (Jest on the synthesised templates); npx cdk synth --quiet with BRANCHBOOK_ALERT_EMAIL=alerts@example.com and BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true, so every stack, BranchbookAccount included, synthesises. No AWS credentials: synth never calls AWS. It replaced the path-filtered infra.yml, so it can be a required check.
deployOn a push to main or a manual run, never a pull request; needs api, web, stack and infra; ubuntu-24.04-arm, so the ARM64 image is built natively; environment aws-demo, which admits main and v* tags only; id-token: write; concurrency group deploy-aws, which never interrupts a running deployment. Signs in through OIDC as branchbook-github-deploy, with the account ID masked; npx cdk deploy BranchbookData BranchbookApp with BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true; scripts/smoke.sh against https://branchbook.click; then npx cdk deploy BranchbookRelease, failing unless /branchbook/release reads back GITHUB_SHA (ADR-0007). It never sets BRANCHBOOK_ALERT_EMAIL, so BranchbookAccount is never in its CDK app.
dependabot and releaseDependabot proposes weekly updates for Maven, the npm projects in web/ and infra/ (not yet e2e/), Docker, Compose and GitHub Actions, with minor and patch grouped and deliberate major pins ignored. Security alerts and security updates are on. The Protect main ruleset requires api, web, stack, infra and gitleaks to pass before a push or merge, not strictly and with no review. Submission is a v1.0.0 tag on a green main.

Local development without Docker

Prerequisites: the JDK recorded in the README Versions table (Temurin 25; sdkman or brew) and Node 24 (nvm use reads .nvmrc). Database: by default make dev-api starts a throwaway Testcontainers PostgreSQL. Without Docker, a local PostgreSQL 17 on 5432 (btree_gist ships in contrib) with DB_URL overridden in .env. API: make dev-api (./mvnw spring-boot:test-run) (Flyway migrates on start, human-readable logs, Swagger UI at :8080/swagger-ui.html, /api/v1/messages enabled, DemoDataLoader off). Web: cd web && npm ci && npm run dev at :5173; Vite proxies /api and /actuator to :8080, so there is no CORS. make dev-api and make dev-web run them. Tests without Docker: ./mvnw test (Surefire runs every *Test, which SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest keeps free of Docker, in under 20 s) and npm test (run it again with TZ=America/New_York). ./mvnw verify needs Docker: Failsafe runs *IT, OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT among them, on Testcontainers. Contract loop: make contract rewrites docs/openapi.yaml from /v3/api-docs.yaml, then regenerates src/api/schema.d.ts; commit both. make fmt = spotless:apply + prettier --write.

Architecture decision records

IDDecisionRejected alternativeConsequence
ADR-0001
Modular monolith: one jar, one PostgreSQL, packages by capability with ArchUnit (half page)		One Spring Boot deployable serves the SPA from /static, with one database. Packages are catalogue / scheduling / booking / notification / shared, each split into api/application/domain/infrastructure. Four ArchUnit rules: no package cycles, domain has no Spring imports, api never touches infrastructure, transactions open only in application. Dependency arrows are scheduling -> catalogue; booking -> catalogue, scheduling; notification -> booking (implements booking's ConfirmationOutbox port); there is no booking -> notification arrow. The in-process outbox dispatcher is the documented Kafka/MSK seam; scheduled jobs also run in-process: the holiday-coverage check, the hourly purges of expired keys, of the SMS cap's ledger and of closed lookup windows and, where a deployment switches it on, the hourly retention purge.Microservices (network hops multiply the surface for one team and one bounded context). Spring Modulith (a second framework to explain when one ArchUnit test says the same). CQRS/event sourcing (ten tables of CRUD plus invariants). A message broker (only one consumer). nginx as a third compose service (extra config, CSP and proxy files; the jar already serves the SPA, so CORS never arises).A reviewer reads the booking top to bottom in BookAppointmentHandler.handle's numbered steps, with the file list in docs/architecture.md. make up starts two containers. Scaling is a README paragraph (EKS for the container, Aurora behind the same migrations, the dispatcher claim loop becoming an MSK producer) rather than fifteen hours of half-built platform.
ADR-0002
Double booking prevented by btree_gist EXCLUDE constraints, a scoped branch_services row lock with lowest-free-seat selection, and a per-mobile advisory lock for the customer rules		appointments_seat_no_overlap and appointments_customer_no_overlap are EXCLUDE USING gist on tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at, '[)') restricted to status IN ('CONFIRMED','CHECKED_IN'), plus the composite FK to branch_services. BookAppointmentHandler takes locks in a fixed order: idempotency PK, then pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(mobileE164, 0)), then SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on the branch_services row. Under the mobile lock it pre-checks DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT ('one upcoming active appointment per mobile per service', ends_at > :now) and then CUSTOMER_OVERLAP. Under the seat lock it picks the lowest free seat via generate_series(1, capacity) NOT EXISTS, so precedence is deterministic: DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT, then CUSTOMER_OVERLAP, then SLOT_FULL (with alternatives). ConstraintViolationTranslator reads ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and SQLSTATE (23505/23P01). 40P01/40001 trigger one re-run of the transaction, then 503. No problem body ever carries a reference.A partial unique index on (branch_id, service_id, seat_no, starts_at). Within one service's grid this is equivalent today, but it cannot express the customer-overlap rule across services with different durations, nor the production move to a branch-wide consultant pool (EXCLUDE (branch_id WITH =, seat_no WITH =, range WITH &&) under a branches row lock), where unequal grids make a unique index impossible. Also rejected: UNIQUE (customer_mobile, service_id) WHERE active, which nothing in v1 ever releases, so every returning customer would be locked out; seat_no = count + 1 (false 409 after a cancellation gap at capacity > 1); @Version only (protects a row, not a slot); SERIALIZABLE (retry storms); application-level counts; and global, Redis or advisory locks as the seat mechanism (they couple unrelated branches; the advisory lock here is keyed on one mobile and couples nothing).The seat and overlap invariants hold against any code path, proven by ConcurrentBookingIT (20 threads at capacity 1 and 3, plus sameMobileTwoBranchesOverlappingConcurrentlyGivesOne201AndOne409CustomerOverlap) and SchemaConstraintIT (raw JDBC). Exactly one rule, the per-service upcoming rule, is enforced by the application under a lock, and BookAppointmentIT.pastConfirmedAppointmentDoesNotBlockSameServiceBooking proves it. Contention is scoped to one mobile and one branch-service pair. lock_timeout 2 s / statement_timeout 5 s turn a stuck lock into 503 TRY_AGAIN. Capacity per service assumes dedicated service desks. A reschedule, when built, is the same transaction acquiring the new seat before releasing the old one under the same reference.
ADR-0003
Simulated confirmation as a transactional outbox with an in-process SKIP LOCKED dispatcher, one transaction per message, no broker		NotificationOutbox.enqueueConfirmation/enqueueCancellation write notification_messages rows by direct call inside the booking and cancel transactions (SMS always; EMAIL only if the Tier-3 channel ships). DispatcherLoop, a SmartLifecycle, runs NotificationDispatcher.runOnce every 2 s on its own single thread outbox-1, which it owns, because a TaskScheduler bean would replace Boot's default scheduler; chosen for isolation, not throughput. Each tick processes up to 20 messages, each in its own TransactionTemplate: SELECT ... WHERE status='PENDING' AND next_attempt_at <= :now ORDER BY next_attempt_at, id LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, then MessageGateway.send (an exception counts as a refusal), then an immediate JdbcClient UPDATE to SENT or to attempts+1 with backoff 2/4/8/16 s (FAILED on the fifth failed attempt, or at once on a refusal the gateway marks permanent, such as AWS's for an unverified number or a reached spend limit), then commit. If marking fails, a separate short transaction sets FAILED (failure_reason DISPATCH_ERROR, last_error 'MARK_FAILED') so a row can never loop. A cancel deletes the booking's confirmations with the booking (V6), whatever their status, and the dispatcher deletes the cancellation SMS once it is sent or has failed for good. :now comes from the Clock. Appointment status never depends on delivery status.@TransactionalEventListener(AFTER_COMMIT) or any Spring event (the classic lost-confirmation bug when the process dies between commit and send, and it hides the mechanism). Sending on the request thread. One transaction for a batch of 20 (one database error rolls back every mark and re-sends every SMS every tick). Claiming in one transaction and processing in another (double-send after locks drop, or self-deadlock on REQUIRES_NEW). Kafka/RabbitMQ (disproportionate for one consumer). Mailpit/MailHog SMTP (another container, still not SMS). WebSockets/SSE for status (2 s polling of one small resource is enough).Delivery is at-least-once and idempotent on message id; a crash re-sends at most the one in-flight message. FAILED arrives about 35 s after the first attempt for a recipient ending 0000 and stays visible in /messages; replay is POST /messages/{id}/retry, built in M6, where the inbox is on, and the runbook UPDATE where it is off. A cancelled appointment never receives a late 'confirmed' SMS. A real provider is one class plus configuration: since 27 October 2026, where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws, AwsSmsGateway sends every number outside the demo block through AWS End User Messaging SMS, and stores a refusal as a fixed code such as AWS_THROTTLED. When a second consumer appears, the claim loop becomes the MSK producer with the table unchanged.
ADR-0004
Restraint as a decision: no customer login, no ID number, no staff endpoints, full state enum with one reachable transition		Access to an appointment requires possession of the reference (31^8 space, SecureRandom) plus X-Mobile-Last4. Any mismatch returns 404, and LookupAttemptLimiter, counting in PostgreSQL (lookup_attempts, V8) so every instance shares the count, allows 5 failures per reference per hour before returning 429. No problem body ever returns a reference. '082 *** 0101' is shown only to a caller who has just proved the number; /messages, logs, MDC and metrics show '082 *** ****'. Only first name, last name and mobile are collected, plus an optional email only if the Tier 3 email channel ships (POPIA s10). There is no consent checkbox (s11(1)(b)). An inline s18 notice and a /privacy page are provided, and logs are hardened (s19). AppointmentStatus has CONFIRMED, CHECKED_IN, COMPLETED, CANCELLED and NO_SHOW with canTransitionTo guards and tests, but only CONFIRMED -> CANCELLED is reachable through the API. Customer fields live on the appointment; there is no customers table.JWT/OIDC/OTP login (security theatre in one week; the brief says schedule, not log in). SA ID number collection with Luhn, encryption at rest and HMAC lookup (not needed for a booking; identity is verified in branch). Staff/admin endpoints with API keys (an actor the brief does not include). A two-state enum (under-modelled). REQUESTED/HELD states (booking is single-step). A customer aggregate with a racy max-3 policy. Echoing existingReference in 409 bodies (anyone who knows a number could harvest that person's references, then view and cancel).The attack surface and PII footprint are minimal, and every omission is labelled with its production design. Accepted residual risk for the demo: DUPLICATE_APPOINTMENT and CUSTOMER_OVERLAP still reveal that a booking exists for a number the caller typed, and the unauthenticated booking endpoint will SMS any valid number: for real where BRANCHBOOK_SMS_GATEWAY=aws, as on the hosted demo, South Africa only and up to the per-recipient daily cap. Production closes both with OTP verification of the mobile before CONFIRMED (or booking only inside the authenticated app), per-recipient and per-prefix SMS caps, CAPTCHA, premium-prefix blocking and cost alerting. The questions 'why no login' and 'what stops SMS pumping' each have a one-paragraph answer.
ADR-0005
Committed OpenAPI contract, TypeScript client generated from it, React as the default front end (half page)		springdoc generates the spec. OpenApiContractIT fails when /v3/api-docs.yaml differs from docs/openapi.yaml, and make contract regenerates the file after an intended change. openapi-typescript generates web/src/api/schema.d.ts (committed; npm run check:api fails CI when it is stale), and openapi-fetch is the only HTTP client. RFC 9457 problems with stable codes in docs/errors.md are part of the contract. Front end: React + TypeScript strict on Vite.Hand-written response types (drift stays invisible until runtime). Java-side YAML normalisation and comparison code (half a day of no reviewer value). Spec-first server codegen (fights Initializr defaults). Vue 3 or Angular by default (the generated client makes a Vue swap a web/-only change of about eight hours). Pact consumer contracts (a second contract for one consumer).Type drift between API and SPA fails CI. Contract testing is literal and visible. docs/porting.md can promise a Vue 3 port and a .NET port in half a page because the contract and the SQL are stack-neutral.
README-D06
Decision row (no separate file): Idempotency-Key in its own idempotency_keys table as the first statement of the booking transaction		INSERT INTO idempotency_keys (key, request_hash, created_at, expires_at) VALUES (:key, :hash, :now, :now + 24h) ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE SET ... WHERE idempotency_keys.expires_at <= EXCLUDED.created_at RETURNING key, so a key whose 24 hours have run out is taken over in the same statement. If nothing is returned and the hash matches, the 201 is re-rendered from the current appointment with Idempotent-Replayed: true (no response body is stored); a different hash gets 422 IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED. A 409/422/503 rolls the key back. A key is honoured for exactly 24 h, purged or not; the hourly purge (DELETE ... WHERE expires_at <= :now) only keeps the table small. The UI mints one UUID per unchanged payload and persists {payloadHash, key} with the draft in sessionStorage, so a refresh after a timed-out POST reuses the key.A nullable unique column on appointments (the outcome depends on which index PostgreSQL reports first). A key lookup after seat allocation (a same-key retry at the last seat returns 409). A plain JPA insert with catch (continues inside an aborted PostgreSQL transaction).For two concurrent identical requests, the second INSERT ... ON CONFLICT waits on the first's uncommitted row. Once it returns nothing, a fresh READ COMMITTED snapshot read sees the committed 201 and replays the same reference. This is proven with a forced interleaving in concurrentSameKey_bookOnceAndBothReceiveSameReference. Lock order is fixed (idempotency PK, mobile advisory lock, branch_services), and 40P01/40001 trigger one re-run then 503.
README-D07
Decision row: Spring Boot 4.1.x with Initializr defaults, versions fixed by a day-0 spike and recorded in a README Versions table		A one-hour spike before M0 fixes the toolchain, which is never revisited. Spring Boot 4.1.x latest patch (fallback 4.0.x, still in OSS support at submission; never 3.5.x). Initializr's default JDK, Java 25 LTS (the day-0 spike passed). Boot-managed JUnit 6, Testcontainers 2 and Jackson 3. PostgreSQL 17 on an exact patch tag. Node 24 LTS. Vite and Vitest exactly as npm create vite gives them. spring.threads.virtual.enabled=true. No toolchain change after hour 30.Spring Boot 3.5.x (OSS support ended June 2026; a downgrade also means Jackson 3 -> 2, Hibernate 7 -> 6 and renamed starters). A Java 25 bump on submission day (a self-inflicted red build). Hard-coded majors chosen before scaffold day. Gradle Kotlin DSL. A Node or .NET back end (documented as ports).The manifest reads as current and deliberate, and the README Versions table answers 'why this version'. +02:00 output is produced by explicit formatting rather than a Jackson property, so it survives the Jackson 3 default.
README-D08
Decision row: availability returns one bounded day; branch detail carries 30 days of closedDates with reasons		GET /branches/{code}/availability?service=&date= returns one day's slots with seatsAvailable, closedReason and generatedAt. GET /branches/{code} returns closedDates for the next 30 days (CLOSED_DAY | PUBLIC_HOLIDAY with name), so the date strip needs no slot data until a day is chosen.7- or 14-day range endpoints with per-day slot lists (larger responses, awkward cache keys, more to paginate and test).Smallest response and simplest TanStack Query key (branch, service, date). A 30 s staleness, and a banner offering Refresh after ten minutes without activity, keep the grid honest; refocusing the window does not refetch, so times do not change under the customer's finger. POST re-validates through the same SlotCalendar with the same precedence, so UI and API never disagree.

DeliverBuilt

Risks and pitfalls

Status. The planned mitigations are in place: the toolchain spike passed, M4 built the UI with its tests, the Playwright specs run in CI, the clean-clone rehearsal passed, and the hosted demo's controls are built, the real-SMS ones included (South Africa only, five SMS a day per number, 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes, a price cap, the spend limit) with each booking deleted once its appointment is over, or at once when it is cancelled. Accepted and open: anyone can make the demo text any South African number up to the cap, and until AWS grants production access, only verified numbers receive its SMS.

RiskLikelihoodImpactMitigation
UI starvation: back-end depth eats the hours the front end needs and the submission fails half the briefhighhighTracer bullet: a bookable UI with a persisted SMS row by hour 19 (M4), before the proofs and the dispatcher. UI tests count as UI hours. No new feature starts after hour 26. M7 is reserved.
Overall overrun: even Tier 1 slips and the last day collapseshighhighTime log per milestone. The Tier-2 slot (hours 25-27) is the swing buffer. Tier 3 is cut by default. No Tier-2 item starts while any Tier-1 item is red. Submit-anyway protocol: tag at hour 35 with the scope table stating what was not built.
Boot 4.1 / Java 25 plugin friction (springdoc, JaCoCo, Spotless, ArchUnit, Testcontainers 2) burns scaffold timemediummediumA one-hour day-0 spike with go/no-go before M0. The spike passed on Boot 4.1.1 and Java 25, so no fallback was needed; the result is in the README Versions table. No toolchain change after hour 30.
An exclusion violation (SQLSTATE 23P01) is not mapped by constraint name, so a 409 silently becomes a 500mediumhighConstraintViolationTranslator reads ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint() and SQLSTATE, never the message text. Explicit flush points make violations surface at a known line. ConstraintNamesIT checks every mapped name. The row and advisory locks mean the constraints are backstops.
Jackson 3 (Boot 4 default) rewrites +02:00 offsets to ZmediummediumResponse records format offsets explicitly with ISO_OFFSET_DATE_TIME at branch.timezone, through one formatter, TimeFormat.isoOffset. AvailabilityApiIT.rendersStartsAtWithJohannesburgOffset pins the literal, and CI runs the API's unit and API tests again under TZ=America/New_York. The UI formats with timeZone Africa/Johannesburg, and the web tests, and in CI the API's unit and API tests, also run under TZ=America/New_York.
Cold make up is slow or fails on the reviewer's machine (port clash, Docker memory, arm64)mediumhighBuildKit cache mounts and no tests in the image. The spike verifies every base image has linux/arm64. APP_PORT is configurable and the db publishes no host port. --wait on readiness. The M7 rehearsal timed a cold make up from a fresh clone. Rehearsal on day 7. GHCR make up-pull only if the build exceeds five minutes.
Testcontainers needs Docker; Colima/Podman socket quirks, or a reviewer without Docker runs the testsmediumlowDocker-needing tests are the *IT classes, OpenApiContractIT and LogRedactionIT among them, and SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest enforces the split, so ./mvnw test runs without Docker in under 20 s. The README Testing section says this in its first line. Troubleshooting covers DOCKER_HOST and TESTCONTAINERS_RYUK_DISABLED. CI runs everything on every push.
Flaky concurrency proofs or order-dependent ITs turn CI red on submission daymediumhighResetClockExtension before every IT, and DatabaseReset before and after each test that books. All SQL binds :now from the Clock, and NativeSqlUsesBoundNowTest fails the build if a SQL string in the main code reads the database's clock. The race runs through MockMvc from twenty threads with a warm-up and 20 distinct mobiles. Every IT, the race included, runs with the production lock_timeout of 2 s; only a booking held by BookingPause raises its own, and the 503 path is pinned by a separate deterministic test (BookAppointmentIT.lockTimeoutReturns503TryAgainWithRetryAfter). Interleavings are forced with BookingPause, a test-only trigger that holds a booking at step 10 on an advisory lock, while pg_stat_activity shows the waits. Dispatcher tests use runOnce with MutableClock and no sleeps.
Playwright flakiness against a cold compose stackmediumlowFour specs, run one at a time because they share one database. compose --wait on readiness first. Assertions wait on DOM and delivery state, never fixed sleeps. Fixtures pick a random demo number and a free time from the API, and book again with another on a 409. retries: 1 on CI, with the trace kept on failure. The report, traces and compose logs are uploaded. The failed-delivery spec's one number, 082 555 0000, holds at most six upcoming appointments, so the spec fails after six runs against one database; CI starts from an empty one.
Per-customer responses leak data: references in 409 bodies, or unlock digits printed beside references in /messages and logsmediumhighDesigned out. No problem body carries a reference (noProblemBodyEverContainsAnAppointmentReference). '082 *** 0101' goes only to callers who proved the number, and '082 *** ****' appears everywhere else. Where a deployment sets BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES, as the hosted demo does, the inbox shows every reference as APT-****-**** too (InboxReferencesHiddenIT). ADR-0004 states the residual 'a booking exists' leak and its OTP production answer.
Customer rules misbehave: a past appointment blocks rebooking forever, or a same-mobile double submit deadlocks into a 500lowhighAn upcoming-only per-service rule under a per-mobile advisory lock, with fixed lock order and fixed pre-check precedence. 40P01/40001 trigger one re-run, then 503. Covered by pastConfirmedAppointmentDoesNotBlockSameServiceBooking and sameMobileTwoBranchesOverlappingConcurrentlyGivesOne201AndOne409CustomerOverlap.
The outbox re-sends a whole batch after one database error, or a late 'confirmed' SMS follows a cancellationmediumhighOne transaction per message, with MARK_FAILED as a fallback so no row loops. A cancel deletes its booking's confirmations, whatever their status, and retry refuses a superseded one. Covered by poisonedRowDoesNotBlockTheBatch (database-level failure), CancelAppointmentIT.cancelDeletesTheConfirmationWhetherPendingFailedOrSent and RetryMessageApiIT.retryOfSupersededConfirmationIsNoOp, the last two built in M6, the delete since V6.
PII reaches logs through framework or database logging that the app's own redaction does not seemediumhighlogServerErrorDetail=false, Hibernate's org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.error log OFF (Hibernate 7's category, not SqlExceptionHelper), and Postgres started with log_error_verbosity=terse. LogRedactionIT reads the application's formatted log and the container log across 201, replay, 4xx, 409s, 429 and 500, and from M6 across a cancellation and the retry of its messages, for the name, the number in any form and the reference. smoke.sh writes nothing, so it has no booked mobile to look for; LogRedactionIT checks the application's and PostgreSQL's logs instead. On the hosted demo, PostgreSQL's own log is not exported to CloudWatch, because a constraint error's detail line can quote a mobile number. Real SMS add no log of their own: no delivery events are recorded, because they carry the destination number, and an AWS refusal is stored, and logged beside the message id, as a fixed code, never AWS's message.
Demo scripts or the walkthrough break on the second run (fixed mobiles, taken slots, wall-clock days)mediummediumFresh random synthetic mobiles and runtime slot discovery. Separate branches per script (loader BEL/UMH, smoke SAN, demo-409 POL); the end-to-end specs pick a random branch, service and free time, and book again on a 409. A demo-numbers table and 'use a different number per booking' in the README. CI runs smoke twice, before and after the end-to-end tests.
Bots book every slot, or someone SMS-pumps random numbershighmediumADR-0004 records the production controls: OTP before CONFIRMED (or booking inside the authenticated app), per-recipient and per-prefix SMS caps, CAPTCHA, premium-prefix blocking, cost alerting. The per-recipient cap is built (BR-17): an SMS past the cap for its number in 24 hours is FAILED and never sent, counted in a ledger of the number's keyed fingerprints that outlives the messages, so booking and cancelling in turn meets the cap too. The hosted demo, which asks for no sign-in, texts real SMS, so it sets the cap at five a day, lets AWS text South Africa only, allows 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes through AWS WAF, refuses any message dearer than US$0.20, and stops at AWS's monthly SMS spend limit; while the account is in the SMS sandbox, AWS texts only verified numbers. The demo numbers are never texted. Anyone can still make it text any South African number, up to five a day.
lock_timeout 2 s fires on a legitimate wait and the customer sees 503lowlowThe booking transaction takes tens of milliseconds, and the dispatcher locks one row at a time on its own thread with SKIP LOCKED. 503 TRY_AGAIN carries Retry-After, and the client retries once with the same Idempotency-Key, which replays the 201 if the first request won. Documented in docs/errors.md and the runbook.
Holiday or hours data is wrong (4 Nov 2026 proclamation unverifiable, representative branch hours) or the demo loader fails start-uplowmediumpublic_holidays carries a source column, and the proclamation row was verified and is cited. HolidayCoverageCheck logs a WARN from 60 days before the booking window passes the end of the holiday data, and again once it has, at start-up and daily at 06:00 SAST. Hours are labelled representative in the README. DemoDataLoader (Tier 2, built in M6) picks the next open day through SlotCalendar, runs only when the table is empty, on a thread of its own so it never delays readiness, and logs instead of failing start-up.
The hosted demo's database certificate is not verified: sslmode=require encrypts the connection to RDS but does not check the server's certificatelowmediumAccepted for the demo (SECURITY.md): the database, encrypted and refusing any connection without TLS (rds.force_ssl), sits in isolated subnets with no internet route and admits only the task's security group. The production control is sslmode=verify-full with the RDS certificate bundle in the image.
A visitor types a real person's name and number into the public demomediummediumEvery page says the bank is fictional and carries noindex; the banner and /privacy, which gives the POPIA notice, say that the hosted demo texts the number given and never the demo numbers, and ask visitors to use only their own number; the notice also names what outlives a booking. Each booking is deleted, with its messages and idempotency keys, within the hour after its appointment ends (BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED), or at once when it is cancelled, all but its cancellation SMS until that is final, and backups that could still hold it expire after 7 days. The inbox is off, so no visitor sees another's booking, and one number gets at most five SMS a day. Accepting only the demo numbers (BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY) is built, and off since the demo texts real SMS.
The hosted demo's SMS reach only verified numbers while the AWS account is in the SMS sandboxhighlowThe banner says so: only numbers verified on the account receive the text, and any other shows Not delivered. The booking still stands: AWS's refusal of an unverified number is permanent, so the text ends FAILED on its first attempt, about 2 seconds after the booking, and the confirmation page says Not delivered. The demo numbers stay simulated, so the README's examples behave as they do locally. Production access has been requested; until AWS grants it, the sandbox also caps SMS spend at US$1 a month.

Pitfalls to avoid

  • Allocating seats as count + 1, or any read-then-insert without the branch_services row lock: a cancellation gap at capacity > 1 produces false 409s, and the race can only be closed in the database.
  • Relying on which unique index PostgreSQL reports first, on @Version alone, or on parsing constraint names out of message text. The EXCLUDE constraints must be real, named, read via ServerErrorMessage.getConstraint(), mapped to 409 and proven by the race and bypass tests.
  • Placing the Idempotency-Key check after seat allocation or as a nullable unique column on appointments, claiming that a SELECT blocks on the key, or continuing a retry inside an aborted PostgreSQL transaction.
  • Returning existingReference, or any reference, branch or time, in a problem body: anyone who knows a cellphone number could harvest and cancel that person's appointments.
  • Printing '082 *** 0101' next to a reference in /messages, logs or MDC: the last four digits are the unlock factor.
  • A per-customer UNIQUE index with no time bound when nothing in v1 ever moves a past appointment out of CONFIRMED: every returning customer is locked out permanently.
  • Writing the outbox row from @TransactionalEventListener(AFTER_COMMIT) or a Spring event, sending on the request thread, logging instead of persisting, or marking SENT before a send was attempted.
  • Claiming, sending and marking a batch of 20 in one transaction: one database error rolls back every mark and re-sends every SMS each tick. Equally, never claim in one transaction and process in another.
  • Letting a late 'confirmed' SMS follow a cancellation, letting delivery status change appointment status, or adding Kafka, WebSockets, Mailpit or a real provider before the outbox is durable, retried and visible.
  • Calling now() in application SQL while tests drive a MutableClock: backoff, expiry and claim tests then sleep or prove nothing.
  • Mixing JPA write-behind with JdbcClient statements without an explicit flush after the appointment persist: step 10's FK fails and constraint violations surface at commit as the wrong exception.
  • Naming a test *Test when it starts the application or a PostgreSQL container, which breaks the Docker-free unit run the README promises (SurefireSuiteIsDockerFreeTest catches it, and requires every test that starts the application to end in IT).
  • Demo scripts, specs or a walkthrough that only work once: fixed mobiles, hard-coded slots or dates, or the demo loader occupying the race branch.
  • Shipping currency tells or making late toolchain changes: Spring Boot 3.5.x, latest image tags, unpinned versions, H2 in tests, ddl-auto=update, Node without a lockfile, or any JDK, dependency or image change after hour 30.
  • Repo hygiene failures: committed .env, node_modules, target, .vite or web/dist; one squashed commit or 'wip' commits; Dependabot PRs piling up after the tag; a README command never re-run from a clean clone on a second machine on submission day.
  • Impersonating or over-collecting: a real bank's name, logo, brand colours or e-mail addresses; seeded Admin@123-style credentials; ID numbers, addresses or dates of birth; PII in logs (including framework and Postgres logs), error bodies or fixtures; a consent checkbox or marketing field; collecting an email the system never uses.
  • JVM-default-timezone arithmetic, adding +02:00 by hand, relying on a Jackson property for offsets, or a UI that formats with the device zone.
  • Letting a configurable public URL push the SMS past 160 GSM-7 characters, so a tight CHECK fails bookings: check the worst case from the sms_name column bounds with the configured URL at startup and keep the database bound a coarse 320-character guard.
  • Building beyond the vertical slice: staff/admin endpoints, login, customers/audit/closures tables, split DB roles, NO_SHOW or retention jobs, reschedule, reminders, .ics, Kubernetes, Terraform, a sleeping free-tier demo.
  • Polish or toolchain adventures before the tracer bullet and the proof tests are green: dark mode, i18n, Trivy/arm64/axe pipelines beyond one scan, coverage gates, more than four ArchUnit rules.
  • A README that is an essay or a lie: over 300 lines, generic feature lists, emoji bullets, badges for things not set up, features listed that do not exist, test names that check-test-refs.sh cannot find.
  • Tests that prove nothing: getter tests, all-mocked service tests, happy path only, 'concurrent' tests that never actually interleave, no test touching real PostgreSQL, or a coverage gate used as theatre.
  • Front-end neglect: missing loading/empty/error/stale states, a confirmation that is a toast, no 409 recovery, an inaccessible slot grid, any in TypeScript, inline styles that the CSP blocks only in Docker, dead buttons, console errors.
  • Deviating from the canonical names (tables, states, endpoints, headers, codes, reference format, test names) without amending the spine, or hard-coding dates such as 'tomorrow 09:00' in demo data that lands on a holiday.

DeployBuilt

Deployment on AWS with CDK

Status. Built and deployed: BranchbookAccount (us-east-1), which the administrator deploys by hand, and in af-south-1 BranchbookRelease and the hosted demo's BranchbookData and BranchbookApp, which the pipeline deploys on every push to main once every check is green, the demo stacks since 27 October 2026. The demo was first deployed by hand on 2026-10-26, is live at https://branchbook.click, and runs two tasks. BranchbookApp also holds the SMS protect configuration through which the tasks text South African numbers; the account is still in the SMS sandbox, and production access has been requested. The planned BranchbookDns was not needed, because Route 53 created the hosted zone when the domain was registered. The cost table is an estimate for two tasks, not a bill.

The hosted demo runs in AWS Cape Town, and AWS CDK owns every resource except the domain and its hosted zone, which Route 53 created when branchbook.click was registered. make up stays the gate. The hosted demo has run at https://branchbook.click since 2026-10-26, before M6, behind the guardrails in this group. Its confirmation and cancellation SMS go to the number given, South African numbers only, through AWS End User Messaging SMS in Cape Town; the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 0199 are never texted, and while the account is in the SMS sandbox only verified numbers receive a text. It deletes each booking once its appointment is over, or at once when it is cancelled.

What CDK controls

The stacks are split by lifecycle, so the app can be torn down and redeployed without touching the database. Four exist, all deployed: BranchbookAccount, BranchbookRelease, BranchbookData and BranchbookApp. There is no DNS stack: Route 53 created the hosted zone when the domain was registered.

StackRegionDeployedWhat it owns
BranchbookAccountus-east-1By hand, by an administrator, and again after a budget change; never by the pipelineGitHub OIDC provider and the deploy role CI assumes; the monthly cost budget with e-mail alerts. IAM and Budgets are global, and this stack holds no customer data.
BranchbookReleaseaf-south-1Every push to main, by the pipeline, lastThe SSM parameter /branchbook/release: the last commit the pipeline deployed to the hosted demo and smoke-tested there. A hand deploy since is not recorded.
BranchbookDataaf-south-1Every push to main, by the pipeline (first by hand, 2026-10-26); destroy keeps a final snapshotVPC across 2 AZs with public and isolated subnets and no NAT gateway; RDS for PostgreSQL 17.11 on db.t4g.micro, Single-AZ, encrypted, private, 7-day backups; a parameter group with rds.force_ssl=1, so only TLS connections are accepted; PostgreSQL's own log is not exported; the generated credentials secret branchbook/database; and the generated secret branchbook/sms-cap-key, 48 letters and digits, the HMAC key of the SMS daily cap's ledger, deleted with the stack.
BranchbookAppaf-south-1Every push to main, by the pipeline (first by hand, 2026-10-26)ECS cluster and two always-on Fargate tasks (ARM64, 0.5 vCPU, 1 GB each), one in each Availability Zone, built from the repo Dockerfile, with the SMS cap's key injected from branchbook/sms-cap-key; ALB (HTTPS on the TLS 1.3 policy, plain HTTP redirected to it) with an ACM certificate and the apex alias record; WAF /actuator block, a limit of 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes, both on the URL-decoded, lower-cased path, and three AWS managed rule groups; an AWS End User Messaging SMS protect configuration that allows SMS to South Africa only, and the task role's permission to send through it and nothing else; CloudWatch logs (7 days), alarms and an SNS e-mail topic.

That is about R1,900 a month at R18 to the dollar, or about R2,185 with 15% VAT. New AWS accounts get $100 in credit at sign-up and up to $100 more for onboarding tasks: about 4.1 weeks of hosting for the first $100 and 8.2 weeks for both. Add the domain, registered through Route 53, at about $3 a year. The WAF line counts three rules; the built web ACL has five, three of them AWS managed rule groups, so expect a few dollars more. Real SMS are charged on top: AWS refuses any message dearer than $0.20, and the account's monthly SMS spend limit caps the total, $1 while the account is in the SMS sandbox; once a monthly spend limit is reached, each further SMS fails at once, with no retries, until the month ends. Unit prices come from the AWS price list, checked in October 2026. The WAF line is an estimate, and x86 instead of ARM64 would add about $9.70 for the two tasks. The budget is $110 a month, with e-mails at $55, $88 and $110.

Time, and how it fits the plan

WhenHours
In M0, as you write it0.3
In M00.6
In M30.5
In M50.8
After M6 is green9.4
M71.0
One-off: account, Region, domain, bootstrap and DNS1.5
First deploy and fixing what it finds1.5
Total15.6

This adds about 16 hours to a 35-hour plan. The small structural changes, about 2 hours, go in while you build, because they cost far more to retrofit. The hosted demo itself went live on 2026-10-26, after the booking UI (M4) and before M6. Since 27 October 2026 the deploy job of ci.yml, once every check passes, deploys the data and app stacks on every push to main, smoke-tests the live address, and only then records the commit in /branchbook/release; before that, the administrator deployed them by hand. Never let the hosted demo delay the tag.

Why this shape and not another

OptionWhy not
Free tiers (Render Free, Neon Free, Koyeb free, Fly trial)They sleep or run out of quota mid-review. The dispatcher then stops, and confirmations stay PENDING with no error.
AWS App RunnerIt is closed to new customers since 30 April 2026. It also throttles CPU when no request is in flight, which starves the dispatcher.
RDS Proxy or any transaction-mode poolerThey reject or pin the JDBC options parameter that sets lock_timeout and statement_timeout, and break Flyway's session advisory lock. Two tasks with a 10-connection pool each need no pooler.
Aurora DSQL, CockroachDB, YugabyteDBThey have no btree_gist, no EXCLUDE constraints and no SKIP LOCKED or advisory locks, so the schema and the booking transaction cannot run.
EKS for the demoThe $73 a month control plane and the cluster operations buy nothing for two tasks. The production path documents EKS instead.
EC2 with CaddyAt about $39 a month it costs half as much, but the host, patching and TLS become yours, and deploys drop traffic for 30 to 90 seconds. ECS rolls deploys behind readiness, and CDK models it cleanly.

DeployBuilt

The CDK app

Status. Built: account-stack.ts, release-stack.ts, data-stack.ts, app-stack.ts and sms-countries.ts, with Jest tests; dns-stack.ts was never needed. infra/ wins where the sketches below differ: the account stack trusts the repository by its immutable owner and repository IDs, uses OidcProviderNative, takes the alert address from BRANCHBOOK_ALERT_EMAIL, and lets the deploy role read /branchbook/release; the demo stacks exist in the app only when BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO is true, as the pipeline's deploy job sets it, ask for no sign-in, redirect plain HTTP to HTTPS, run two tasks, configure them with environment variables instead of Spring profiles, give them the SMS cap's key from Secrets Manager, and let them text South African numbers only, through an AWS End User Messaging SMS protect configuration.

Scaffold the folder with npx cdk init app --language typescript, keep the versions it pins on that day, and replace the generated files with these. TypeScript is the natural fit: the CDK CLI runs on Node anyway, and web/ already uses Node 24 and TypeScript.

Settled on the first deploy. Cape Town offers PostgreSQL 17.11, the version make up runs, and it is the constant POSTGRES_VERSION in data-stack.ts rather than a cdk.json value. The other open question, a WAF basic-auth gate or an in-app fallback, no longer applies: the demo asks for no sign-in, because a sign-in prompt in front of a demo turns away the people it is for.

infra/cdk.json

Context values, as planned. The built file holds the repository's public IDs, the GitHub environment, the budget, $110 since the second task, demoDomain and demoHostedZoneId; the PostgreSQL version is a constant in data-stack.ts, and the alert addresses come from the environment.

{
  "app": "npx tsc && npx tsx bin/branchbook.ts",
  "context": {
    "domainName": "branchbook.co.za",
    "githubRepo": "<github-owner>/<repo>",
    "alertEmail": "<you>@example.com",
    "postgresVersion": "<newest 17.x that af-south-1 lists>",
    "monthlyBudgetUsd": 90
  }
