infra/bin/branchbook.ts

The CDK app as planned. The built app has four stacks, all deployed, BranchbookAccount, BranchbookRelease, BranchbookData and BranchbookApp, and no DNS stack; the last two are in the app only when BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO is true, as the pipeline's deploy job sets it, and BranchbookData hands the SMS cap's key to BranchbookApp. Everything that holds data is in Cape Town. BRANCHBOOK_SMS_AWS_DRY_RUN=true at deploy time deploys a task that asks AWS to check each SMS without sending it.

#!/usr/bin/env node
import { App, Tags } from 'aws-cdk-lib/core';
import { AccountStack } from '../lib/account-stack';
import { DnsStack } from '../lib/dns-stack';
import { DataStack } from '../lib/data-stack';
import { AppStack } from '../lib/app-stack';

const app = new App();
const ctx = (key: string): string => app.node.getContext(key);
const account = process.env.CDK_DEFAULT_ACCOUNT;
const capeTown = { account, region: 'af-south-1' };

new AccountStack(app, 'BranchbookAccount', {
  env: { account, region: 'us-east-1' }, // IAM and Budgets are global; no customer data here
  githubRepo: ctx('githubRepo'),
  alertEmail: ctx('alertEmail'),
  monthlyBudgetUsd: Number(ctx('monthlyBudgetUsd')),
});
const dns = new DnsStack(app, 'BranchbookDns', { env: capeTown, domainName: ctx('domainName') });
const data = new DataStack(app, 'BranchbookData', { env: capeTown, postgresVersion: ctx('postgresVersion') });
new AppStack(app, 'BranchbookApp', {
  env: capeTown,
  domainName: ctx('domainName'),
  zone: dns.zone,
  vpc: data.vpc,
  db: data.db,
  alertEmail: ctx('alertEmail'),
  appVersion: process.env.GITHUB_SHA ?? 'local',
});
Tags.of(app).add('project', 'branchbook');

infra/lib/account-stack.ts

GitHub OIDC trust for one repository environment, and the cost budget.

import { Duration, Stack, StackProps } from 'aws-cdk-lib';
import { Construct } from 'constructs';
import * as budgets from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-budgets';
import * as iam from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-iam';

export interface AccountStackProps extends StackProps {
  githubRepo: string;       // owner/repo
  alertEmail: string;
  monthlyBudgetUsd: number;
}

export class AccountStack extends Stack {
  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: AccountStackProps) {
    super(scope, id, props);

    const github = new iam.OidcProviderNative(this, 'GitHubOidcProvider', {
      url: 'https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com',
      clientIds: ['sts.amazonaws.com'],
    });

    // Only the aws-demo environment of this one repository can assume the role.
    const deployRole = new iam.Role(this, 'DeployRole', {
      roleName: 'branchbook-github-deploy',
      maxSessionDuration: Duration.hours(1),
      assumedBy: new iam.WebIdentityPrincipal(github.openIdConnectProviderArn, {
        StringEquals: {
          'token.actions.githubusercontent.com:aud': 'sts.amazonaws.com',
          'token.actions.githubusercontent.com:sub': githubSubject(props), // repo:OWNER@OWNER_ID/REPO@REPO_ID:environment:ENV
        },
      }),
    });
    // CDK deploys through the roles that `cdk bootstrap` created; this role may only assume those.
    deployRole.addToPolicy(new iam.PolicyStatement({
      actions: ['sts:AssumeRole'],
      resources: [`arn:${this.partition}:iam::${this.account}:role/cdk-hnb659fds-*`],
    }));

    new budgets.CfnBudget(this, 'MonthlyBudget', {
      budget: {
        budgetName: 'branchbook-monthly',
        budgetType: 'COST',
        timeUnit: 'MONTHLY',
        budgetLimit: { amount: props.monthlyBudgetUsd, unit: 'USD' },
      },
      notificationsWithSubscribers: [50, 80, 100].map((threshold) => ({
        notification: {
          notificationType: 'ACTUAL',
          comparisonOperator: 'GREATER_THAN',
          threshold,
          thresholdType: 'PERCENTAGE',
        },
        subscribers: [{ subscriptionType: 'EMAIL', address: props.alertEmail }],
      })),
    });
  }
}

infra/lib/dns-stack.ts

Not built. Route 53 created the hosted zone when branchbook.click was registered through it, so no stack owns DNS and no name servers need delegating; the zone's ID is in cdk.json.

import { CfnOutput, Fn, Stack, StackProps } from 'aws-cdk-lib';
import { Construct } from 'constructs';
import * as route53 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-route53';

export interface DnsStackProps extends StackProps {
  domainName: string;
}

export class DnsStack extends Stack {
  readonly zone: route53.PublicHostedZone;

  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: DnsStackProps) {
    super(scope, id, props);
    this.zone = new route53.PublicHostedZone(this, 'Zone', { zoneName: props.domainName });
    // Copy these into the .co.za registrar once (NS delegation).
    new CfnOutput(this, 'NameServers', { value: Fn.join(' ', this.zone.hostedZoneNameServers!) });
  }
}

infra/lib/data-stack.ts

VPC without NAT, and PostgreSQL 17 with the PII-safe parameter group. As built, the parameter group sets only rds.force_ssl=1, and PostgreSQL's own log is not exported, because a constraint error's DETAIL line can quote a mobile number. It also generates branchbook/sms-cap-key into Secrets Manager, 48 letters and digits: the HMAC key under which the SMS daily cap's ledger counts each number, kept with the database so that a new task or a redeployed app stack keeps every count, and deleted with the stack.

import { Duration, RemovalPolicy, Stack, StackProps } from 'aws-cdk-lib';
import { Construct } from 'constructs';
import * as ec2 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ec2';
import * as rds from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-rds';

export interface DataStackProps extends StackProps {
  postgresVersion: string; // a 17.x minor that af-south-1 offers, e.g. from describe-db-engine-versions
}

export class DataStack extends Stack {
  readonly vpc: ec2.Vpc;
  readonly db: rds.DatabaseInstance;

  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: DataStackProps) {
    super(scope, id, props);

    this.vpc = new ec2.Vpc(this, 'Vpc', {
      maxAzs: 2,
      natGateways: 0, // tasks run in public subnets behind a locked-down security group
      subnetConfiguration: [
        { name: 'public', subnetType: ec2.SubnetType.PUBLIC, cidrMask: 24 },
        { name: 'data', subnetType: ec2.SubnetType.PRIVATE_ISOLATED, cidrMask: 24 },
      ],
    });

    const engine = rds.DatabaseInstanceEngine.postgres({
      version: rds.PostgresEngineVersion.of(props.postgresVersion, '17'),
    });

    // PII hygiene on the server side: no statement text or DETAIL lines with customer data in PostgreSQL logs.
    const parameterGroup = new rds.ParameterGroup(this, 'Params', {
      engine,
      parameters: {
        log_error_verbosity: 'terse',
        log_min_error_statement: 'panic',
        log_parameter_max_length: '0',
        'rds.force_ssl': '1',
      },
    });

    this.db = new rds.DatabaseInstance(this, 'Postgres', {
      engine,
      parameterGroup,
      instanceType: ec2.InstanceType.of(ec2.InstanceClass.BURSTABLE4_GRAVITON, ec2.InstanceSize.MICRO),
      vpc: this.vpc,
      vpcSubnets: { subnetType: ec2.SubnetType.PRIVATE_ISOLATED },
      credentials: rds.Credentials.fromGeneratedSecret('branchbook_owner'),
      databaseName: 'branchbook',
      allocatedStorage: 20,
      maxAllocatedStorage: 40,
      storageType: rds.StorageType.GP3,
      storageEncrypted: true,
      multiAz: false,
      publiclyAccessible: false,
      backupRetention: Duration.days(7),
      autoMinorVersionUpgrade: false, // CDK owns the version: bump postgresVersion in cdk.json and deploy
      deletionProtection: false,
      removalPolicy: RemovalPolicy.SNAPSHOT,
    });
  }
}

infra/lib/app-stack.ts

The always-on task, HTTPS load balancer, health checks, WAF guardrails and alarms. As built: two always-on tasks, which ECS spreads across the two Availability Zones, so one can fail or be replaced while the other serves; an HTTPS listener, with plain HTTP redirected to it; no sign-in; the simulated inbox off, with references redacted should it be switched on; the /actuator block first, then a limit of 10 booking and cancel POSTs per IP in 5 minutes, both on the URL-decoded, lower-cased path, then three AWS managed rule groups; real SMS through an AWS End User Messaging SMS protect configuration that allows SMS to South Africa only and blocks every voice and MMS destination, with sms-voice:SendTextMessage on it alone for the task role and at most five SMS a day to one number, counted under the key the task receives from branchbook/sms-cap-key as BRANCHBOOK_SMS_RECIPIENT_CAP_KEY; the retention job on and the demo-number check off; only health and info served by actuator; environment variables in place of Spring profiles; no container /livez check; and alarms on 5xx answers, no healthy task and less than 2 GB of database storage.

import * as path from 'node:path';
import { CfnOutput, Duration, RemovalPolicy, Stack, StackProps } from 'aws-cdk-lib';
import { Construct } from 'constructs';
import * as acm from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-certificatemanager';
import * as cloudwatch from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-cloudwatch';
import * as cwActions from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-cloudwatch-actions';
import * as ec2 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ec2';
import { Platform } from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ecr-assets';
import * as ecs from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ecs';
import * as ecsPatterns from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ecs-patterns';
import * as elbv2 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-elasticloadbalancingv2';
import * as logs from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-logs';
import * as rds from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-rds';
import * as route53 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-route53';
import * as sns from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-sns';
import * as subs from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-sns-subscriptions';
import * as wafv2 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-wafv2';

export interface AppStackProps extends StackProps {
  domainName: string;
  zone: route53.IHostedZone;
  vpc: ec2.IVpc;
  db: rds.DatabaseInstance;
  alertEmail: string;
  appVersion: string;
  image?: ecs.ContainerImage; // tests pass a stub; real deploys build the repo Dockerfile
}

export class AppStack extends Stack {
  constructor(scope: Construct, id: string, props: AppStackProps) {
    super(scope, id, props);

    // Gate H8: the SMS template only fits one segment with a base URL of 28 characters or fewer.
    const baseUrl = `https://${props.domainName}`;
    if (baseUrl.length > 28) {
      throw new Error(`${baseUrl} is ${baseUrl.length} characters; the SMS allows 28 characters`);
    }
    const basicAuth = process.env.DEMO_BASIC_AUTH; // "reviewer:long-passphrase", from a GitHub secret
    if (!basicAuth || !basicAuth.includes(':')) {
      throw new Error('Set DEMO_BASIC_AUTH=user:passphrase before cdk synth or deploy');
    }

    const certificate = new acm.Certificate(this, 'Cert', {
      domainName: props.domainName,
      validation: acm.CertificateValidation.fromDns(props.zone),
    });
    const logGroup = new logs.LogGroup(this, 'AppLogs', {
      retention: logs.RetentionDays.ONE_WEEK,
      removalPolicy: RemovalPolicy.DESTROY,
    });
    const dbSecret = props.db.secret!;

    const web = new ecsPatterns.ApplicationLoadBalancedFargateService(this, 'Web', {
      cluster: new ecs.Cluster(this, 'Cluster', { vpc: props.vpc }),
      desiredCount: 1,          // one always-on task: the outbox dispatcher needs CPU between requests
      minHealthyPercent: 100,   // a deploy starts the new task before the old one stops
      maxHealthyPercent: 200,
      cpu: 512,
      memoryLimitMiB: 1024,
      runtimePlatform: {
        cpuArchitecture: ecs.CpuArchitecture.ARM64,
        operatingSystemFamily: ecs.OperatingSystemFamily.LINUX,
      },
      taskSubnets: { subnetType: ec2.SubnetType.PUBLIC },
      assignPublicIp: true,     // no NAT gateway; the task security group admits only the ALB
      publicLoadBalancer: true,
      protocol: elbv2.ApplicationProtocol.HTTPS,
      certificate,
      sslPolicy: elbv2.SslPolicy.RECOMMENDED_TLS,
      redirectHTTP: true,
      domainName: props.domainName,
      domainZone: props.zone,
      healthCheckGracePeriod: Duration.seconds(180), // Flyway migrates before readiness turns UP
      circuitBreaker: { rollback: true },
      taskImageOptions: {
        containerName: 'app',
        containerPort: 8080,
        image: props.image ?? ecs.ContainerImage.fromAsset(path.join(__dirname, '..', '..'), {
          platform: Platform.LINUX_ARM64,
          exclude: ['infra', '.git', '**/node_modules', '**/target', 'web/dist'],
        }),
        logDriver: ecs.LogDrivers.awsLogs({ streamPrefix: 'app', logGroup }),
        environment: {
          SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE: 'prod,demo',
          DB_URL: `jdbc:postgresql://${props.db.dbInstanceEndpointAddress}:${props.db.dbInstanceEndpointPort}/branchbook`,
          DB_SSLMODE: 'require',
          BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_PUBLIC_BASE_URL: baseUrl,
          APP_VERSION: props.appVersion,
          JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS: '-XX:MaxRAMPercentage=70 -XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError',
          TZ: 'UTC',
        },
        secrets: { // injected at task start, so a restart always reads the current value
          DB_USER: ecs.Secret.fromSecretsManager(dbSecret, 'username'),
          DB_PASSWORD: ecs.Secret.fromSecretsManager(dbSecret, 'password'),
        },
      },
    });

    web.targetGroup.configureHealthCheck({
      path: '/readyz',
      healthyHttpCodes: '200',
      interval: Duration.seconds(15),
      timeout: Duration.seconds(5),
      healthyThresholdCount: 2,
      unhealthyThresholdCount: 3,
    });
    web.targetGroup.setAttribute('deregistration_delay.timeout_seconds', '30');

    // Liveness: ECS replaces the task if /livez fails, which includes the dispatcher's last tick.
    const taskDef = web.taskDefinition.node.defaultChild as ecs.CfnTaskDefinition;
    taskDef.addPropertyOverride('ContainerDefinitions.0.HealthCheck', {
      Command: ['CMD-SHELL', 'wget -q -O /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:8080/livez || exit 1'],
      Interval: 30,
      Timeout: 5,
      Retries: 3,
      StartPeriod: 180,
    });

    web.service.connections.allowToDefaultPort(props.db, 'app to PostgreSQL');

    // Edge guardrails: reviewer gate, booking rate limit, no actuator from the internet.
    const visibility = (metricName: string) => ({ cloudWatchMetricsEnabled: true, metricName, sampledRequestsEnabled: false });
    const exact = (field: object, value: string) => ({
      byteMatchStatement: { fieldToMatch: field, positionalConstraint: 'EXACTLY', searchString: value,
        textTransformations: [{ priority: 0, type: 'NONE' }] },
    });
    const acl = new wafv2.CfnWebACL(this, 'Waf', {
      scope: 'REGIONAL',
      defaultAction: { allow: {} },
      visibilityConfig: visibility('branchbook'),
      customResponseBodies: {
        gate: { contentType: 'TEXT_PLAIN', content: 'BranchBook demo. Sign in with the reviewer credentials from the submission e-mail.' },
      },
      rules: [
        {
          name: 'demo-gate', priority: 0,
          statement: { notStatement: { statement: exact({ singleHeader: { Name: 'authorization' } },
            `Basic ${Buffer.from(basicAuth).toString('base64')}`) } },
          action: { block: { customResponse: { responseCode: 401, customResponseBodyKey: 'gate',
            responseHeaders: [{ name: 'www-authenticate', value: 'Basic realm="BranchBook demo"' }] } } },
          visibilityConfig: visibility('gate'),
        },
        {
          name: 'booking-rate-limit', priority: 1,
          statement: { rateBasedStatement: { limit: 30, evaluationWindowSec: 300, aggregateKeyType: 'IP',
            scopeDownStatement: { andStatement: { statements: [
              exact({ method: {} }, 'POST'),
              exact({ uriPath: {} }, '/api/v1/appointments'),
            ] } } } },
          action: { block: {} },
          visibilityConfig: visibility('booking-rate'),
        },
        {
          name: 'no-actuator', priority: 2,
          statement: { byteMatchStatement: { fieldToMatch: { uriPath: {} }, positionalConstraint: 'STARTS_WITH',
            searchString: '/actuator', textTransformations: [{ priority: 0, type: 'LOWERCASE' }] } },
          action: { block: {} },
          visibilityConfig: visibility('no-actuator'),
        },
      ],
    });
    new wafv2.CfnWebACLAssociation(this, 'WafOnAlb', {
      resourceArn: web.loadBalancer.loadBalancerArn,
      webAclArn: acl.attrArn,
    });

    const alerts = new sns.Topic(this, 'Alerts');
    alerts.addSubscription(new subs.EmailSubscription(props.alertEmail));
    const alarmOn = (id: string, metric: cloudwatch.IMetric, threshold: number) =>
      new cloudwatch.Alarm(this, id, {
        metric,
        threshold,
        evaluationPeriods: 2,
        comparisonOperator: cloudwatch.ComparisonOperator.GREATER_THAN_OR_EQUAL_TO_THRESHOLD,
        treatMissingData: cloudwatch.TreatMissingData.NOT_BREACHING,
      }).addAlarmAction(new cwActions.SnsAction(alerts));
    alarmOn('UnhealthyTask', web.targetGroup.metrics.unhealthyHostCount({ period: Duration.minutes(1) }), 1);
    alarmOn('Target5xx', web.loadBalancer.metrics.httpCodeTarget(elbv2.HttpCodeTarget.TARGET_5XX_COUNT,
      { period: Duration.minutes(5) }), 5);
    alarmOn('DbCpu', props.db.metricCPUUtilization({ period: Duration.minutes(5) }), 80);

    new CfnOutput(this, 'Url', { value: baseUrl });
  }
}

infra/lib/sms-countries.ts

Not in the plan; built with real SMS. It lists the 245 countries AWS End User Messaging reports for a protect configuration in af-south-1, as get-protect-configuration-country-rule-set returned them on 2026-10-27, and the voice and MMS lists. A protect configuration allows every country by default, so app-stack.ts blocks each one by name except South Africa; listing only codes AWS reports keeps a deploy from failing on one it does not know.

infra/test/hosted-demo.test.ts

Jest assertions that prove the decisions; the built file has 18 tests, among them that the protect configuration allows SMS to South Africa alone, that the task role may send through it and nothing else, that two tasks run, that the SMS cap's key reaches the task only as a secret, and that a dry-run deploy sets the switch. The infra job of ci.yml runs them on every push to main and every pull request, and the deploy job waits for it; a hand deploy of the demo stacks does not run them.

import { App } from 'aws-cdk-lib';
import { Match, Template } from 'aws-cdk-lib/assertions';
import * as ecs from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ecs';
import { DnsStack } from '../lib/dns-stack';
import { DataStack } from '../lib/data-stack';
import { AppStack } from '../lib/app-stack';

const env = { account: '111111111111', region: 'af-south-1' };

function synth(domainName = 'branchbook.co.za') {
  process.env.DEMO_BASIC_AUTH = 'reviewer:test-only';
  const app = new App();
  const dns = new DnsStack(app, 'Dns', { env, domainName });
  const data = new DataStack(app, 'Data', { env, postgresVersion: '17.6' });
  const web = new AppStack(app, 'App', {
    env, domainName, zone: dns.zone, vpc: data.vpc, db: data.db,
    alertEmail: 'ops@example.com', appVersion: 'test',
    image: ecs.ContainerImage.fromRegistry('public.ecr.aws/docker/library/busybox:stable'),
  });
  return { data: Template.fromStack(data), app: Template.fromStack(web) };
}

test('PostgreSQL 17 is private, encrypted, logs no customer data and has no NAT gateway', () => {
  const { data } = synth();
  data.hasResourceProperties('AWS::RDS::DBInstance', {
    Engine: 'postgres', EngineVersion: Match.stringLikeRegexp('^17'),
    StorageEncrypted: true, PubliclyAccessible: false, MultiAZ: false, AutoMinorVersionUpgrade: false,
  });
  data.hasResourceProperties('AWS::RDS::DBParameterGroup', {
    Family: 'postgres17',
    Parameters: Match.objectLike({ log_error_verbosity: 'terse', log_min_error_statement: 'panic' }),
  });
  data.resourceCountIs('AWS::EC2::NatGateway', 0);
});

test('exactly one always-on ARM64 task behind HTTPS, checked on /readyz', () => {
  const { app } = synth();
  app.hasResourceProperties('AWS::ECS::Service', { DesiredCount: 1 });
  app.hasResourceProperties('AWS::ECS::TaskDefinition', {
    Cpu: '512', Memory: '1024',
    RuntimePlatform: { CpuArchitecture: 'ARM64', OperatingSystemFamily: 'LINUX' },
  });
  app.hasResourceProperties('AWS::ElasticLoadBalancingV2::TargetGroup', { HealthCheckPath: '/readyz' });
  app.hasResourceProperties('AWS::ElasticLoadBalancingV2::Listener', { Protocol: 'HTTPS' });
});

test('the WAF gate, rate limit and actuator block are attached to the load balancer', () => {
  const { app } = synth();
  app.hasResourceProperties('AWS::WAFv2::WebACL', {
    Scope: 'REGIONAL',
    Rules: Match.arrayWith([Match.objectLike({ Name: 'demo-gate' }), Match.objectLike({ Name: 'no-actuator' })]),
  });
  app.resourceCountIs('AWS::WAFv2::WebACLAssociation', 1);
});

test('a domain that would overflow the one-segment SMS fails at synth time', () => {
  expect(() => synth('branchbook-demo.example.co.za')).toThrow(/28 characters/);
});

DeployPartly built

First deploy, pipeline and teardown

Status. Done: bootstrap, BranchbookAccount, the aws-demo environment, and the first hand deploy of BranchbookData and BranchbookApp on 2026-10-26; the site asks for no sign-in, plain HTTP is redirected to HTTPS, and /actuator answers 403. Since 27 October 2026 the deploy job of ci.yml, once api, web, stack and infra pass, deploys BranchbookData and BranchbookApp on every push to main, smoke-tests https://branchbook.click, then deploys BranchbookRelease and checks that /branchbook/release holds the pushed commit; deploy.yml and infra.yml are gone. For real SMS, the account is still in the SMS sandbox, and production access has been requested. Not built: scripts/smoke-aws.sh. Teardown is due on 2026-11-30.

Steps 1 to 7 happen once, and so does step 11. After that, every push to main deploys the hosted demo and then the release record through GitHub Actions with no AWS keys, once every check is green; rerunning step 8 by hand is the fallback when the pipeline cannot run.

  1. Register the domain on day 0. As built, branchbook.click was registered through Route 53 on 2026-10-26, for about $3 a year, in place of the planned branchbook.co.za; Route 53 creates its hosted zone on registration. Do it before the build starts: corporate web filters often block newly registered domains for weeks. If it is taken, pick another name whose https:// address stays within 28 characters.
  2. Create the AWS account. Sign up with the advanced option so you can pick Regions, stay on the Paid plan so the account never auto-closes, and claim the $100 sign-up credit plus up to $100 for the onboarding activities. Turn on MFA for the root user, create the IAM user branchbook-admin with AdministratorAccess and MFA, which signs in through aws login, then enable the opt-in Region Africa (Cape Town). Enabling can take a while, so do it a day ahead.
    aws login --profile branchbook-admin
  3. Find the PostgreSQL 17 version Cape Town offers. As built, it is 17.11, the version make up runs, set as POSTGRES_VERSION in data-stack.ts; RDS applies later minor versions itself (autoMinorVersionUpgrade).
    aws rds describe-db-engine-versions --engine postgres --region af-south-1 \
  --query "DBEngineVersions[?starts_with(EngineVersion,'17')].EngineVersion" --output text
  4. Create the CDK app and bootstrap both Regions. Scaffold infra/, replace the generated files with the ones below, then bootstrap. Bootstrapping creates the deploy roles and the asset bucket and ECR repository CDK uses.
    mkdir infra && cd infra && npx cdk init app --language typescript
npm install
npx jest
npx cdk bootstrap aws://<account-id>/af-south-1 aws://<account-id>/us-east-1 --profile branchbook-admin
  5. Deploy the long-lived stacks from your laptop. BranchbookAccount creates the GitHub deploy role and the budget. No DNS stack was needed: the hosted zone came with the domain's Route 53 registration. The pipeline never deploys this stack, so after a change to monthlyBudgetUsd in cdk.json, such as the rise to $110 for the second task, the administrator deploys it again the same way.
    BRANCHBOOK_ALERT_EMAIL=<address> npx cdk deploy BranchbookAccount --profile branchbook-admin
  6. Delegate DNS. Not needed as built: a domain registered through Route 53 is already delegated to its hosted zone. Check that it answers before the first deploy, because ACM validates the certificate through that zone.
    dig NS branchbook.click +short
  7. Wire GitHub to AWS. In the repository settings, create the environment aws-demo. Add the environment secret AWS_DEPLOY_ROLE_ARN (the stack output DeployRoleArn). The hosted demo needs no other secret: the pipeline's deploy job sets BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true itself, and reads the alarms' address from the optional environment secret BRANCHBOOK_DEMO_ALERT_EMAIL, not set today. The demo's own secrets, the database credentials and the SMS cap's key, are generated into Secrets Manager by BranchbookData. On a private repository on the Free plan, environments are not available: trust repo:<owner>/<repo>:ref:refs/tags/v* in AccountStack instead.
  8. First deploy of the data and app stacks. As built, it ran once by hand, on 2026-10-26, from the administrator's machine, signed in as branchbook-admin, with Docker running: cdk deploy built the ARM64 image locally and pushed it to the bootstrap ECR repository. Since 27 October 2026 the pipeline deploys both stacks on every push to main, building the image on an ARM runner, so the command below is the fallback when the pipeline cannot run, and the way to deploy a dry run. Nothing records a hand deploy, and the next push to main deploys main over it, with the alert address only if the aws-demo environment holds it. The alert address is optional. To prove the task may send SMS before anyone is texted, add BRANCHBOOK_SMS_AWS_DRY_RUN=true: AWS then only checks each send, and the app records an accepted one as SENT although nothing arrives, so make one test booking and deploy again without it. While the account is in the SMS sandbox, book with a verified number: AWS refuses even a dry run to an unverified one, and that message ends FAILED, its last error AWS_CONFLICT_DESTINATION_PHONE_NUMBER_NOT_VERIFIED. RDS takes about 10 to 15 minutes; the certificate and the first task take another 5 to 10.
    BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true \
BRANCHBOOK_DEMO_ALERT_EMAIL=<your address> \
  npx cdk deploy BranchbookData BranchbookApp --profile branchbook-admin
  9. Prove the edge and the app. Plain HTTP must answer 301 to HTTPS, the site must open with no sign-in, and /actuator must answer 403, even written /%61ctuator, because WAF decodes the path first. Book with a demo number and watch the SMS turn SENT on the confirmation page within about 4 seconds; the demo numbers stay on the simulated gateway. scripts/smoke.sh https://branchbook.click runs the read-only checks CI runs and counts WAF's 403 as a closed endpoint, and the pipeline runs it after every deploy, before it records the commit; scripts/smoke-aws.sh is not built, so the booking check is by hand.
    curl -sI http://branchbook.click/ | grep -iE "^HTTP|^location"   # 301 to HTTPS
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://branchbook.click/                   # 200, no sign-in
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://branchbook.click/actuator/health   # 403 from WAF
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://branchbook.click/%61ctuator/health # 403: WAF decodes the path
  10. Check the logs hold no personal data. Search the last hour of application logs for anything shaped like a South African mobile number. A real number need not start with 082, so add the number you booked with to the pattern too. Two kinds of match are expected: the start-up line that names the demo block, 082 555 0000 to 0199, and message ids that happen to contain 082. Anything else is personal data in the logs.
    aws logs filter-log-events --region af-south-1 --log-group-name /branchbook/app \
  --filter-pattern '?"082" ?"+2782"' --start-time $(($(date +%s) - 3600))000
  11. Let the demo text real numbers. The CDK app creates the protect configuration, the task role's permission and the SMS settings; the account owner does the rest in the AWS console, in af-south-1. A new account is in the SMS sandbox, where AWS texts only verified destination numbers, at most 10, and spends at most $1 a month: verify your own number in AWS End User Messaging SMS, book with it, and watch the SMS turn SENT. To text any other South African number, open an AWS Support case for production access in af-south-1, and ask in it for a higher monthly spend limit. Until AWS grants it, a text to an unverified number ends FAILED on its first attempt, shown as Not delivered on the confirmation page, and the booking still stands; the banner says only verified numbers get a text while the account is in the sandbox.

.github/workflows/deploy.yml, now the deploy job of ci.yml

The plan, below, had the pipeline deploy the data and app stacks and smoke-test them, and since 27 October 2026 it does, from the deploy job of ci.yml; deploy.yml and infra.yml are deleted. The job runs on a push to main or a manual run, never a pull request, and only once api, web, stack and infra have passed (needs). On an ubuntu-24.04-arm runner, so the ARM64 image is built natively, it signs in through OIDC, deploys BranchbookData and BranchbookApp with BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true, runs scripts/smoke.sh against https://branchbook.click, and only then deploys BranchbookRelease and fails unless /branchbook/release reads back the pushed commit. Unlike the plan, the Jest tests run in the infra job, there is no DEMO_BASIC_AUTH, because the demo asks for no sign-in, and the smoke test is the read-only smoke.sh, not smoke-aws.sh. It never sets BRANCHBOOK_ALERT_EMAIL, so BranchbookAccount, which defines its own role, is never in its CDK app (ADR-0007).

name: deploy
on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:
permissions:
  id-token: write         # GitHub OIDC token for AWS; no access keys stored anywhere
  contents: read
concurrency:
  group: deploy-aws
  cancel-in-progress: false
jobs:
  deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04   # native arm64 build for the Graviton task, no QEMU
    environment: aws-demo
    timeout-minutes: 30
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with:
          node-version: 24
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: infra/package-lock.json
      - uses: aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v6
        with:
          role-to-assume: ${{ secrets.AWS_DEPLOY_ROLE_ARN }}
          aws-region: af-south-1
      - name: Install the CDK app
        working-directory: infra
        run: npm ci
      - name: Unit-test the infrastructure
        working-directory: infra
        run: npm test -- --ci
      - name: Deploy data and app stacks
        working-directory: infra
        env:
          DEMO_BASIC_AUTH: ${{ secrets.DEMO_BASIC_AUTH }}
        run: npx cdk deploy BranchbookData BranchbookApp --require-approval never --ci --progress events
      - name: Smoke-test the version that is actually serving
        env:
          DEMO_BASIC_AUTH: ${{ secrets.DEMO_BASIC_AUTH }}
          EXPECTED_VERSION: ${{ github.sha }}
        run: scripts/smoke-aws.sh https://branchbook.co.za

scripts/smoke-aws.sh

Planned, not built: it needs the X-App-Version header, which does not exist, and a smoke.sh that books; the built one writes nothing, by design, and it is what the pipeline runs against the live demo. It would check the version, the actuator block and the SPA route, then book with the reserved smoke number and wait for SENT.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Usage: DEMO_BASIC_AUTH=user:pass EXPECTED_VERSION=<git sha> scripts/smoke-aws.sh https://branchbook.co.za
set -euo pipefail
BASE="$1"
AUTH="${DEMO_BASIC_AUTH:?set DEMO_BASIC_AUTH}"
WANT="${EXPECTED_VERSION:-}"

[ "${#BASE}" -le 28 ] || { echo "FAIL: $BASE is longer than 28 characters"; exit 1; }

code=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' "$BASE/")
[ "$code" = 401 ] || { echo "FAIL: expected 401 without credentials, got $code"; exit 1; }

got=""
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do   # up to 5 minutes for the rolling deploy
  got=$(curl -fsS -u "$AUTH" -D - -o /dev/null "$BASE/api/v1/branches" \
        | awk -F': ' 'tolower($1)=="x-app-version" {print $2}' | tr -d '\r')
  { [ -z "$WANT" ] || [ "$got" = "$WANT" ]; } && break
  sleep 5
done
[ -z "$WANT" ] || [ "$got" = "$WANT" ] || { echo "FAIL: serving $got, expected $WANT"; exit 1; }

code=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' -u "$AUTH" "$BASE/actuator/health")
[ "$code" = 403 ] || { echo "FAIL: /actuator should be blocked at the edge, got $code"; exit 1; }

curl -fsS -u "$AUTH" -o /dev/null "$BASE/manage"   # SPA route served from the jar

# Book with the reserved smoke number, wait for SENT, cancel (the existing script does the work).
BASE_URL="$BASE" CURL_AUTH="$AUTH" SMOKE_MOBILE="${SMOKE_MOBILE:-0825550199}" scripts/smoke.sh
echo "OK: $BASE is serving $got"

Teardown

Run this when the review window closes; the teardown date, 2026-11-30, is in infra/README.md. BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true must be set, because the demo stacks are in the CDK app only then. The pipeline deploys both stacks on every push to main, so take them out of ci.yml's deploy job first, or the next push re-creates the demo. The SMS cap's key, branchbook/sms-cap-key, is deleted with BranchbookData.

# after the review window: the app first, then the data (RDS keeps a final snapshot)
BRANCHBOOK_HOSTED_DEMO=true \
  npx cdk destroy BranchbookApp BranchbookData --profile branchbook-admin
aws rds describe-db-snapshots --region af-south-1 --snapshot-type manual \
  --query "DBSnapshots[].DBSnapshotIdentifier" --output text
aws rds delete-db-snapshot --region af-south-1 --db-snapshot-identifier <final-snapshot-id>
# CloudFormation keeps the log group /branchbook/app: delete it in CloudWatch
# no stack owns the domain or its hosted zone: turn off auto-renew in Route 53, delete the zone by hand
# verified SMS destination numbers belong to the account: delete them in AWS End User Messaging SMS
# keep BranchbookAccount for the budget alerts

DeployPartly built

App changes and guardrails for hosting

Status. Done: the exec ENTRYPOINT, the multi-platform build, the 20-second graceful shutdown, the /readyz and /livez probes, forwarded headers (set on the hosted task), the fictional-bank banner, the privacy page and the noindex meta tag (M4), and the edge guardrails: the booking rate limit, the /actuator block and the AWS managed rule groups, with the simulated inbox switched off. Also built: the retention job, which deletes each booking once its appointment is over; the demo-number check, off on the hosted demo since it began texting real SMS; and the real-SMS limits, South Africa only and five a day to one number, counted under a key from Secrets Manager that every task shares. Not built: the management port 9091, the version header, the dispatcher liveness indicator, the X-Robots-Tag header and robots.txt, and smoke-aws.sh.

The application needs a few changes to run safely behind a load balancer on the public internet. The first rows are cheap structural fixes that also help under make up. The demo rows are what makes a public URL acceptable under POPIA; as built they are environment variables on the hosted task, not a demo Spring profile.

application-demo.yml + DemoPersonasWhen the demo profile is on, accept only the published demo numbers (082 555 0101 to 0110, plus 082 555 0000, which always fails delivery so reviewers can watch the retries) and lock each one to a fictional persona name on the server. Any other number gets a friendly 422. GET /api/v1/config lists the personas. 082 555 0199 is reserved for the smoke test and not offered in the UI. As built there are no personas: BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY refuses any number outside 082 555 0000 to 0199 with 422 DEMO_NUMBER_REQUIRED, before anything is written. The hosted demo had it on until it began texting real SMS; it is off there now, so any South African mobile number may book.After M6 is green90
DemoDataPurgeJobAn hourly job in the demo profile deletes appointments older than 48 hours with their outbox and idempotency rows, and logs counts only. Built as RetentionPurgeJob, behind BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED, on for the hosted demo and off by default: every hour, first a minute after start-up, it deletes each appointment once its end has passed, with its messages and idempotency keys, and logs one line of counts. It first shipped deleting each booking 48 hours after it was made, which removed appointments that had not happened yet.After M6 is green60

The privacy page names you as the responsible party, the purpose, hosting in AWS Cape Town, the 48-hour deletion, your contact and the Information Regulator. Built in M4, differently, and updated for real SMS: the banner says Fynbos Bank is fictional, that the hosted demo texts the number given, so use only your own, that the demo numbers 082 555 0000 to 0199 are never texted, and, while AWS keeps the demo in its SMS sandbox, that only numbers verified on its account receive the text; there is no persona picker; /privacy names the fictional bank as the responsible party and AWS End User Messaging as the operator that sends each SMS, and states the purpose, deletion within the hour after the appointment ends on the hosted demo, deletion at once of a cancelled booking, whose cancellation SMS alone is kept until it is sent or has failed for good, a keyed fingerprint of the number kept about a day to count SMS, a one-way fingerprint of a booking's details kept about a day, backups that expire after 7 days, and the Information Regulator.After M6 is green60

As built, smoke.sh takes the base URL as its argument and writes nothing, so it neither books nor cancels; it counts a 403 from WAF as a closed endpoint, and EXPECT_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY=1 adds the demo-number check. smoke-aws.sh is not built; the pipeline runs smoke.sh against https://branchbook.click after every deploy.After M6 is green40

Guardrails for a public URL

GuardrailHow
Demo numbers onlyBuilt, and off on the hosted demo since it began texting real SMS. BRANCHBOOK_BOOKING_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY=true refuses any mobile outside 082 555 0000 to 0199, for a deployment that must never hold a real person's number; EXPECT_DEMO_NUMBERS_ONLY=1 scripts/smoke.sh fails while it is off.
Real SMS, South Africa onlyAny South African mobile number may book, and the confirmation and cancellation SMS go to it through AWS End User Messaging SMS from af-south-1. The demo numbers stay on the simulated gateway, so the README's examples never text a stranger. A protect configuration allows SMS to South Africa only and blocks every voice and MMS destination; the task role may send through it and nothing else; AWS refuses a message dearer than $0.20; and at most five SMS a day go to one number, counted in the database under a fingerprint keyed with branchbook/sms-cap-key from Secrets Manager, so both tasks, and any new one, keep the same count. With no one-time code, anyone can make the demo text any South African number up to that cap, a known risk in SECURITY.md. The account is still in the SMS sandbox, where AWS refuses every unverified number; such a refusal, like a monthly spend limit reached, cannot be cured by sending again, so the message ends FAILED on its first attempt, and the banner tells visitors that only verified numbers get a text. Production access has been requested.
Open, over HTTPSThe demo asks for no sign-in, because a sign-in prompt in front of a demo turns away the people it is for. Plain HTTP is redirected to HTTPS, and HSTS keeps browsers there. The simulated inbox is off, so no page lists other people's bookings; each booker sees their own SMS on the confirmation page. Should it be switched on, it shows every reference as APT-****-**** (BRANCHBOOK_INBOX_REDACT_REFERENCES).
Booking rate limitWAF blocks more than 10 POSTs per IP address in 5 minutes to /api/v1/appointments and the paths under it, bookings and cancellations together, because each can send a real SMS. Ten is the lowest limit WAF allows. It stops a script from filling the calendar.
Actuator never publicWAF blocks /actuator at the edge with 403, before any other rule, comparing the URL-decoded, lower-cased path, so /%61ctuator and /ACTUATOR are blocked too. The hosted task exposes only health and info (MANAGEMENT_ENDPOINTS_WEB_EXPOSURE_INCLUDE), so the Prometheus metrics stay private. There is no separate port 9091. The ALB checks /readyz on port 8080.
No real bank on the pageThe banner says the bank is fictional and names no real bank. The README carries the non-affiliation statement.
Not indexedThe page carries <meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">. The X-Robots-Tag header and robots.txt are not built.
Short retentionEach booking, with its name and number, is deleted within the hour after its appointment ends, as the privacy notice states: RetentionPurgeJob, switched on in the task with BRANCHBOOK_RETENTION_ENABLED. It replaced the first rule, deletion 48 hours after booking. A cancelled booking goes at once, with its confirmations; only its cancellation SMS outlives it, until it is sent or has failed for good. The SMS cap's ledger keeps a keyed fingerprint of each number, never the number, for 24 hours. RDS backups, which could still hold a deleted booking, expire after 7 days, and the privacy notice says so; CloudWatch logs are kept 7 days and hold no personal data. Delete the final snapshot at teardown.
Logs without personal dataPostgreSQL's own log is not exported from RDS, because a constraint error's DETAIL line can quote a mobile number. pgjdbc logServerErrorDetail=false keeps DETAIL lines out of the app. No AWS End User Messaging delivery events are recorded, because they carry the destination number. Grep the logs for mobile numbers after every smoke run.
Data stays in South AfricaThe app, database, backups, logs and image all live in af-south-1, and the SMS are sent through AWS End User Messaging SMS there. Only the IAM role and the cost budget sit in us-east-1, and they hold no customer data.
A budget and an end dateBudget e-mails go out at 50, 80 and 100 percent of $110, raised from $90 for the second task; the budget lives in BranchbookAccount, which the pipeline never deploys, so the new amount applies once the administrator deploys that stack again. Each SMS is charged on top, up to the account's monthly SMS spend limit. The teardown date, 2026-11-30, is in infra/README.md; the banner does not carry it.
